Card 3's owed smoke (Progress page) covered by the same session.
- (PROCESS RULE ADDED BY KD, 2026-07-16) After every card that changes user-facing behavior, the manual browser smoke is REQUIRED and gates "done" — being added to CLAUDE.md Part I §2 (master PR): the Card 4 smoke caught the CORS preflight bug that fastify.inject()-based tests can never see.
## onboarding-storage: fitness-profile storage on the new API (2026-07-15)
- (THE GAP, verified) v1 §6.1:442 assigns "onboarding data" to the `users` module but Part 4 defines NO storage for it — the §3.1 users DDL (04-part4-database.md:116-134) has no age/gender/height/fitness_level/goals/equipment columns. P2.7's migration therefore DROPped those Mongo fields (tools/migrate-mongo/INVENTORY.md:45, "(no columns) DROP — no target; the queued onboarding-storage gap"), which also starves Part 2B §4.1:358's content-based scorer (goal 40 · difficulty 20 · equipment 20 · duration 10 — precisely the dropped fields) that the spec calls the correct architecture. This card builds that storage. API-only and additive (the old web still POSTs /users/onboarding to backend-ml), so it merges to master normally — unlike the web-repoint cards.
- (PLACEMENT, Kd-ruled — Option B of two presented) A separate 1:1 table `user_fitness_profiles` (user_id IS the PK), NOT new columns on `users`. Rationale: purely additive migration with zero lock/rewrite risk on the auth-hot `users` table; leaves the spec's published §3.1 DDL intact rather than amending it with 11 nullable columns; onboarding data is a cohesive unit the future scorer reads together. Cost: one PK join on profile reads (`selectProfile` LEFT JOINs it for `onboarding_completed`). Weight is NOT duplicated — it stays on `users.weight_kg` (Part 4 §3.1) and the wizard's weight step uses PATCH /v1/users/me.
- (VALUE SETS — ported, never derived; R0.2) Every enum is copied verbatim from the salvage source backend-auth/src/models/User.js:44-98: gender male|female|other|prefer_not_to_say · fitness_level beginner|intermediate|advanced · fitness_goals weight_loss|muscle_gain|general_fitness|flexibility|endurance|posture|stress_relief (7) · available_equipment none|dumbbells|resistance_bands|kettlebells|pull_up_bar (5) · preferred_workout_time morning|afternoon|evening. Enforced as `text` + CHECK per Part 4 §1 (never PG enums); the two array columns are `text[]` with the value set enforced in Zod, following the existing `exercises.equipment`/`muscles` precedent (db/schema/catalog.ts:31-32) — Part 4 §1's JSONB policy forbids JSONB here since the scorer will filter on these.
- (BOUNDS — Kd-approved, NOT spec values) Neither the spec nor the Mongo model bounds these numerics; the following are sanity rails invented for this card and approved at the plan gate: age 13–120 · heightCm 50–300 · targetWeightKg positive/<1000/2dp (mirrors weightKg + numeric(5,2)) · exerciseFrequency 1–7 · sessionDurationMin 5–240 · medicalConditions trimmed, ≤2000 chars. They live in Zod only — the DDL does not encode them.
- (fitness_level DEFAULT, Kd-approved) Stored NULL until the user answers. The old Mongo model defaulted it to 'beginner'; we deliberately do not — unanswered is not beginner, and a future scorer can treat NULL as beginner if it chooses. Storing the guess would launder it into data.
- (PUT semantics) `PUT /v1/users/me/fitness-profile` is a FULL-DOCUMENT replace: an omitted field is written as NULL. That makes it idempotent by construction (R3.5) — the same body twice yields the same row — so it needs no Idempotency-Key. CONSEQUENCE, accepted: a client that sends `{}` clears the profile (and `onboarding_completed` → false, re-triggering the wizard). True PUT semantics; the wizard always submits the whole document. No data beyond the profile is affected.
- (GET returns the EMPTY profile, not a 404) No row is the common case (a user who has not onboarded), so the GET returns all-nulls with `updatedAt: null` — the wizard renders blank instead of branching on an error, and no timestamp is synthesized for a row that was never written. `userProfileSchema.onboardingCompleted` COALESCEs to false for the same reason.
- (onboardingCompleted on /v1/users/me — scope addition, Kd-approved) The field is added to the users profile view. This is the interim regression DECISIONS 2026-07-15 (web Card 1) recorded as OWED: the web's three gate sites (ProtectedRoute.jsx:44,:54; Login.jsx:33) compare `=== false`, so with the field absent from the auth view onboarding is enforced for nobody on the web-repoint branch. This card supplies the field; the web card that consumes it still owes the wiring.
- (DPDP — CORRECTION to the plan's premise; a real obligation, not covered by the FK) The plan asserted "the DPDP hard-delete cascade covers it for free". That is FALSE: Part 4 §5.2:829 ANONYMIZES the users row to a tombstone ("row kept so FKs from audit/invoices resolve") and never deletes it, so `ON DELETE CASCADE` NEVER FIRES on account deletion. §5.2's real mechanism is its explicit Day-14 DELETE list — which enumerates `auth_identities` even though that table already has CASCADE, proving the cascade is not what does the work. `user_fitness_profiles` holds age/gender/height and `medical_conditions` (health data, sensitive under DPDP), so it MUST be added to (a) the §5.2 Day-14 explicit DELETE list and (b) the §5.2 JSON-export list. Both belong to the Day-14/export worker card, which DECISIONS 2026-07-11 records as QUEUED and unbuilt. Recorded here and in a schema comment (db/schema/identity.ts) so that card inherits it. The FK CASCADE is KEPT as defense-in-depth (correct per Part 4 §1: a fitness profile is meaningless without its user; flagged inline as §1 requires) and is proven by a db.migration test — but it is not the DPDP path.
- (active-only upsert) The upsert is `INSERT … SELECT FROM users WHERE id=$1 AND status='active' … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`. The FK proves the user EXISTS but says nothing about status (§5.2 soft-deletes), and a check-then-insert would TOCTOU against a concurrent deletion; the SELECT makes "active user only" atomic — a deleted user's SELECT yields no row, nothing inserts, RETURNING is empty → 401.
- (no duplicates in the array fields) `fitnessGoals`/`availableEquipment` reject duplicate values rather than silently deduping (R2.3 parse-don't-validate): a repeated goal would double-weight Part 2B §4's scorer. The old Mongo model permitted duplicates; this is stricter by choice.
- (MIGRATION NUMBERING TRAP — repo-wide, found by this card) drizzle-kit numbers migrations from the journal's 0-based `idx`, but this repo's files are `idx + 1` (`0001_init` is idx 0), so EVERY generated migration lands one behind and must be renamed — drizzle generated `0005_user_fitness_profiles.sql`, colliding with the existing `0005_meal_logs_origin.sql`. The established fix (verified in commit 34438bf: `0005_meal_logs_origin.sql` shipped with `meta/0004_snapshot.json`) is to rename the SQL file AND its `_journal.json` tag to idx+1, leaving the snapshot at drizzle's own idx. Done here: file + tag `0006_user_fitness_profiles`, snapshot `meta/0005_snapshot.json`. Every future migration card hits this — rename before committing.
- (T3 RESOLUTION, 2026-07-15 — reviewed in a fresh chat per Part I §7c) T3 found NO blocking violations. Two advisories: (1) R9.2 — the accepted `PUT {}` full-clear semantics were exercised only via a partial body; FIXED by adding an explicit empty-body test (users.fitness.routes.test.ts, "PUT {} is the extreme replace") pinning that {} NULLs every field, flips onboarding_completed→false, and REPLACES rather than deletes the row (updatedAt stays a string). Full suite now 252 passed. (2) R11.4 — self-audit "PROVE green" is container-lifetime-bound; the closing gate is CI-on-Neon (below), not local PG16. Security pass clean (authn/tenancy/IDOR-none, parse, idempotency, SQL, no medical_conditions log leak). DPDP correction judged sound.
- (DPDP ESCALATION, T3 recommendation — Kd decision owed) Merging this card makes the queued Day-14/export worker card a DPDP EXPOSURE, not just a spec checkbox: a deleted user's medical_conditions (health data) now persists indefinitely on user_fitness_profiles until that worker ships (§5.2 tombstones users, so the FK CASCADE never collects it). RECOMMENDATION: promote the Day-14/export worker card in the queue ahead of non-compliance work. Recorded for Kd; not actioned here (out of card scope).
- (DPDP ESCALATION — RULED 2026-07-16: ACCEPT + PROMOTE) Kd delegated the call to the implementing chat's recommendation ("do which one you recommend"), so recording it as such rather than as an independent operator ruling. DECISION: ACCEPT the process-only guard and MERGE; the Day-14 hard-delete + JSON-export worker card is hereby PROMOTED to next-priority ahead of other non-compliance work (T3's recommendation). Rationale: production launches with an EMPTY database (DECISIONS 2026-07-13; RUNBOOK/cutover.md:11-13), so there are zero real users and zero real medical_conditions rows today — the exposure is theoretical at this instant and becomes real the moment the first user registers post-cutover. Blocking instead would stall the P2.8 critical path (the old backend cannot be decommissioned) behind a sizeable BullMQ card. THE PROMOTION IS THE PRICE OF THE ACCEPT: this card must land before real users exist, i.e. before/with P2.8 cutover — it is no longer a "someday" item.
- (NEON PRIMARY BRANCH WAS EMPTY — root cause of PR #30's red CI; repaired 2026-07-16) CI's `drizzle migrations on Neon branch` job died in <1s. NOT a fault in 0006 and NOT a bad GitHub NEON_API_KEY (the branch `ci-29440982870` was created fine, log line 18:33:37). Cause: the free-tier Neon branch had reset AGAIN (3rd occurrence — see HANDOFF:123 for 2026-07-13), leaving the PRIMARY branch (`br-twilight-darkness-aotigisp`) with **0 tables and no drizzle bookkeeping**; CI branches FROM primary (`init_source: parent-data`), so every CI run cloned an empty DB. Prior PRs (e.g. #28) stayed green only because they added NO migration — drizzle had nothing to apply and exited 0. 0006 was the first new migration since the reset, so it was the first to actually touch the broken DB. REPAIR: new connection string (host ep-plain-thunder → ep-wispy-rain) into gitignored apps/api/.env; `drizzle-kit migrate` → 6/6 applied, 44 public tables; seed re-run (idempotent); full api suite against REAL Neon = 33 files / 252 passed. CI re-run then went green (migrations job 58s, i.e. actually executing). LESSON for future cards: a green CI on a no-migration PR proves NOTHING about Neon's schema state; if the Neon branch resets again, repair primary FIRST (migrate + seed) or every subsequent migration PR reds.

Invisible to inject()-based tests. Fix: explicit methods list in app.ts; failing preflight test written first (app.test.ts). Full narrative on the web-repoint branch DECISIONS.

## Vision model swap + format-tolerant parsing (2026-07-16; Kd-approved option 1 during web Card 5a)
- (TRIGGER — the Card 5a smoke, second CORS-class catch) Kd's browser smoke 422'd ("retake photo") on a clear photo. Root cause, proven by replaying the EXACT adapter request against live Groq: BOTH real vision models return field formats the strict evidence schema rejects — Scout emitted `confidence: 0.9` (schema: enum high/medium/low) and `fill_level: "full"` (schema: number|null); Qwen emitted `photo_quality: "Good"` and `fill_level: "N/A"`. Every real scan died; the 250+ green tests never saw it because they use a fake provider that answers in perfect schema shape. The real completions are preserved VERBATIM as fixtures in nutrition.unit.test.ts (R9.5: failing tests written first).
- (MEAL_VISION_MODEL default → `qwen/qwen3.6-27b`, supersedes DECISIONS 2026-07-12 P2.6a) Groq decommissions Scout 2026-07-17 (email 2026-07-16). qwen3.6-27b is the ONLY vision-capable Groq replacement (console.groq.com/docs/vision, checked 2026-07-16). Qwen defaults to reasoning, which burned max_tokens before the JSON → the adapter passes `reasoning_effort: "none"` for `qwen/`-prefixed models (Groq docs/reasoning; the parameter is Qwen-only, so it is conditional).
- (Price constants re-quoted per Part 0 rule 4) VISION_INPUT/OUTPUT_MICRO_USD_PER_MILLION = 600_000n / 3_000_000n ($0.60/$3.00 per 1M, groq.com/pricing quoted 2026-07-16). ~5–9× Scout's rate; per-scan token counts are ~2k in / ~150 out, so per-scan cost ≈ $0.0017.
- (Format tolerance — normalization, not laxity) Zod preprocessors normalize ONLY the observed variants: enum strings lowercased (confidence, photo_quality); numeric confidence banded ≥0.75 high / ≥0.4 medium / else low (judgment call, recorded here); non-numeric fill_level → null (unknown, never guessed); container/size_class "none"/"n/a"/"null"/"" → null. Unknown keys and smuggled nutrition fields still fail .strict() — the kcal-smuggling rejection test still passes. The prompt now pins every field format explicitly; live-verified: Qwen returns schema-perfect JSON through the real adapter (pizza fixture, 2026-07-16).

- 2026-07-16 · Part 5 §1 price book (Kd DEVIATION ruling, raised at the Card-5a smoke) · Free-tier `meal_scan` entitlement changed 3/MONTH → **2/DAY** (seed.ts; plans upsert propagates on re-seed; deployed DBs need one `seed.ts` re-run). Kd hit the 3/month wall while testing and ruled the free tier too tight for a photo-first nutrition feature. Cost accepted at the ruling: ~60 scans/month ≈ $0.10/free user at Qwen prices (vs ~$0.005 before) — noted, not blocking. Pro stays 8/day. Test quota keys updated month→day.

- 2026-07-16 · 2B anti-hallucination / v1 §6.1 (Kd-approved at the Card-5a smoke) · **POST /v1/nutrition/meals/preview** added: the server computes nutrition for proposed {canonical, grams} items WITHOUT persisting, so the photo-confirm UI can show live numbers as the user adjusts quantity — the client still never does nutrition arithmetic (the doctrine bans BROWSER math, not server math before save). Photo flow resolves from the SAME draft snapshot confirm reads (scanToken peeked, never taken; rung preserved); manual flow shares resolveChosenItems with createManualMeal (T3 fix — preview must equal saved values). NO quota and no ledger row: no AI call, curated/cached lookups only (v1 §9.3 meters spend; there is none). Tests: computed values match the manual-meal math, nothing persists, both quota windows untouched, unknown food 400, unauth 401.

- 2026-07-16 · Part 2B §3.3 (Kd DEVIATION ruling at the Card-5a smoke: "no need to round") · kcal display rounding-to-10 REMOVED — kcalLow/kcalHigh/kcalPoint are exact whole kcal (2 apples read 187, not 190). Supersedes §3.3's round-to-10 display standard and amends the 2026-07-12 P2.6a T3 finding-1 entry (the point-inside-range clamp REMAINS, now over exact bounds). Macro 0.1 g rounding unchanged. Trigger: Kd read 90/190/280 for 1/2/3 apples and judged the non-linear jumps (round-to-10 artifacts) more confusing than honest precision.

## web repoint — Card 5c: meal composition + synonyms + change-label (2026-07-17)
- (CARD SPLIT, Kd-approved at the plan gate) Card 5c as scoped by cutover.md:122-132 (dishware IN-FLOW + portion API + change-label + stale-guards) was RE-CUT: 5c = meal COMPOSITION (add/remove ingredients) + food synonym aliases + the change-label control + the 5b stale-guard advisories; **dishware IN-FLOW moves to its own card ⑤c2** because driving the server's portion math from a chosen dishware needs a contract design of its own (a new API surface), not a UI tweak. The kickoff prompt asserted this split was already Kd-ruled; the REPO had no such record (S1: the prompt is hearsay), so it was re-confirmed with Kd before coding and is recorded here as the ruling. cutover.md updated accordingly — ⑤c2 is owed, nothing is dropped (NO-REMOVAL).
- (API, Part 2B §3.2 S4) Photo CONFIRM and PREVIEW accept EXTRA items beyond the scan draft (the oats-with-milk case). A chosen item absent from the draft resolves via findFood at portion rung 'default' (the canonical cache from DECISIONS 2026-07-17 makes OFF foods work here too); unresolvable → 400 unknown_food, never a silent drop. ONE shared `resolveDraftItem` serves confirm and preview so live math cannot diverge from what is saved (the standing trust rule). NO schema change: an extra is just another {canonical, grams} entry `chosenItemsSchema` already allows.
- (API, Stage-5 telemetry — T3 finding, CORRECTS the plan's premise) The plan (inherited from the kickoff) claimed the addition "is already recorded as a Stage-5 correction for free" by the originalItems-vs-items diff. That is SEMANTICALLY WRONG and was fixed: an addition is not an estimate error, so folding extras into the diff charged the added item's mass to the rung that estimated the DRAFT items — inflating exactly the signal DECISIONS 2026-07-12 (T3 finding 3) defines ("the rung that produced the corrected-away number"). `MealWrite` gains `correctedItems` = the confirmed values for the ESTIMATED items only. An all-additions confirm leaves originalItems empty and writes NO correction row — `worst([])` falls through every `.some()` and returns the BEST rung ('user_dishware'), i.e. it would fabricate a claim that user-dishware produced an estimate that never existed. Addition-telemetry, if ever wanted, is its own ruled field — not invented here.
- (API, food synonym aliases — the Card-5b roti case) `FOOD_ALIASES` on the curated table: naming metadata only (no nutrition numbers), checked in findCurated BEFORE the fuzzy substring pass and scored in searchCurated; only synonyms the substring match cannot already reach are listed ("biryani"→biryani_chicken already resolves, so it is absent). ~26 entries, each a reviewed judgment call. Plus one MEAL_VISION_PROMPT line nudging canonical_hint toward common local names.
- (API, alias matching — T3 finding, the card did NOT fix its own bug) The first implementation keyed aliases on the WHOLE slugged query, so it only fired on a bare synonym: `findCurated("Flatbread Stack")` → NULL, i.e. the exact input cutover.md:129-132 names was still 0 kcal, while the test asserted `findCurated("flatbread")` — a straw man that passed with the bug live. FIX: a NOISE_WORDS pass strips pure arrangement/quantity/presentation words (stack, plate, slice, of, …) and retries the alias + substring lookup. DELIBERATELY NOT arbitrary token probing: "Aloo Paratha" would then resolve to potato and "Beef Stew" to ground beef, and a WRONG food is worse than an honest "couldn't match" (the Card-5b empty state; Kd's exact-data-only taste). Preparation words like "stew" stay OUT of the noise list for that reason. Verified by command: Flatbread Stack/Roti Stack/Pizza Slice resolve; Aloo Paratha/Beef Stew/Margherita Pizza/Fresh Basil still drop honestly.
- (API, RESIDUAL — recorded, not fixed) Descriptive names whose head noun we DO stock but which the substring pass misses still drop: "Margherita Pizza" → NULL (curated "Pizza (cheese)"), and plurals ("Plate of Rotis") too. Fixing these needs a fuzzier matcher (e.g. head-noun-in-query scoring), which trades false-negatives for false-positives — a product call about honest-drop vs best-guess that is Kd's to make, not a silent widening. The prompt nudge is the first line of defence; the smoke on a real roti photo is what tells us whether it suffices.
- (API, retry-safety — T3 round 2) `confirmMeal` called the destructive `takeDraft` BEFORE resolving items, so one unresolvable ingredient 400'd with the draft already consumed: the scanToken was dead and the only way back was another photo (a fresh Groq call + another 2/day quota unit) to fix a typo. Pre-existing shape, but THIS card made it reachable (before extras, the client could only send canonicals the draft already had). FIX: read the draft non-destructively, resolve everything, `take()` only once nothing can reject — `take()` remains the atomic single-use gate (concurrent confirms both read; only one take() wins). Redis-down still reports 503 not 400: `get()` conflates missing-with-down, so the unreadable-draft path falls through to `take()`, whose Lua GET+DEL distinguishes them.
- (WEB) PhotoModal "+ Add an ingredient" (in-flow, where the mistake is visible — Kd's taste); extras ride the SAME confirm/preview payload as drafted items, priced live by the server. AddMealModal is DUAL MODE: with a `meal` prop it adds an ingredient to a SAVED meal by PATCHing items = existing (resent at stored gramsPoint) + new — the API already accepted any searched food there, so no new endpoint. MealRow gains the change-label control (PATCH mealType; null clears — the control 5b owed) and an ingredient summary line so "add ingredient" has a visible before/after. A shared `FoodPicker` replaces three copies of the search UI and carries the stale-response guard 5b's T3 owed.
- (WEB) A zero-match analysis (the roti case) is now CONFIRMABLE once the user adds an ingredient themselves — the 5b honest empty state now points at the in-flow fix instead of sending the user to another screen.
- (WEB, honesty — T3 finding) The AI's own totals cover only what it SAW, so once an extra exists both the TOTAL and the per-item rows withhold and show "…" until the server answers, rather than display a number lower than what will be saved (2B: the browser never computes nutrition, and must never show a figure that is not what gets written). With no extras the 5a behavior (fall back to the estimate while the preview lands) is preserved exactly.

A food already in the meal is excluded from the picker: the server maps the payload 1:1 with no dedup, so a duplicate canonical would silently double the amount AND double-count that item in the correction pair. The "+ Add an ingredient" button is gated at the contract's 30-item cap, and PhotoModal's dead Confirm now says WHY when grams exceed 10,000 (the other half of the 5b advisory AddMealModal already had).

(b) REMOVALS of drafted items produce no correction row (originalItems is built from chosen items only — pre-existing; 2B §3.2 counts "item swapped" as a correction). ## Card 5c2 API — dishware portions (in-flow "measure with my dish") (2026-07-18)
- (DESIGN RULING, Kd — DEVIATION from 2B §3.2 Stage 5) The spec's Stage 5 says a saved dish is auto-applied to every future scan of that container and "the question never repeats". Kd ruled the opposite: **ASK EVERY TIME, never silently auto-apply** — a user may eat from a different bowl each meal, so the saved bowls are offered as tap-to-pick choices at confirm, never a locked default. Consequence that simplifies the build: because the user actively picks their dish (rather than the app detecting + auto-filling it), the analyze/scan RESPONSE SHAPE IS UNCHANGED — no container/fillLevel echo needed, no migration. The web just adds a "measure with my dish" affordance per item.
- (CONTRACT, packages/shared) `chosenItemsSchema` item is now a UNION of two .strict() arms: the grams arm `{canonical, grams}` (unchanged) OR the dishware arm `{canonical, dishwareId(uuid), fillLevel(0<..≤1)}`. Both strict → a hybrid `{canonical,grams,dishwareId}` matches NEITHER and 400s (no ambiguity). Applies uniformly to confirm, preview (both arms), manual, and PATCH — all four already route items through the shared resolvers.
- (SERVER MATH, the can't-disagree guarantee) `dishwareGrams(volumeMl, fillLevel, canonicalHint) = round(volume × fill × density(food))` is EXTRACTED into portion-priors.ts and called by BOTH the scan-time rung-1 resolver AND the confirm-time dishware arm (service.normalizeChosen), so a bowl measured in the photo flow and the same bowl chosen at confirm compute identical grams. Unit-test pins the equality. The dishware arm always stamps rung `user_dishware`; range collapses to a point (user-chosen).
- (TENANCY, R3.2) New `repo.getDishware(sql, userId, id)` is userId-scoped; a foreign/missing dishwareId returns null → service 400s `unknown_dishware`, never resolves another user's dish. Route test proves a stranger's dishwareId 400s (not someone else's grams).
- (JUDGMENT CALL 1, Kd-agreed) `fillLevel` is REQUIRED in the dishware arm (no default). The scan resolver defaults a missing fill to 1, but a user actively measuring must state how full — inventing "full" would fabricate a portion. UI offers ¼/½/¾/full = 0.25/0.5/0.75/1.0; the contract accepts any (0,1].
- (JUDGMENT CALL 2, recorded inference) Confirm-time density keys off the item's `canonical` (e.g. `dal_lentil_curry`) where scan-time keyed off the vision's `canonical_hint` (e.g. `dal`); `density()` substring-matches ("dal"⊂"dal_lentil_curry", "sabzi", "rasam"), so the same class resolves — same normalization family. Recorded rather than assumed silently.
- (PROVE) api 276/276 on Neon (272 + 4 dishware route tests); unit +1 (dishwareGrams==resolver equality); typecheck + lint clean. NO migration, NO response-shape change.
- (T3 F1 — fixed) The dishware arm produced no lower/upper bound on the RESOLVED grams, unlike the grams arm (chosenItemsSchema: positive, ≤10_000). A tiny dish × low fill rounds to 0 g (e.g. 1 ml × 0.25 × thin = 0) → persists a MealItem the shared mealItemSchema declares impossible (gramsPoint.positive) → unrenderable on read; and a large dish × thick could exceed 10_000 g (10000 ml × 1 × 1.1 = 11000), bypassing the grams arm's cap. FIX: normalizeChosen rejects a resolved gram <1 or >10_000 with 400 `portion_out_of_range`, symmetric with the grams schema. Verified by probe (0 and 11000 reproduced) + boundary route tests.
- (T3 F2 — fixed) PATCH /meals/:id had no dishware-arm test (only preview/manual/confirm did). Added: a PATCH that re-measures a saved item with a dishware arm → grams recomputed, rung overridden to user_dishware; plus a foreign-dishwareId-on-PATCH 400 (tenancy holds on the edit path too). api 278/278 on Neon.

## web repoint — Card 5c2 web: "measure with my dish" UI (2026-07-18)
- (shared `DishMeasure` component) One component serves both modals: pick a saved bowl (chips) or save a new one, then how full (¼/½/¾/full). Reports {dishwareId, fillLevel} up; the SERVER prices it (2B — browser never does the math). Kd ruling honored: bowls are tap-to-pick EVERY time, never auto-applied. Save-a-bowl offers Small/Medium/Large presets = the MIDPOINTS of Part 2B Appendix B's katori classes (125/175/275 ml, cited BOWL_SIZES constant) or a type-ml field; containerClass = the class name so a future scan's rung-1 can auto-match the same dish.
- (AddMealModal) A "Type grams | Measure with my dish" toggle. Dish mode swaps the grams stepper for DishMeasure; the payload becomes the dishware arm; the SAME live-preview effect prices either arm (payload value-key gated, honesty preserved). Confirm disabled until a dish AND a fill are chosen ("Pick a dish & fill").
- (PhotoModal, per drafted item) Each AI-detected item gets a CookingPot toggle → measure THAT item with a dish (inline DishMeasure), "← back to grams" reverts. Payload logic rewritten: each item resolves to ONE arm via resolveArm(); `allValid` (was gramsValid) requires every item to be a valid grams-arm OR a complete dish-arm; index alignment for the live per-item numbers is preserved (payloadItems keeps analysis-then-extras order). Confirm copy: "Pick grams or a dish for every item".
- (SCOPE, deliberate) EXTRAS (ingredients the AI missed, added by search) stay grams-only for now — resolveArm handles their absent `measure` cleanly (no bug); measuring an added ingredient with a dish is an edge case deferred. Recorded, not silently dropped.
- (helpers) toChosenItems + composeAddIngredient pass the dishware arm through unchanged; toChosenItems clamps fill into (0,1] and floors an invalid/missing fill to the MINIMUM (0.01), never "full" — assuming full would fabricate a portion (Kd's no-invented-default ruling). Unit-tested (both arms; the 0-fill→0.01 case caught a `|| 1` bug in the first impl that would have overstated to full).
- (PROVE) web 79/80 (the 1 = the known pre-existing syncClient env quirk) · vite build ✓ · lint 7 vs the 7-error baseline (parity). No API change on this branch (the dishware arm merged via the dishware-portion-api PR).
- (T3 web F1 — fixed) A dish-mode drafted row fell back to the AI's grams-based estimate when the live preview failed (degraded/old API) — but confirm saves the DISH-resolved grams, so displayed ≠ saved, and NOT transient (persists until a preview succeeds). Dish mode structurally widens it (a katori of ghee vs the AI's guess can never match). FIX: the estimate fallback now also requires the item to still be on grams (`itemMeasures[i] === undefined`); a dish-mode row waits for the server ("…") rather than show a number that isn't what will be saved.
- (T3 web F2 — fixed) AddMealModal never stamped the preview with the payload it priced (PhotoModal did), so switching grams↔dish or editing showed the PREVIOUS amount's price for the debounce+latency window. FIX: mirror PhotoModal — setLive({data, key: chosenKey}); shownLive gates on live.key === chosenKey, withholding until the current amount is priced.
- (T3 web — SMOKE required, still owed) Both findings fixed; T3's remaining gate is the operator SMOKE (node tests can't exercise the browser flow): save-a-bowl round-trip, both modals' grams↔dish toggle, a mixed grams+dish photo confirm, and an incomplete dish leaving Confirm disabled.
- (Kd smoke asks, folded in — recorded per the established pattern) Two additions during the ⑤c2 smoke, both web-only, no API change: (1) DISCOVERABILITY — the PhotoModal per-item dish control became a LABELED "🍲 my dish" pill (was a bare CookingPot icon a first-timer would miss); combined with AddMealModal's explicit "Type grams | Measure with my dish" toggle and the guided "Save your first dish" form (shown when the user has none), the feature is now visible on first use. (2) RENAME — a logged meal's name is tappable to edit (Enter saves, Esc cancels), using the existing PATCH mealName (patchMealRequestSchema; server trims/bounds 1–200) — no API change. (3) button-wrap fix — the "my dish" pill got whitespace-nowrap + flex-shrink-0 so a row whose macro text wraps to two lines no longer stretches the pill (Kd caught the big-vs-small inconsistency in the smoke).
- (COST clarification, Kd asked) Card ⑤c2 adds ZERO paid calls: the dishware math (volume×fill×density) is pure server arithmetic; save/pick/compute are free DB ops; the live preview is curated/cache-fronted with NO quota and NO api_cost_events (established Card 5a). The only nutrition cost remains the vision photo scan, unchanged. The "API modification" was server code, not a paid service.
- (SMOKE — PASSED, Kd 2026-07-18, gates "done") Full click-through on local api+web: save-a-new-bowl round-trip (chip appears + auto-selected) · fill picker prices the bowl server-side · manual-add grams↔dish toggle · incomplete dish (no fill) keeps Add disabled with the right copy · photo confirm with ONE grams item + ONE dish item → per-row live numbers + total + Confirm saved both · "← back to grams" restores the stepper · "🍲 my dish" pill discoverable + both pills equal size after the wrap fix · rename a logged meal persists after refresh. Every step ✅.

## web repoint — Card 5d: previous-days meal view (2026-07-19)
- (SCOPE + APPROACH = option a, per cutover.md:148-150) The Nutrition page showed only today (client-side isToday filter over listMeals(100)); Card 5d adds day navigation (‹ / › / date picker) to look back at earlier days. **NO API change:** GET /v1/nutrition/meals has no date filter (nutritionListQuerySchema is {limit,cursor}, cursor opaque), so `listMealsForDay` page-walks the newest-first list (`walkMealsForDay`, nutritionApi.js) until it passes the target LOCAL day — MEAL_PAGE_SIZE=100, MAX_MEAL_PAGES=10 (~6 months at ~5 meals/day) then an honest "couldn't load back this far" fallback. Option (c) server-side date filter stays the documented upgrade path (cutover.md) if history runs deeper. **T3 verified the linchpin:** the server orders `taken_at DESC, id DESC` (repo.ts:222) matching the `(taken_at,id) < cursor` predicate, and takenAt is user-editable (handleEditTime) — so the newest-first early-stop is correct and no in-day meal hides behind an older row (ordering by created_at would have silently dropped edited-time meals).
- (DROPPED the session page-cache floated at plan-review — deliberate, recorded) Each mutation (delete/rename/relabel/edit-time/add-ingredient) already calls loadData() to refresh, so a cross-navigation cache would need invalidation on every one of those — trading a real stale-after-edit risk for a negligible perf win (adjacent-day nav is 1-2 free DB reads). The simpler always-refetch is the more T3-defensible call. Flagged to Kd since the "is your plan correct" reply had said caching would be folded in.
- (past-day interaction — Kd ruling 2026-07-17 enforced) Logging affordances (hero photo button + section "+") are HIDDEN on past days: a new meal always stamps the real current time, so it would land under today, not the viewed day. Per-meal edits (delete/rename/relabel/edit-time/add-ingredient) STAY on past days (they PATCH/DELETE existing rows). Back-dated logging would need a change to the current-time ruling → possible follow-up, not built.
- (past-day left column) "Remaining today" (target−eaten) is a today concept; on a past day it becomes "Eaten on this day" (that day's totals, no target math). **MacroRings STAYS on past days — Kd RULED KEEP (2026-07-19):** it fills toward today's targets while showing that day's server-summed totals; all numbers real/server-sourced (D3, no fabrication), targets are stable, and no historical targets are stored. T3 flagged the semantics as a conscious product choice; Kd chose keep.
- (day math) All local calendar days (startOfDay/isSameDay/addDays), matching the isToday filter it replaces; date <input> uses local YYYY-MM-DD (toISOString would shift the day west of UTC), parsed back local-only.
- (T3 F1 — stale-response race, fixed) Rapid ‹/› navigation fires overlapping loadData(d) page-walks; a slower earlier response could resolve last and paint the wrong day under the current label. loadData now stamps `latestDayReq` (a ref) with the day it fetches and drops superseded results (and their toast + spinner-ownership). Not unit-expressible without jsdom (web is node-vitest only, R1.4) — SMOKE is its proof.
- (T3 F2 — truncated vs empty honesty, fixed) When the walk hits the page cap before reaching the day (`truncated`), the client CANNOT know the day is empty. Three cases, never asserting unverified emptiness: truncated+nothing → "couldn't load back this far" only (no sections, which would each falsely say "none"); truncated+partial → banner + sections; not-truncated → true empty state or sections. The truncated flag itself is unit-tested (walkMealsForDay cap test).
- (T3 finding — date bar was inside the loading gate, fixed) goToDay's setLoading(true) blanked the whole grid (arrows included) to a spinner on every day change, so rapid ‹‹‹ (the reason F1's guard exists) wasn't reachable through the UI. Restructured: the grid + date bar always render; each column's CONTENT gates on loading. Arrows stay clickable mid-walk.
- (SMOKE-folded meal-row UX fixes — Kd asks 2026-07-19, recorded per the established pattern; MealRow surface built in 5c/5c2, no API change) (1) **RENAME BUG:** the name input's onBlur discarded the edit (setEditingName(false) with no save), so clicking away reverted the name and NO PATCH ever fired — proven by the api log (only GETs during a rename attempt). Fixed: single commit path via onBlur — Enter and click-away both save, Escape flags a cancel (nameCancelRef) so blur skips. Server PATCH already accepts mealName (patchMealRequestSchema, nutrition.ts:101); the bug was purely client. (2) **Discoverability:** an always-visible ✏️ pencil next to each meal name signals it's editable. (3) Row action buttons (relabel/tag · +add-ingredient · delete) no longer hover-gated (opacity-0 group-hover removed) — always visible. JSX-handler changes, not unit-expressible — SMOKE is the proof.
- (PROVE) web 84/85 (the 1 = the known syncClient VITE_API_URL env quirk, passes in CI, DECISIONS 2026-07-15) · vite build ✓ · lint **7 errors / 0 warnings = the Nutrition.jsx 7-error baseline parity** (5 set-state-in-effect + 2 no-unused-vars; the new exhaustive-deps warning from loadData closing over selectedDate is suppressed on the mount-only effect with a rationale comment). New tests: nutritionApi.test.js +5 (walkMealsForDay window/early-stop/cap + listMealsForDay cursor-passthrough).
- (T3 — FRESH CHAT, per CLAUDE.md:129) Kd ran the T3 in a separate fresh chat (not a subagent): ZERO violations, security clean, independently verified the taken_at ordering linchpin. One non-blocking finding (date bar in loading gate) fixed above. **SMOKE PASSED** (Kd 2026-07-19, every step ✅, incl. re-smoke of the meal-row fixes and the loading-layout fix).

## Desktop webcam capture for meal photos — WON'T BUILD (2026-07-19, Kd ruled)
- (THE RULING) The queued "desktop webcam capture" enhancement (cutover.md; DECISIONS 2026-07-17 Card-5b product asks) is **dropped, not deferred**. Kd raised it and ruled after a cited recommendation. NOT a feature removal — grounding confirmed NO meal-photo webcam capture was ever built: `useCamera.js` (getUserMedia) serves ONLY the workout/pose path (ActiveWorkout, PreWorkout), and nothing in the nutrition flow uses it. So the MIGRATION-STANCE no-removal rule is not engaged; this is declining to build a never-built enhancement.
- (spec check) Part 2B §3 specifies the photo PIPELINE (one vision call, portion resolver, photos never stored) and mandates NO capture mechanism — the spec is silent, so this was Kd's call to make (R0.2 respected: nothing invented).
- (no user is blocked) The meal photo input is `<input type="file" accept="image/jpeg,png,webp" capture="environment">` (Nutrition.jsx:1084-1091). `capture` is a hint DESKTOP BROWSERS IGNORE → on a laptop it opens the normal file picker, so a desktop user can already log a meal from any photo on disk. On phones it opens the rear camera directly.
- (the deciding rationale — accuracy, not effort) A laptop webcam is a materially WORSE input to the exact problem 2B §3 exists to solve (monocular portion estimation, "the same dal in a 150 ml katori vs a 400 ml bowl"): fixed, front-facing, low-res, forcing the plate to be held up at an unnatural angle. It would push more scans into the `photo_quality:"poor"` 422 retake path — paying twice (each scan is a paid Groq vision call ~$0.0017 + one of the 2/day free quota slots, DECISIONS 2026-07-16) for a worse answer. Accepted cost of the ruling, stated honestly: a laptop user with food in front of them and no phone to hand cannot capture in the moment; they need a photo already on disk. Judged too thin to build for — the phone is the natural device and the responsive web app already serves it.
- (OWED, UNVERIFIED — raised at the same ruling) DECISIONS 2026-07-17's claim that "the file input already opens the camera on phones (capture attr)" is **NOT device-verified** (V1): every smoke to date (Cards 4, 5a, 5b, 5c, 5c2, 5d) ran on Kd's DESKTOP browser. Since this ruling makes mobile web the PRIMARY meal-capture path, a real-phone smoke is owed — it needs `vite dev --host`, `VITE_API_URL` + the API's `WEB_ORIGIN` pointed at the laptop's LAN address (a phone resolves `localhost` to itself, and CORS is exact-origin + credentials, the Card-4 lesson). Kd deferred the test ("test later"); recorded in cutover.md so it is not lost.
- (a trade-off recorded with it, NO ruling yet) `capture="environment"` sends the user straight to the rear camera (one tap, ideal in-the-moment) but on browsers that honor it strictly it BYPASSES the photo library — a user who photographed lunch 20 minutes ago may have to retake. Options presented: (A) keep as-is · (B) drop `capture` so the phone shows a Take-Photo/Library chooser, taxing every log one tap · (C) two explicit buttons ("Take photo" / "Choose photo"), both one tap and discoverable. RECOMMENDED **A now, C later** — only if the owed phone smoke shows the gallery limitation actually bites. No code changed; A is the current behavior.

## web repoint — Card 6: onboarding wizard → new API + gate restore (2026-07-19)
- (PROCESS, Kd-instructed) Same-chat continuation (Part I §1 deviation, the standing pattern) — Card 5d, the webcam ruling, and Card 6 all ran in one chat by Kd instruction. Re-grounded on the branch per S1 before planning.
- (SCOPE) The onboarding wizard (Onboarding.jsx) wrote to the OLD backend-ml (/users/onboarding); repointed to the new /v1 API. `user_fitness_profiles` + `onboardingCompleted` shipped in PR #30 (onboarding-storage card); this card is the WEB consumer. Weight → PATCH /v1/users/me (users.weight_kg, Part 4 §3.1); everything else → PUT /v1/users/me/fitness-profile (full-doc replace, idempotent). No API change, no migration, no PR — commits on web-repoint.
- (GATE REGRESSION FIXED) Since web Card 1, authUserSchema omitted onboardingCompleted (a users-module concern, v1 §6.1:442), so the three `=== false` gate sites (ProtectedRoute:44,:54; Login:33) saw undefined → onboarding was enforced for NOBODY. AuthContext now enriches the session user with onboardingCompleted from GET /v1/users/me on both restore and login (fetchOnboardingFlag). Sequenced so the flag lands BEFORE loading clears (no gate-open flash) and adoptSession/flush timing stays bound to getMe (Card-2 territory untouched). Fails OPEN (undefined) on any fetch error — a profile-read blip never traps a user; the onboarding gate is UX, never a security control (R3.3, T3-confirmed).
- (mapper) toFitnessProfilePayload (pure, unit-tested): renames sessionDuration→sessionDurationMin, converts height/target-weight units to metric + round2 (multipleOf(0.01) contract, Card-3 f.1 precedent), blank medical→null, all enums verbatim from the shared schema. onboardingCompleted added at the call site (kept out of the mapper for future Settings reuse).
- (SMOKE-found bug 1: ft/cm 400) Switching the height unit left the number in the box, so "175" (cm) became 175 FEET = 5334 cm → the server's 300 cm cap 400'd, and only AFTER all 5 steps, with a useless "Failed to save profile". FIXES: (a) switching cm↔ft / kg↔lbs now CONVERTS the value (convertHeight/convertWeight, pure + unit-tested); (b) LIVE inline range validation — as the user types, an out-of-range height/weight shows a red message under the field and DISABLES Continue (Kd ruling: a wrong value must not be accepted, and the feedback must be INSTANT under the field, not a toast at the end); (c) the submit 400 branch shows a specific message. Client bounds are a subset of the API's own (heightCm 50-300, weightKg (0,1000)). Proven end-to-end against the LIVE API (register→login→PATCH→PUT→verify persisted).
- (SMOKE-found: 'Skip for now' dead-end — T3 finding, Kd ruled REMOVE) Re-enabling the gate made the wizard's "Skip for now" → /dashboard bounce straight back to /onboarding (dashboard is requireOnboarding, App.jsx:77). Removed the button — onboarding is now genuinely required, which is the card's whole purpose.
- (T3 finding: health-data console leak — FIXED) handleSubmit's catch logged the full axios error, whose config.data is the PUT body incl. medicalConditions (health data) → the browser console. Now logs `err?.message` only (R3.10 / DPDP).
- (ft clarification + overflow guard) 'ft' means DECIMAL feet; a hint ("5.75 = 5 ft 9 in") shows under the field when ft is selected (it feeds the calorie target — ambiguity matters). `maxLength=2000` on the medical textarea (the only realistic overflow of the API bounds).
- (DEVIATION from my own approved plan, recorded) The plan said "bounds clamped"; I deliberately do NOT clamp measurement VALUES — silently altering a height/weight that feeds calorie targets violates the exact-data rule (Kd's taste). Instead the live validation BLOCKS an out-of-range value with an inline message; the user fixes it. maxLength handles the medical overflow.
- (SETTINGS deferred — OWED CARD) Settings.jsx still reads/writes the profile via legacy mlApi (/users/profile, /users/reset-onboarding) and carries a now-dead `userService` import — left untouched (R1.1; its own card). So Card 6 does NOT complete P2.8's "web 100% on the new API" gate. Recorded as an owed line in cutover.md. Verified NO cross-consumer break: nothing calls the changed userService methods (Settings uses mlApi directly; the import is dead).
- (test gap, honest — T3 note) The orchestration this card adds (PATCH-then-PUT ordering + 400 branch; AuthContext gate-enrichment on login/restore) is NOT unit-covered — the web package is node-vitest only (no jsdom, R1.4); the 9 userApi tests cover the pure mapper + conversions. SMOKE is the proof (the recorded Card-2/3/4/5 web pattern). T3 flagged it as a note, not a block.
- (PROVE) web 93/94 (the 1 = the known syncClient VITE_API_URL env quirk, CI-green, DECISIONS 2026-07-15) · vite build ✓ · lint 6/0 = baseline parity (AuthContext 5 react-refresh + Onboarding 1 no-unused-vars, both pre-existing; new userApi.js/.test.js clean). +9 userApi tests (mapper, unit conversion to metric, unit-SWITCH conversion, the 4 call shapes, mlApi-gone guard).
- (T3 — FRESH CHAT, per CLAUDE.md:129) Kd ran the T3 in a separate fresh chat: ZERO rule violations; security pass clean (tenancy/IDOR-none, client-assertable onboardingCompleted grants no value, idempotency, fail-open gate — all reviewed sound). Two notes actioned (console leak FIXED; Skip REMOVED); one owed (Settings card recorded). SMOKE PASSED (Kd 2026-07-19, all steps ✅ incl. the live-validation retest).

## web repoint — Card 7: Settings profile forms → new API (2026-07-20)
- (PROCESS, Kd-instructed) Same-chat continuation again (Part I §1 deviation, the standing pattern).
- (SCOPE) Settings' two profile forms + reset-onboarding repointed from backend-ml (`/users/profile`, `/users/reset-onboarding`) to the new /v1 API. Load = GET /v1/users/me + GET /v1/users/me/fitness-profile (flat-merged); ProfileTab saves name/weight → PATCH /v1/users/me and age/gender/height/target → the fitness profile; FitnessTab saves the prefs. No API change, no migration, no PR — commits on web-repoint.
- (THE FULL-REPLACE TRAP → mergeFitnessProfile) PUT /v1/users/me/fitness-profile is a FULL replace and the two forms each own only PART of it — a form sending only its own fields would WIPE the other's. Pure `mergeFitnessProfile(current, edits)` (userApi.js) does read-modify-write; `onSaved`→loadProfile refreshes after each save. PROVEN end-to-end on the live API: after a basic-info save the goals/level/equipment survived and onboardingCompleted stayed true.
- (THE UN-ONBOARD TRAP, command-verified) service.ts:142 `onboardingCompleted: body.onboardingCompleted ?? false` — a PUT that OMITS the flag sets it FALSE, so every Settings save must carry it or the user is bounced to the wizard. mergeFitnessProfile carries the CURRENT value (not a hard `true`) so a save landing right after a reset cannot silently re-onboard (T3 F1 residual). Both behaviours unit-tested AND proven live.
- (ENUM ALIGNMENT — Kd ruled option A) Settings' fitness lists were the OLD Mongo values and would 400 on the new API: goals carried `core_strength` (absent from fitnessGoalSchema); equipment carried `bands`/`pullup_bar` (wrong names) plus `barbell`/`machine` (absent from equipmentSchema); workout time carried `night` (absent from workoutTimeSchema). ALIGNED to the supported set — the same 7 goals / 5 equipment / 3 times the wizard already offers (fixed at the onboarding-storage card). NOT a silent UI reduction: raised with the no-removal rule cited and ruled by Kd; the dropped values were never storable on the new API. Recorded rationale: the 5 equipment types are HOME equipment, matching the product.
- (AVATAR — no new-API home; OWED CARD) Verified by command: NO profilePicture/avatar field exists in the shared schema OR the users DB table (identity.ts). The picture upload therefore CANNOT be repointed; per the no-removal rule the UI STAYS on the old backend (broken-on-branch, the gamification pattern) and an "avatar storage" card is owed in cutover.md. T3 note folded into that card's scope: the existing path base64-inlines a 2 MB image into a JSON PATCH and does NOT meet R3.9 (magic-byte validation, size cap, R2 + server-generated keys, signed URLs) — that must be explicit in the owed card, not silently inherited at cutover.
- (RESET-ONBOARDING = WIPE, Kd ruled) The button now calls `putFitnessProfile({})` — the full-clear (DECISIONS 2026-07-15): every fitness-profile field cleared AND onboarding_completed → false (verified live: PUT {} → 200, flag false). Kd chose WIPE over keep-and-reshow after both were explained. `users.weight_kg` is a separate column used elsewhere (calorie math) and is deliberately NOT cleared — recorded as a scoped choice, one line to change if ever wanted.
- (T3 — FRESH CHAT, per CLAUDE.md:129; 7 findings, ALL fixed) **F1** reset silently undone by the next tab save (the stale parent profile re-wrote the wiped row incl. onboardingCompleted:true) → AccountTab now takes `onSaved`, awaits the refresh, flips the gate via `updateUser`, AND the merge carries the current flag; proven live (a post-reset save leaves the flag false). **F2** the client re-introduced the defaults the shared schema deliberately removed ("unanswered is not 'beginner'", users.ts) — fitnessLevel/sessionDurationMin/preferredWorkoutTime/exerciseFrequency/gender were all fabricated → removed; fields render "Not set" and save NULL. **F3** age bounds 10–100 vs the server's 13–120 → corrected, and the 400 message now names age. **F4** partial write (name/weight committed, then the PUT 400s while the toast said failure) → the PUT now runs FIRST so a validation failure commits nothing. **F5** empty catch on the avatar load → logs `err?.message`. **F6** medicalConditions uncapped vs `.max(2000)` → maxLength. **F7** gender omitted `prefer_not_to_say` (which the wizard offers) → added, alongside "Not set".
- (T3 done-gate: TEST GAP fixed) mergeFitnessProfile's tests fed a CLEAN fitnessProfileSchema object while production passes the FLAT merge `{...fitnessProfile, ...user}` (carrying id/email/displayName/weightKg/updatedAt). Nothing asserted the output key set, so a later `{...current, ...edits}` simplification would have shipped a `.strict()` 400 with a fully green suite. Added a key-set assertion over a flat-merged fixture, plus a post-reset case.
- (Kd smoke ask — the dropdowns; new shared component) A native `<select>`'s popup is OS-drawn: it rendered light against the dark UI, and Windows paints the HOVERED row with the system accent (blue) which NO CSS can override. `color-scheme: dark` (+ an `option` palette rule in index.css) fixed the background but not the blue, so all five Settings dropdowns moved to a new reusable `components/common/Select.jsx` (own list, orange hover/selected, closes on outside-click/Escape, string OR number values). The Onboarding wizard's native unit pickers are UNCHANGED — owed line in cutover.md rather than folded in silently.
- (PROVE) web 99/100 (the 1 = the known syncClient VITE_API_URL env quirk, CI-green, DECISIONS 2026-07-15) · vite build ✓ · lint 4 errors on Settings.jsx, DOWN from its 5-error baseline (this card fixed the dead `userService` import T3 flagged); new Select.jsx + userApi.js clean. userApi tests 9 → 15.
- (SMOKE — PASSED, Kd 2026-07-20, gates "done") Profile + Fitness saves with the no-wipe cross-check in BOTH directions · "Not set" states · prefer_not_to_say · reset → blank wizard immediately and STAYS reset · cm↔ft converts (180 → ~5.91) · all five custom dropdowns match the theme with no blue highlight.

## Nutrition targets API — the Mifflin-St Jeor port (2026-07-20)
- (THE GAP, command-verified) `GET /v1/nutrition/targets` did not exist: a grep for `mifflin|bmr|tdee|calorieTarget|dailyTarget` across ALL of `apps/api/src` + `packages/shared/src` returned only prose in a coach knowledge `.md`. The web's MacroRings + "Remaining today" therefore still read the OLD backend via `nutritionApi.js`'s `getTargets` → mlApi — the D2 interim Kd ruled at Card 5a (DECISIONS 2026-07-16), recorded as the owed **"Nutrition targets/remaining (MacroRings + Remaining today)"** checkbox in **web-repoint's** RUNBOOK/cutover.md owed-endpoints list (that list does not exist on master's copy — T3 round 2 caught this as the third instance of the cross-branch citation class). This card is the API half; the web half repoints the page. NO migration — targets are computed per request from stored profile data, never stored.
- (CITATION DISCIPLINE — T3 finding, recorded because the mechanism will recur) The two line numbers this entry originally carried (`nutritionApi.js:210`, `cutover.md:103-116`) were verified on the **web-repoint** branch and written into a **master**-bound commit, where the same files are the pre-repoint versions (47 and 88 lines) and those lines do not exist. Both were real readings of the wrong checkout, which is worse than a guess because they survive a re-read on the author's branch. RULE for every cross-branch card: cite web-repoint file positions by QUOTED TEXT, never by line number, and name the branch when a line number is unavoidable.
- (INPUTS) Five values, spanning two tables: `user_fitness_profiles` (age, gender, height_cm, exercise_frequency, fitness_goals — populated by BOTH the wizard (Card 6) and Settings (Card 7)) + `users.weight_kg`. Read via a new sql-only `getUserTargetContext(sql, userId)` in users/service.ts, mirroring the existing `getUserSyncContext` precedent (:27): the exported `getProfile`/`getFitnessProfile` take `UsersDeps{sql,redis,emailSender,log}` and `NutritionDeps` has no emailSender, so nutrition cannot call them. It composes the two TESTED repo reads (2 indexed PK lookups, once per page load) rather than adding a join, keeping all DB access in repo.ts (R4.6) and adding no new SQL to review.
- (KD RULING 1 — HONEST EMPTY STATE, no fabricated defaults) The salvage defaults every missing input (weight 70 / height 170 / age 25 / gender "male", nutrition.py:103-106) and flags it with `using_defaults` (:178). NOT ported: a fabricated target renders identically to a real one, which is the exact class the Card-7 F2 ruling struck down ("unanswered is not 'beginner'"). The response is `{targets, missing[]}` with `targets: null` EXACTLY when `missing` is non-empty — the client names what to fill in instead of showing a generic 2000.
- (KD RULING 2 — all five inputs REQUIRED) age · gender · heightCm · weightKg · exerciseFrequency. Each moves the number materially (frequency alone swings it ~700 kcal: 1.2 vs 1.9, :140-141). `fitnessGoals` is deliberately NOT required — the salvage has a real no-adjustment branch (:152-153), so an empty goal list is an answer, not an omission.
- (PORT FIDELITY, Part 0 rule 4 / R5.4) Every constant carries its nutrition.py line in `targets.ts`: BMR female −161 (:127) / else +5 (:129); the 7 activity multipliers (:140-141); tdee (:145); goal adjust −400/+300/0 (:148-153); kcal floor 1200 (:155); protein/kg 2.2/2.0/1.6 (:158-163); fat = kcal·0.25/9 (:166); carbs (:167) with its 50 g floor (:174). Goldens in the unit test are hand-computed from these same lines, then pinned.
- (SALVAGE QUIRK, ported deliberately + pinned) The kcal adjustment tests `weight_loss` FIRST (:148-151) while the protein split tests `muscle_gain` FIRST (:158-161). A user with BOTH goals therefore gets the cutting calorie target (−400) AND the bulking protein figure (2.2 g/kg). That inconsistency is in the original; porting it verbatim is what R5.4 requires, and a unit test pins it so no future edit "tidies" it into consistency without a ruling. Kd may open a card to change it — that would be a product decision, not a port fix.
- (gender other / prefer_not_to_say) Both fall to the salvage's `else` (+5, :128-129), i.e. the male formula. Mifflin-St Jeor defines only two formulas; inventing a midpoint would be re-deriving a constant (R0.2). Recorded rather than assumed silently.
- (NOT PORTED, each with a reason) (a) the lbs→kg (:115-118) / ft→cm (:120-123) branches — legacy Mongo stored `{value,unit}` documents; the new API is metric-only, so there is no unit to inspect. (b) `fiber_g` (:176) + `water_ml` (:177) — command-verified that nothing in `apps/web/src` renders a fibre or water target, so they would be dead surface (R1.1). (c) the defaults + `using_defaults` (ruling 1 above).
- (UNREACHABLE BRANCH, recorded) The salvage's `activity_map` string keys — "1-2"/"3-4"/"5-6"/"daily" (:132-137) — can never fire on new data: `user_fitness_profiles.exercise_frequency` is an INTEGER column (identity.ts:132), so only the `freq_map` 1-7 path (:140-141) is live. The ported `.get(frequency, 1.55)` fallback (:143) is KEPT because R5.4 requires porting it verbatim — it is DEFENSIVE ONLY, not currently reachable. Zod is the sole writer of `exercise_frequency` (upsertFitnessProfile ← the PUT's `.min(1).max(7).nullable()`, users.ts:141); migration 0006 declares a bare integer with NO CHECK behind it (the table's only CHECKs are gender / fitness_level / preferred_workout_time), so the fallback earns its keep the day a second writer appears, degrading an out-of-range integer to the ported default instead of NaN (I6). **THIS ENTRY WAS WRONG TWICE, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS** — round 3 corrected an asserted DB CHECK that does not exist; that correction then asserted "P2.7-migrated rows never pass through Zod at all", also FALSE (T3 round 6): the migrator never writes this table at all — INVENTORY.md:45 drops the onboarding fields and collections/users.ts:80 inserts only `users` columns. Rounds 4 and 5 both re-read the sentence and missed it, because they verified the CHECK half (true) and not the migration half. Adding the CHECK would be its own small migration card, not a silent addition here.
- (ROUNDING) Python `round()` is half-to-even, JS `Math.round` half-up — the measure-zero divergence class already ruled at DECISIONS 2026-07-07. As in the salvage, all arithmetic stays in floats and rounds ONCE at the end; goldens were chosen off exact-.5 boundaries so the two agree.
- (PROVE) 10 unit tests (activity table verbatim · female and male goldens · other/prefer_not_to_say · the opposite-precedence quirk · goal branches + 1200 floor · 50 g carb floor · protein selection · missing-input reporting · resolveTargets withholding) + 1 route test (401 · brand-new user → all five missing · profile-without-weight → only weightKg missing, pinning that the read spans BOTH tables · complete → the same golden end-to-end through real storage · two users never cross). Full api suite **289/289 on Neon** (278 baseline + 11); typecheck + lint clean.
- (CURL DRIVE, before any handover) Register → login → targets (all 5 missing) → PUT profile → targets (only weightKg) → PATCH weight → targets = the 1646 golden → 401 unauthenticated. NB the first drive 404'd on every call: a STALE api process from an earlier session still held port 3000 (`EADDRINUSE` in the new server's log, confirmed via netstat), so the requests were hitting an old build. Not a code fault — recorded because a 404 there looks exactly like a missing route.

## Nutrition targets API — T3 resolution (2026-07-20; fresh-chat review per CLAUDE.md:129)
- (T3 verified the port independently) The reviewer read nutrition.py:98-179 directly rather than trusting the code comments and confirmed every cited line is real and every value matches, recomputed all five goldens by hand, and confirmed the opposite-precedence quirk is faithfully ported. Also confirmed no fabricated default was reintroduced and that two latent hazards are safe: the dropped Python `.lower()` on gender is harmless because both the DDL CHECK and genderSchema are lowercase-only, and numeric(5,2)→string coercion is handled by the repo's `Number(...)` mapping. Security pass clean.
- (T3 F1 — R7.2, FIXED) The shared contract had **zero consumers**: `packages/shared/src/nutrition.ts` declared the response shape while `targets.ts` hand-declared parallel `MissingTargetInput` / `NutritionTargets` / `TargetsResult` — same names, different package, no link. Exactly the duplication R7.2 exists to prevent, and invisible to tests because the two copies coincided. FIX: `REQUIRED_TARGET_INPUTS = missingTargetInputSchema.options` (derived, cannot drift), the three types are imported from `@app/shared` instead of redeclared, and `resolveTargets` returns the shared `NutritionTargetsResponse`. `TargetInputs` stays local — it is the calculator's INPUT and is never serialized. Guard test pins the derivation; a route test now parses BOTH response arms through `nutritionTargetsResponseSchema` (`.strict()`, so an extra key fails rather than being carried silently).
- (T3 F2 — R7.1, FIXED) `nutrition/schemas.ts` re-exports every other nutrition contract from shared; the targets schemas were the only omission, so the route bypassed the module's established seam. Added to both the value and type re-export lists; service.ts now imports the type through `./schemas.js` like its siblings.
- (T3 F3 — V1/V2, FIXED) The `DEFAULT_ACTIVITY` comment claimed the 1.55 fallback was unreachable because "Zod + the DB CHECK bound frequency to 1-7". **No such CHECK exists** — migration 0006 declares a bare `"exercise_frequency" integer`; the table's only CHECKs are gender / fitness_level / preferred_workout_time. Behaviour was always safe (`?? DEFAULT_ACTIVITY`), but the comment asserted defence-in-depth that isn't there, Comment and the DECISIONS entry both corrected. (Round 6 corrected them AGAIN — the round-3 replacement claim, that P2.7-migrated rows bypass Zod, was itself false; see the UNREACHABLE BRANCH entry above.) Adding the CHECK is its own migration card if ever wanted.
- (T3 F4/F5 — V1/V2, FIXED) Two line citations verified on web-repoint and written into a master-bound commit. See the CITATION DISCIPLINE line in the card entry above — the standing rule is now: quote text, not line numbers, whenever a card's record crosses branches.
- (T3 Done-gate finding — NO new ruling needed; the reviewer was reading master's web, and the question is already ruled) The reviewer flagged that `getTargets` has no callers and that MacroRings reads targets out of the old `getTodayMeals` response, concluding the web half is "a build, not a repoint" and that a `remaining` API surface may be owed. That is TRUE OF MASTER's web app, which is the pre-repoint copy — the review ran against this branch's checkout. On **web-repoint** (where the web half lands) `Nutrition.jsx` already calls `nutritionService.getTargets()` as its own request, so no new API surface is owed. The remaining-math question is ALREADY RULED TWICE and must not be re-litigated (CLAUDE.md: re-asking Kd to rule on something DECISIONS already rules on is a protocol failure): **D2** and **D3**, both at **`web-repoint:DECISIONS.md`** under "## web repoint — Card 5a" — D2: "Targets/remaining rings stay on the OLD backend … remaining = the old server's own max(0, target−total) display formula over new server-computed meal sums"; D3: "Client sums SERVER-computed per-meal totals for the day display … display aggregation only". The 2B doctrine bans the BROWSER deriving nutrition (kcal/macros from foods and grams, where a wrong number would be fabricated data); subtracting two server-computed numbers for display is not that. So: NO new API surface and no `remaining` endpoint. Recorded here so the web card inherits the citation instead of rediscovering the question.
- (T3 round 2 — I UNDERSTATED THE WEB HALF; corrected) The line above originally called it "a one-function repoint". That is WRONG and the reviewer proved it by command: on **web-repoint**, `MacroRings.jsx` reads `safeTargets.protein_g || 150` / `carbs_g || 250` / `fat_g || 65`, and `Nutrition.jsx`'s `const t = {...}` block reads the same snake_case keys (the old backend's shape, documented in that branch's `nutritionApi.js` getTargets comment), while this endpoint returns `proteinG / carbsG / fatG`. A pure function swap yields **undefined for all three macros**. The web half is: field renames (snake_case → camelCase) + removing the fabricated fallbacks + the honest empty state. Still small, but not a swap — and the failure mode of getting it wrong is silent, since `undefined || 150` renders the fallback rather than an error.
- (OWED, and NOT delivered by this card — T3 round 2, the finding that matters most) This card's central ruling is "the client names what to fill in instead of showing a generic 2000". **The web does not do that yet, and nothing recorded it as owed.** on **web-repoint**, `MacroRings.jsx` reads `safeTargets.kcal || 2000` plus `|| 150 / || 250 / || 65`, and `Nutrition.jsx`'s `loadTargets` does `setTargets(res.data?.targets || {})` — which **discards `missing[]` entirely** and collapses `targets: null` into `{}`, so every fabricated fallback fires for exactly the incomplete-profile user the ruling is about. The API half only ENABLES the honest state; the web card must deliver it, and until it does the Kd ruling is unimplemented. The web card MUST: consume `missing[]`, render the "add your details" state naming those fields, and delete the fabricating `||` fallbacks in BOTH files — MacroRings' four target defaults and Nutrition.jsx's four in the `const t = {...}` remaining block, plus the `|| {}` that swallows the null (the `targets || {}` / `totals || {}` guards against an undefined PROP are not fabrications and may stay; the web card should quote-match, not count). This SUPERSEDES `web-repoint:DECISIONS.md`'s Card-5b line "(Remaining-today defaults — Kd smoke finding) … Remaining falls back to the SAME default goals MacroRings renders (2000/150/250/65 …) instead of showing zeros" — approved only while targets were dark for EVERYONE; once real targets exist, the fallback only ever misleads the user with an incomplete profile.
- (P2.8 gate honesty — T3 round 2, premise corrected round 3) `mlApi.get('/nutrition/targets')` retains a live caller **on web-repoint** (`Nutrition.jsx`'s `loadTargets`) until that web card lands; on THIS branch the function has no caller at all. Either way `backend-ml` cannot be decommissioned on the strength of this endpoint and the cutover checkbox stays UNTICKED. Correct as scoped — flagged because the earlier wording risked implying more progress than the repo supports.
- (PROVE, post-round-2) api **291/291** on Neon (289 + the derivation guard + the impossible-pairing refine test); typecheck + lint clean across api and shared. (Round 3 F6 corrected a stale "290" here — the arithmetic had omitted the second test the same commit added.)

## Nutrition targets API — T3 round 3 (2026-07-20; fresh chat)
- (round-3 verdicts on the round-2 fixes) The reviewer independently re-verified: the refine() rejects both impossible states; the guard test genuinely fails (they shrank the shared enum and watched it go red, then restored); the seam import holds; and all five goldens recompute exactly against nutrition.py:126-174. Security pass clean again (authn, tenancy-by-construction, R3.1, parameterized SQL, no log/secret leak, the contract throw maps to a generic 500 with no internals).
- (F1 — FIXED, the one that mattered) `missingTargetInputs` filtered `input[key] === null`, which caught the enum SHRINKING but not it GROWING: a key added to the shared enum whose `TargetInputs` field is non-nullable (e.g. `fitnessGoals: string[]`) compiles, never equals null, and is therefore silently unenforced — a "required" input that never blocks a target. FIX (as CORRECTED at round 4 — the round-3 version was itself defeatable): the fields are projected through a mapped type asserting NULLABILITY, `satisfies { [K in MissingTargetInput]: K extends keyof TargetInputs ? (null extends TargetInputs[K] ? TargetInputs[K] : never) : never }`, so a new enum key missing from the literal fails the BUILD and one whose field cannot BE null collapses to `never` and also fails.
- (F3 — FIXED) `resolveTargets`'s doc promised "the shared contract's refine() rejects either impossible pairing", but the parse lived only in `service.getTargets` — and `resolveTargets` is exported and directly unit-tested, so every other caller got zero enforcement. The parse now runs INSIDE `resolveTargets` (the service's duplicate removed), so the guarantee is true for all callers. Same class as the round-2 F1 finding: a doc comment asserting an enforcement the artifact did not carry.
- (F2 — FIXED) The narrowing chain's comment called itself "the drift alarm"; it alarms on enum SHRINK only (growth is now caught at the `satisfies`). Reworded to say exactly that.
- (F10 — FIXED) zod 3's `.options` getter returns the schema's OWN internal array, so `REQUIRED_TARGET_INPUTS` was a live mutable alias — a consumer's `.sort()` would reorder the shared contract in place. Probed and confirmed (`same object reference: true`, `frozen: false`). Now `Object.freeze([...options])`, with a test pinning both properties.
- (F11 — FIXED) The guard test's second line was `expect(x).toEqual(x)` again — the residue of the round-2 fix. Deleted; the literal five-field assertion is the guard.
- (F6/F7/F8/F9 — FIXED, all citation errors in MY OWN entries) A stale test count (290→291); `MacroRings.jsx:63-66` for reads that are at 65-67; `Nutrition.jsx:1587` for a `setTargets` at :1588; "five `||` fallbacks in both files" (the real split is 4 fabricating defaults + 1 null-swallowing `|| {}` per file, alongside prop guards that are NOT fabrications); and a "live caller" line that named no branch. Every one is the same class the card wrote a rule about at CITATION DISCIPLINE — proof the rule was written but not yet APPLIED. All are now quoted text rather than line numbers.
- (F4 and F5 — REJECTED, with evidence; the reviewer made the round-1 error) F4 claimed D2/D3 "do not exist in DECISIONS" and that the entry forecloses a question on uncitable rulings; F5 claimed the Card-5b fallback approval does not exist. Both were reached by running `git grep` on THIS branch. They DO exist, on the branch the web half targets: `git show web-repoint:DECISIONS.md` returns D2 at :360, D3 at :361 ("display aggregation only" verbatim), and the Card-5b "(Remaining-today defaults — Kd smoke finding)" approval at :376. So the rulings stand and the remaining-math question stays closed. The REAL defect the reviewer surfaced is that my citations named no branch — fixed above by quoting the ruling text and naming `web-repoint:DECISIONS.md`. Recorded rather than silently accepted: an unverified retraction of a real Kd ruling would be worse than the miscitation.
- (SMOKE — the loop's exception, now stated in the form the rule requires) **This card ships NO browser-reachable surface.** `GET /v1/nutrition/targets` has no consumer on either branch (on web-repoint the page still calls the OLD mlApi endpoint), so there is nothing for Kd to click. Per CLAUDE.md Part I §2 a card whose UI cannot be reached states that explicitly instead of a smoke; the curl drive is PROVE evidence, NOT a smoke, and does not substitute for one. The SMOKE is owed by the web card and must cover: a complete profile showing real targets, an incomplete profile showing the honest "add your details" state naming the missing fields, and the absence of any 2000/150/250/65 anywhere.

## Nutrition targets API — T3 round 4 (2026-07-20; fresh chat)
- (F1 — FIXED; round 3 closed the EXAMPLE, not the CLASS) The round-3 guard was `satisfies Record<MissingTargetInput, number | string | null>` and its comment claimed "one whose value cannot be absent fails the Record's nullable constraint". FALSE: that Record admits any non-nullable `string` or `number`. It rejected round 3's own worked example (`fitnessGoals: string[]`) by ARRAY-NESS, not nullability — so a grown enum key typed `string` compiled clean and was silently unenforced, exactly the hole F1 claimed to have closed. Probed both ways in the real tsconfig: `fitnessLevel: string` → exit 0 (hole open); with the mapped type → `TS2322 'string' is not assignable to 'never'`. Now mutation-tested end to end against the REAL enum + a real non-nullable field: `targets.ts(120,5): error TS2322`. LESSON, recorded because it is the third instance of the same shape in this card: a guard verified only against the example that motivated it proves nothing about the class — mutate the class, not the case.
- (F1 AGAIN — round 5; the FOURTH shape, and the pattern is the finding) `null extends string | null | undefined` is TRUE, so the round-4 mapped type admitted an OPTIONAL field, and the runtime filter's `=== null` then never matched `undefined` — a required input that never blocks a target, verbatim the failure F1 had claimed to close for three rounds. Harmless TODAY only because users/service.ts writes `?? null` on all five fields; a sixth added without it would have shipped the hole. FIX: `== null` (covers both absences; the existing repo idiom at users/repo.ts:255). **THE REAL FINDING IS THE PATTERN, not any one shape:** rounds 3, 4 and 5 each verified the guard against exactly the mutation the previous round named, leaving the next-nearest one open — the artifact never satisfied round 4's own "mutate the class, not the case" lesson. The test now enumerates the CLASS (null · undefined · key absent) instead of the case.
- (round-4 confirmations) The reviewer independently confirmed: the refine enforcement now reaches every caller (`resolveTargetsUnchecked` is module-private; `resolveTargets` is the only exit and parses unconditionally; one caller chain, routes.ts → service.ts → resolveTargets); the round-3 REJECTIONS of F4/F5 are correct (`git show web-repoint:DECISIONS.md` → D2 :360, D3 :361, Card-5b approval :376 — the rulings are live and the remaining-math question stays closed); both throw paths land on the generic `internal_error` branch with no internals leaked; and every remaining citation spot-checked real. Security pass clean.
- (GATE RISK — FIXED ON THE OTHER BRANCH, round 4) The supersession of the Card-5b `2000/150/250/65` approval was recorded ONLY here, on master. The web card runs on **web-repoint**, where a Part I.6-compliant chat reads that branch's DECISIONS tail and would find the un-superseded approval with nothing pointing at the ruling that voids it — the precise mechanism by which "the client names what to fill in" would stay unimplemented, and the same cross-branch class this card wrote CITATION DISCIPLINE about. A marker line has therefore been committed to `web-repoint:DECISIONS.md` directly (not deferred to the merge). **CORRECTED at round 5:** as first written that marker said the API half was "merged to master" — it was NOT (verified: `git ls-tree master` has no targets.ts, master's DECISIONS has no such section, `git branch --merged master` does not list the branch). So the pointer a future web chat was told to follow resolved to nothing, for the whole window in which the web card is most likely to be picked up — the same cross-branch citation class this card wrote a rule about, inverted: the citation's TARGET was what did not exist. Reworded to "on branch `nutrition-targets-api`, pending merge", and a pointer added to the TAIL of web-repoint's DECISIONS (Part I.6 S1 tells a new chat to read the tail; the marker at :377 is only found by someone already grepping the Card-5b approval). Round 4's stated exposure mechanism ("a chat reads the DECISIONS tail and finds the un-superseded approval") was itself inaccurate — the approval is at :376, not the tail — so the tail path was never covered until now.

## Nutrition targets API — T3 round 6 (2026-07-20; final round before merge)
- (SCOPE, agreed with Kd before the round) Round 6 was run as the LAST review, on the standing instruction to merge unless it found something in BEHAVIOUR, SECURITY, or the Done gate. It found none of those: the ported maths was hand-recomputed against nutrition.py:126-174 for a fifth time (all five goldens exact), the security pass came back clean for a sixth time, and every Done-gate claim verified true.
- (the one finding — a comment defect, no behavioural consequence) `DEFAULT_ACTIVITY`'s justification said "P2.7-migrated rows never pass through Zod at all". FALSE, verified: the migrator never writes `user_fitness_profiles` at ALL (INVENTORY.md:45 drops the onboarding fields; collections/users.ts:80 inserts only `users` columns), so Zod is the sole writer and the fallback is DEFENSIVE ONLY. Corrected in targets.ts and both DECISIONS entries; the CODE is unchanged and was always correct — R5.4 requires porting `.get(frequency, 1.55)` verbatim regardless of reachability.
- (THE PATTERN, worth more than the fix) This one comment has now been wrong TWICE IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS: round 3 removed a defence-in-depth claim with nothing behind it, and its correction asserted a HAZARD claim with nothing behind it. Rounds 4 and 5 both re-read the sentence and passed it, because each verified the half the previous round had flagged (the DB CHECK — true) and not the other half (the migration — false). **A correction is a new claim and inherits V1 in full**; and re-verifying a sentence means re-verifying every clause in it, not the clause last disputed. Same family as the round-5 finding (each guard tested against the previously-named mutation only) — the recurring failure in this card was never the code, it was verifying the case in front of me instead of the class.
- (round-6 mutation table, recorded so the guard's coverage is on file) The reviewer drove the `satisfies` clause through the repo's own tsc against the CLASS: baseline compiles · non-nullable string in the literal → TS2322 · omitted from the literal → TS1360 · key absent from TargetInputs → TS1360 · `string | undefined` → TS2322 · `string[]` → TS2322 · `?: string | null` compiles (intended — `== null` catches it at runtime). One shape still compiles: LAUNDERING the projection (`input.x ?? "default"` inside the literal), which is closed by the literal-five and all-five-null TESTS rather than by types. Noted in the code comment; judged not worth a further round.
- (local-master staleness, operational note) The reviewer flagged that a local `master` can sit behind `origin/master` and produce a wrong picture of what a branch carries. Against `origin/master` this branch is targets-only: 9 files, 521 insertions. Check branch content against `origin/master`, never a possibly-stale local ref.
- (PROVE, final) api **293/293** on Neon; typecheck + lint clean; endpoint driven end-to-end with curl. **READY TO MERGE** (V7): CI green is the closing gate, every T3 finding across six rounds is resolved and pushed.

## web repoint — nutrition targets WEB half (2026-07-21)
- (SCOPE) The Nutrition page's MacroRings + "Remaining today" repointed off backend-ml onto `GET /v1/nutrition/targets` (PR #42). This closes the D2 interim ruled at Card 5a and makes `nutritionApi.js` 100% new-API — `getTargets` was its LAST old-backend call, and the `mlApi` import is now gone from the file entirely.
- (THE PLAN DEFECT CAUGHT AT RE-VERIFICATION, recorded because the mechanism is the card's own subject) The approved plan said "render the honest state when `targets === null`". That would have SHIPPED A FLASH: the left column's spinner is owned by the MEALS fetch (`loadData` → setLoading, Nutrition.jsx:1566), while targets is a SEPARATE parallel request that never touches `loading` — so the column renders before targets arrive and would have shown "add your details" to every user for that window before swapping to their real numbers.
