It was invisible during planning for exactly the reason this card exists: `{}` → `|| 2000` made the not-yet-loaded state look correct. **A fabricated default did not just mislead the user, it hid a loading bug from the author.** Fixed with THREE distinct states (`undefined` not loaded · `null` no target · object real) and a gate on `loading || !targetsLoaded`.
- (toDisplayTargets, the silent-trap seam) The API speaks camelCase (`proteinG`); MacroRings and the Remaining card read snake_case (`protein_g`). The rename lives in ONE pure exported function, unit-tested per macro, because a missed rename yields `undefined` — which the old `|| 150` rendered as a plausible fake number rather than an error, i.e. the bug would have looked like success. A malformed/unreadable payload maps to `undefined`, NEVER `null`: telling a user their profile is incomplete when we merely failed to parse would be a fabricated claim of its own.
- (FABRICATED DEFAULTS DELETED — the Kd ruling delivered) MacroRings' `|| 2000 / 150 / 250 / 65` and the Remaining block's four are gone. Supersedes the Card-5b "(Remaining-today defaults)" approval, which was granted only while targets were dark for EVERYONE; with a real calculator live they would fire ONLY for the incomplete-profile user — precisely the person who must not see a stranger's goal rendered identically to their own. `totals || {}` STAYS (it defends against an undefined prop and invents nothing); `targets || {}` did NOT stay — it was REPLACED by the early return, since `targets` now carries meaning in all three of its states and coercing it to `{}` would erase exactly that. **CORRECTED at T3 (F6):** this line first claimed both guards were kept, which is false at MacroRings.jsx — the same wrong-comment class the API half spent commit 57443d7 fixing twice in opposite directions.
- (honest state, one prompt not two) `targets == null` → MacroRings renders `NoTargets`, naming the fields the SERVER said are missing in plain English (`heightCm`→"height", `exerciseFrequency`→"workouts per week") with a Settings link. An unknown key passes through untranslated rather than being dropped — naming fewer fields than are required would send someone to fix the wrong thing. The Remaining card is then HIDDEN on Today (two prompts would nag) but STILL RENDERS on a past day, where "Eaten on this day" is pure server-summed totals and needs no target.
- (usage guard tightened, not loosened) The guard test moved from `mlApi.(get|post|patch|delete)` to asserting NO import (`from '…mlApi'`) and NO call (`mlApi.`) — both vectors, since an unused import is dead code a later reader might revive. Deliberately not a bare `/mlApi/` grep: the header comment names the retired client to explain the history, and prose is not a dependency.
- (D2/D3 honored, no new API surface) `remaining = max(0, target − eaten)` stays a client-side subtraction of two SERVER-computed numbers — expressly allowed by D2 ("the old server's own display formula over new server-computed meal sums") and D3 ("display aggregation only"). The 2B ban is on the BROWSER deriving nutrition from foods and grams, which this is not.
- (PROVE) web **103/104** (the 1 = the known syncClient VITE_API_URL env quirk, `window is not defined` in node, CI-green — DECISIONS 2026-07-15) up from the 99/100 baseline, i.e. +4 new tests and nothing else disturbed · `vite build` ✓ · lint **7 errors = exact Nutrition.jsx baseline parity**, MacroRings.jsx and nutritionApi.js both still clean (0, their baseline) · both response arms driven live against the local API before handing over the smoke.
- (SMOKE — PASSED, Kd 2026-07-21, every step ✅) Complete profile → real server-computed rings (2638 kcal / 154 g / 341 g / 73 g on Kd's own profile — nothing like the deleted 2000/150/250/65) with "Remaining today" counting from them · Gender → "Not set" in Settings → Nutrition names **only gender** ("Add your gender to see your daily calorie and macro targets"), no rings, no numbers, Remaining card hidden, one prompt not two · restoring Gender brings the numbers straight back · F5 goes spinner → rings with NO flash of the honest prompt (the plan defect this card fixed, confirmed absent in the browser) · past day still shows "Eaten on this day" totals.
- (SMOKE METHOD CORRECTED BY THE RUN ITSELF, recorded — the test account was wrong, not the code) The handover proposed an "incomplete profile" account reached by skipping onboarding. **Not reachable:** Card 6 made onboarding REQUIRED (the "Skip for now" dead-end was removed), and the wizard collects all five calculator inputs — so every user who can reach the app has a complete profile, and a freshly-registered account cannot demonstrate the empty state. The REAL path into it is CLEARING a detail afterwards: Settings offers "Not set" for Gender (Settings.jsx:164) and Weekly Workout Target (:390), which save NULL (the Card-7 F2 ruling that removed fabricated defaults). Verified by API before re-issuing the steps: clearing gender → `{"targets":null,"missing":["gender"]}`, restoring it → the targets return. Any future smoke of this surface must use the clear-a-field path, not a new account.
- (T3 — FRESH CHAT, 6 findings, ALL fixed) **F1 (the serious one, R0.1)** `MacroRings` gated on `targets == null` — LOOSE equality, so `undefined` fell into the null branch: the three-state model survived `toDisplayTargets` and **died at its only consumer**. A failed/unreadable targets request therefore told a user with a COMPLETE profile "Add your profile details", sending them to Settings to fix nothing — precisely the fabricated claim `toDisplayTargets`' own contract comment promises never to make. The reviewer proved the branch is only ever reachable that way: the shared contract's `refine()` enforces `targets === null ⟺ missing.length > 0`, so the generic no-field wording could ONLY appear on a failure. FIX: `=== null` → `NoTargets`, `=== undefined` → a new `TargetsUnavailable` ("couldn't load your targets… your logged meals are unaffected") that blames the REQUEST, not the user. **The lesson is the shape of the mistake:** I ported `== null` from the API half, where it is correct (there, absence IS null-or-undefined), into the one place where the two values carry DIFFERENT meanings. An idiom is not portable across a semantic boundary.
- (T3 F2, same class) `remaining` repeated the loose check, so on a failed request the Remaining card vanished too, leaving the false prompt as the sole explanation. Now `=== null || === undefined` — both suppress the card (there is no target to subtract either way), but MacroRings tells them apart.
- (T3 F3, R10) The new `loading || !targetsLoaded` gate coupled the whole left column to a request with NO BOUND — `authApi` sets no global timeout, so a hung targets call would spin the column forever even though meals had returned (strictly worse than the pre-card behaviour, where `loading` alone released it). FIX: `TARGETS_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000` scoped to this call, degrading into F1's unavailable state. Changing the shared client's default is a separate decision, not smuggled in here.
- (T3 F4/F5, defence in depth) `toDisplayTargets` type-checked the CONTAINER but not the VALUES: a payload short one macro yielded `fat_g: undefined` → the ring rendered "/ undefinedg" and Remaining rendered `NaN`, which `|| 0` turned into a confident "0g left" invented from nothing — this card's own thesis one field short. Now every macro must be a finite number or the payload degrades to `undefined` (unavailable), never `null` (which would blame the profile). And `missingTargetLabels` used `?? []`, which THREW on a non-array — after `setTargets` had already stored good targets, so the catch discarded real numbers and showed the prompt. Now `Array.isArray`. Neither is producible by today's `.strict()` API; both are pinned by tests.
- (T3 diagnosability) `catch {}` swallowed the error entirely while the column now WAITS on that request — invisible AND load-bearing. Now logs `err?.message` (message only; the error object carries the request config, R3.10).
- (T3 SMOKE HOLE — owed on the re-smoke, the reviewer's Done-gate point) The passed smoke exercised only SUCCESS arms; F1's entire failure path was structurally invisible to it, the same class as the Card-4 CORS bug. The re-smoke MUST add: stop the API (or block `/v1/nutrition/targets` in devtools) → reload Nutrition → expect **"couldn't load your targets"**, NOT "add your profile details".
- (RE-SMOKE — PASSED, Kd 2026-07-21; the F1 failure path CONFIRMED IN A BROWSER) With `*nutrition/targets*` blocked in Chrome devtools (request row → "Block request URL") and the user still signed in, the Nutrition page renders **"Today's Macros — Couldn't load your targets just now. Refresh to try again — your logged meals are unaffected."** — NOT "Add your profile details". The F1 fix is proven on the exact path that would otherwise have shipped: a complete-profile user told to go fix a profile that was never broken.
- (SMOKE METHOD, corrected twice — record it so the next chat does not repeat both) (a) A freshly-registered "incomplete profile" account CANNOT demonstrate the empty state: onboarding is mandatory (Card 6) and collects all five calculator inputs. Use Settings → set Gender (or Weekly Workout Target) to "Not set" → Save. (b) Killing the whole API CANNOT demonstrate the targets FAILURE state either: AuthContext's `/v1/auth/me` fails first and signs the user out before Nutrition ever renders, so the tester never reaches the card. Block ONLY the targets URL in devtools, leaving auth alive. Both were learned by sending Kd down the wrong path — the code was right in both cases, the method was not.

## OPEN QUESTION for Kd — privacy-law scope is wider than DPDP (raised 2026-07-21)
- (RAISED BY KD, not yet ruled) Kd's point: the product should satisfy privacy law beyond India — EU/UK GDPR, US state laws (CCPA/CPRA et al), Brazil's LGPD — not DPDP alone. NOTHING HAS BEEN BUILT OR CHANGED on the strength of this; it is recorded so it cannot be lost, and it needs Kd's ruling before any card acts on it (R0.2 — the spec does not settle it).
- (what the spec actually says, command-verified) It is NOT purely India-scoped: Part 4 §3.5.2:829 heads the flow "DPDP/GDPR account deletion", 2B:538 says "under India's DPDP Act 2023 and GDPR for EU users", Part 3:143 treats the join-code as "the consent record (DPDP/GDPR)". But the spec NEVER enumerates the GDPR-specific deltas, and never mentions CCPA/CPRA or LGPD at all. So the direction is in-spec; the detail is a genuine SPEC GAP.
- (engineering assessment, so the ruling is informed) The Day-14 worker's MECHANISM is common to all of these: a hard-delete pass over an enumerated table list + a JSON export. The deltas are mostly TIMERS AND METADATA, not architecture — DPDP's 14 days vs GDPR's "without undue delay" (conventionally ≤30) vs CCPA's 45. **Consequence: building the worker now does NOT paint us into a corner, PROVIDED the retention window is one named constant rather than a hard-coded 14 scattered through the code, and the export shape is a list rather than a bespoke format.** The worker card should be built that way regardless of how Kd rules on scope.
- (what is NOT code, and is Kd's alone) Lawful basis / consent records, the privacy notice, breach-notification duties, data-processing agreements, an EU representative, and whether the product is even OFFERED in those jurisdictions at launch. These are policy decisions with legal consequences; a chat must not infer them (R0.2). Kd may also reasonably rule "India-only at launch, widen before an EU/US launch" — which is a legitimate answer that keeps the worker card unchanged.

## web repoint — Progress plan-limit notice (2026-07-21)
- (SCOPE) Closes T3 Card 3 finding f.4, owed since 2026-07-16: every `/v1/progress` response carries `limitedToDays` (the caller's plan history window, `null` = unlimited — shared/progress.ts on all SIX shapes) and the page ignored it entirely (grep over apps/web/src returned zero hits). A free user picking "1 Year" got 90 days of data captioned "in 1 Year": the numbers honest, the label not. Web-only; no API change.
- (THE TRAP, verified live by curl before any code) The server reports `limitedToDays` **UNCONDITIONALLY** — `workouts/service.ts:134,:231` return `gate.limitedToDays` without ever comparing it to the requested period. Proven against the running API on a free account: `period=7d` → `limitedToDays: 90`, same as `1y` and `all`. So the obvious implementation (`limitedToDays !== null` → show the notice) would tell a user viewing 7 days that their view is limited to 90 — **a warning about a limit that is not limiting: sourced from a real field, still false.** Same family as the fabricated calorie targets the previous card removed. `effectiveClamp` therefore compares the clamp against the REQUESTED window and returns null when it does not actually cut (7d/30d/90d under a 90-day plan all stay silent; `all` is unbounded so any finite clamp cuts it).
- (THE FACT EVERY STRING RESPECTS — read gate, NOT deletion) Kd asked directly whether users lose old history. They do not, and this was verified rather than assumed: `seed.ts:44` says `history_days: 90, // read-gate, not deletion (Part 4 §0.2)`; `workouts/repo.ts:183,:285` merely add `AND started_at >= since`; a grep for any DELETE of workouts returns NOTHING. Upgrading sets `history_days: -1` (proEntitlements, seed.ts:30), the floor becomes null and the entire history reappears instantly — no restore step. The notice copy is written to that fact: "Your plan shows the last 90 days, so this view stops there. **Older workouts are still saved.**" A notice implying data loss would be its own falsehood, and is exactly what a user currently infers from a "1 Year" label over 90 days.
- (the caption is half the bug) A notice sitting beside a stat still captioned "in 1 Year" only half-fixes it, so `clampNotice` also returns the corrected caption ("the last 90 days"), consumed at BOTH sites — the Workouts StatCard sub and the weekly-chart footnote. Unclamped periods keep the existing label verbatim, so paid plans and short windows are visually untouched.
- (also covered, found while grounding) `consistencyPct` (workouts/service.ts:222-223) normally reports 0 for 'all', but when the plan floor bites it computes a REAL percentage over the visible 90 days — so a free user on "All Time" sees a genuine figure derived from a window the page never named. The corrected caption covers this site too; no separate treatment needed.
- (single source, deliberately) `limitedToDays` is read from the OVERVIEW response only. All six carry the same gate value by construction (one `historyGate` call per request), so six pieces of state could never disagree.
- (no upsell, deliberate) Copy is neutral and names no plan or price. Selling is the paywall card's job (P3.9); a limit notice that upsells reads as a dark pattern on a screen the user opened to see their own data.
- (PROVE) web **113/114** (the 1 = the known syncClient VITE_API_URL env quirk, CI-green) — +8 new tests · `vite build` ✓ · lint **0 errors on Progress.jsx = its baseline, verified by stashing**, and the two new files clean · the clamp verified live on a real free account by curl (7d/1y/all all return 90) before any smoke was written.
- (SMOKE — PASSED, Kd 2026-07-21) On a real free account: 7 Days / 30 Days / 90 Days show NO notice (all inside the 90-day allowance — the trap case, and it stayed silent), while 1 Year and All Time show the notice and flip the Workouts caption to "in the last 90 days". Empty charts throughout (the fixture account has no workouts) — not what the card tests.
- (T3 — FRESH CHAT, 6 findings, ALL fixed) **The two that mattered: the notice closed only HALF of f.4.** `effectiveClamp` is period-driven, but TWO cards on the page read PERIOD-LESS endpoints that are gated anyway — so they stayed silent while their own headings lied, at exactly the three periods (7d/30d/90d) where the notice correctly says nothing. (a) The heatmap fixes `since = clamp(now−365d, gate.floor)` (service.ts:257-259), so a 90-day plan cuts it at EVERY period while the heading read a hard-coded "Activity — Last 365 Days" — f.4's exact lie, one card lower on the same page, shipped by the card that existed to remove it. (b) `personalRecords` passes `gate.floor` with NO period at all (:281), so a gated user's records are 90-day records always. FIXED with `heatmapCaption` / `recordsNote`, each captioning from ITS OWN window rather than the selected period. **`longestStreak` is carved out by name** — it is deliberately ungated (service.ts:288-290, "streaks are identity, not history reads"), so a flat card-level "last 90 days" would have been a NEW inaccuracy introduced by the honesty fix.
- (T3 R9 — the one string the user reads was the one string no test covered) The module pluralises and the suite pinned "the last 1 day", then the JSX hard-coded `the last {clamp.days} days` and would have rendered "the last 1 days". The notice now interpolates the TESTED caption; the duplicate wording is gone.
- (T3 R1) `PERIODS` lived twice — in the page and as `PERIOD_LABELS` here — with no link, so a period added to one would silently degrade the other's caption to a fallback. One exported list now, and it is node-testable (the page's copy was not).
- (T3 low, caught by Kd's own smoke conditions) The notice rendered ABOVE the empty state, so a gated user with zero workouts read "Older workouts are still saved" directly above "No workout data yet" — precisely the account the smoke ran on. Gated on `!isEmpty`.
- (T3 V1/V3 — a false claim in my own record, struck) The entry above asserted "the tests pin that the SMALLEST clamp wins rather than the most generous". No such test exists and nothing reconciles six values — only the overview's field is read. Struck rather than reworded. Third instance in this project of a CORRECTION or a self-description outrunning what the code does; the standing lesson holds — a claim about the artifact is verified against the artifact, not against intent.
- (PROVE, post-T3) web **120/121** (the 1 = the known syncClient env quirk) — 15 clamp tests · `vite build` ✓ · lint 0 on all three files = baseline parity.
- (RE-SMOKE — PASSED, Kd 2026-07-21, and it caught a bug the T3 fix CREATED) Verified live: no notice at 7d with the corrected "Activity — Last 90 Days" heading, the notice + corrected caption at 1y/all, the records note with its all-time-streak carve-out, and no notice stranded above "No workout data yet". **Kd then reported having to scroll sideways to reach the grid — a real defect, mine.** `ActivityHeatmap` hard-coded `subDays(today, 364)`, so making the HEADING honest left the GRID drawing a full year: 53 columns of mostly-empty squares under a heading promising 90 days, with the actual data three months off-screen to the right. The heading and the grid disagreed — the identical fault this card exists to remove, introduced by the fix for it. The grid now draws `windowDays = limitedToDays ?? 365`, i.e. exactly the window it is captioned with.
- (Kd smoke ask, folded in — GitHub-style labelling) The grid rendered bare squares with no way to tell WHEN anything happened. Added: month labels above the column where each month starts, a Mon/Wed/Fri weekday gutter (GitHub's own subset — labelling all seven crowds 12px cells), and a readable tooltip ("19 Jul 2026 — 1 workout, 210 kcal", was a raw `yyyy-MM-dd`). Also fixed silently-wrong alignment: the old code chunked a raw day list into 7s, so each ROW held an arbitrary weekday rather than a fixed one; the grid now pads back to the week boundary and the padding cells render empty rather than as false zero-days.
- (window semantics, Kd asked "what if someone has trained more than 90 days") Free plan → 90 days; paid → 365 (verified: `proEntitlements.history_days: -1`, seed.ts:30, so the clamp never applies). Beyond a year is shown to NOBODY: `progressHeatmap` is a fixed 365-day read by design (service.ts:257-259, "Last 365 days fixed", progress.py:194). Older workouts still exist and still count toward Total Workouts and Longest Streak — they are simply not drawn on that one chart. Nothing hidden permanently, nothing deleted.

## web repoint — timezone capture (2026-07-21)
- (THE BUG, owed since 2026-07-11 and tracked nowhere until the 2026-07-21 OWED audit) The web never sent a timezone, so `users.timezone` stayed NULL for every account and the server's day-bucketing fell back to UTC (DECISIONS 2026-07-11 P2.3 GAP-3). Streaks and "today" therefore rolled over at the wrong local hour for every user outside UTC — in a Jorhat pilot, all of them (05:30 IST instead of midnight). Playbook trap #8 names this exact failure. Verified before building: `PATCH /v1/users/me` has accepted `timezone` since P2.2 (users.ts:40, `.trim().min(1).max(64).nullable().optional()`), and a grep over apps/web found the field sent NOWHERE — so this is a pure WEB card, no API change, no migration.
- (design — the client reports, the server decides) The browser sends only its IANA zone; every day boundary stays server-side. The trap this card must not become is client-side day maths, and it does not add any. Streak state is RECOMPUTED from full history at each sync (DECISIONS 2026-07-11 P2.3 T3 finding 1), so an existing account's streaks heal on their next sync once the zone lands — no backfill needed.
- (`detectTimezone` never guesses) `Intl…resolvedOptions().timeZone` inside a try/catch, returning null if the environment cannot say. An unreadable environment must LEAVE THE STORED VALUE ALONE, because assuming a zone is precisely what caused the bug.
- (`timezoneUpdate` — write only on a real change) Returns null when the stored value already matches, so a page load is not a write; TRIMS because the contract trims and a stored `" Asia/Kolkata"` would otherwise never compare equal and would re-PATCH forever; caps at the schema's max(64) so a bizarre value degrades to silence rather than a 400. Pure and unit-tested (5 cases incl. the 64/65-char boundary).
- (best-effort, the argon2 precedent) A failed write is logged (`err?.message` only — the error object carries the request config, R3.10) and never breaks an otherwise-valid session. Fire-and-forget so it cannot delay rendering or hold the loading spinner.
- (one profile read, two facts) `fetchOnboardingFlag` became `fetchProfileFacts`, returning the gate flag AND the stored timezone from the SAME request the session adoption already made — no extra round trip.
- (SMOKE FINDING — Kd caught a duplicate write the tests could not see) The first smoke showed **TWO** PATCHes on first login: `login()` and the session-restore effect both adopt a session, both read the profile before either write lands, so both saw a null timezone and both wrote. Same value twice — harmless, but a duplicate write on every first login. FIXED with a module-level `timezoneSynced` guard set BEFORE the await, so the second caller cannot slip through the window. **Invisible to node-vitest by construction** (it needs two concurrent mounts in a real browser) — the same class as the Card-4 CORS bug; the browser is the only instrument that sees it.
- (SMOKE — PASSED, Kd 2026-07-21) Payload confirmed `{"timezone":"Asia/Calcutta"}` on a fresh account; after the fix a login writes ONCE and a reload issues only GETs (both rows `userApi.js:112` = getProfile, no `:114` = updateProfile). **Definitive check: the account's stored timezone reads back `Asia/Calcutta` written BY THE BROWSER** — the fixture was created untouched and never PATCHed by curl. (NB Chrome reports the legacy `Asia/Calcutta` alias rather than `Asia/Kolkata`; both are valid IANA ids and the column is free text ≤64.)
- (SMOKE METHOD, recorded — I burned two rounds on it) A smoke account must be UNTOUCHED: the first fixture had already been stamped by my own curl drive, so the app correctly did nothing and the absence of a PATCH proved the wrong half. And devtools must be open BEFORE the action — a panel opened afterwards shows "Currently recording…" and has captured nothing.
- (STILL OWED, unchanged by this card) The §3.5 timezone-TRAVEL rule (a day is kept if it qualifies in either the stored-at-the-time zone or the new one) is NOT implemented — streak replay uses only the current `users.timezone`. It was shielded while everyone was UTC; capturing real zones makes it reachable, so it stays on OWED.md as its own mechanic rather than being silently absorbed here.
- (PROVE) web **125/126** (the 1 = the known syncClient env quirk) — +5 timezone tests, userApi 15→20 · `vite build` ✓ · lint 5 on AuthContext.jsx = its exact baseline, userApi.js clean · endpoint driven end-to-end by curl (null → PATCH 200 → reads back) before the smoke.
- (T3 — FRESH CHAT, 5 findings, ALL fixed; and it caught a LIVE defect I had half-seen and mis-framed) **F1, the real one:** `timezoneSynced` was module-level and never reset, and `logout()` left it TRUE — so the SECOND account signed in during one page load never got its timezone written. On a shared browser (gym front desk, a phone passed between people) every account after the first stays UTC-bucketed: **this card's own bug, reintroduced for everyone but the first user.** I had flagged "might wrongly suppress a legitimate later write" in the handover but illustrated it with a passenger changing zones mid-flight — rare enough to shrug at. The reachable case needs no travel at all, and picking the exotic example is what made it look unimportant. FIXED: `resetTimezoneSync()` exported and called in logout's `finally` beside `adoptSession(null)`.
- (T3 F2 — I OVERCLAIMED, for the fourth time in this project) The duplicate-PATCH fix shipped with NO regression test, justified as "invisible to node-vitest by construction — it needs two concurrent mounts in a real browser". FALSE: the window is plain single-threaded JS with no DOM in it; the only thing preventing a test was that `syncTimezone` lived in the JSX file and was not exported. Moved to `userApi.js` beside `timezoneUpdate` and now covered by `await Promise.all([syncTimezone(null), syncTimezone(null)])` asserting exactly ONE patch — plus the logout-reset case, the unknown-stored case, and a never-rejects case. R9.5 ("no regression test, no fix") was not satisfied by the original commit and is now.
- (T3 F3) `fetchProfileFacts`' catch returned `timezone: null`, conflating "the profile read FAILED" with "the server HAS no timezone" — so a transient GET blip produced a PATCH, precisely when the client knows least, and precisely the write the card's own "a page load is not a write" rule exists to prevent. Now returns `undefined`, and `syncTimezone` returns early on it.
- (T3 F4) `timezoneUpdate` type-guarded `detected` but not `stored`, so `(stored ?? '').trim()` threw on a non-string — inside a `void`-discarded promise, i.e. an unhandled rejection. The web does not Zod-parse the profile response, so nothing upstream guaranteed a string. Guarded + tested.
- (T3 F5, accepted and recorded) `AuthContext.jsx` has ZERO test coverage — the shape change and both call sites are unexercised; only the pure helpers are tested. This is the standing web-package gap (node-vitest, no jsdom, R1.4); moving `syncTimezone` out of the JSX file shrinks it, but the wiring itself is still smoke-only.
- (T3 privacy flag, recorded not actioned) This begins capturing a new item of personal data with no consent or notice copy, while Kd's privacy-law scope question is an open ruling (commit a81f9a0). It lives on the `users` row so the DPDP cascade covers deletion. Recorded here so the eventual privacy card inherits it.
- (RE-SMOKE — PASSED, Kd 2026-07-21, the shared-browser case) Two untouched accounts, both `timezone=None` before: log in as A → log out WITHOUT refreshing → log in as B. **Both now read back `Asia/Calcutta` from the API** — B is the account that would have been stranded on UTC before the fix. NB row-counting in devtools is ambiguous here and must not be used as the proof: `userApi.js:114` is `updateProfile`, which serves BOTH the timezone write AND onboarding's weight save, so a user completing onboarding legitimately shows two `:114` rows. The stored value is the only unambiguous evidence.
- (LOOP VIOLATION, recorded) This card was COMMITTED BEFORE ITS T3 (aba87bc), against Part I §2's order — the same shortcut recorded as a protocol failure on Card 5c. It was caught and self-reported immediately, the T3 then ran on the commit, and its five findings are fixed here; the branch does not reach master until P2.8, so the review still gated the merge. Recorded rather than quietly corrected.
- (PROVE, post-T3) web **130/131** (the 1 = the known syncClient env quirk) — userApi 20→25 tests · `vite build` ✓ · lint 5 on AuthContext.jsx = exact baseline, userApi.js clean.

## OWED-list correction for the coach retry card (2026-07-21; on web-repoint)
- (WHY THIS COMMIT EXISTS, and it is a branch-topology problem worth knowing) The `coach-idempotency` API card cannot record its own deferrals: **`OWED.md` does not exist on master at all**, and master's `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` has no owed-endpoints block — both live only here (verified: `git ls-tree origin/master` shows no OWED.md; `git show origin/master:RUNBOOK/cutover.md` has no "Coach chat retry" line). So CLAUDE.md's "every deferral gets its OWED line in the SAME commit" is unsatisfiable from a master-bound card, and its three deferrals would have lived only as prose in a 400-line diary — the exact way the two audit misses of 2026-07-21 (Google login, timezone capture) got lost. Kd ruled to add them here now rather than wait for the web half.
- (THE CORRECTION THAT MATTERS MOST — a wrong estimate in THREE places) `OWED.md`, `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` and `apps/web/src/api/coachApi.js` all told the next reader that the remaining client work is "one header line". **Verified false** while building the API half: `Coach.jsx` clears the message box on send and never restores it on failure, and there is NO retry affordance anywhere — so a key minted inside `sendMessage` differs on every attempt and dedupes nothing, while the user's only route back is retyping, which is a new message. The real client half is a **"Try again" control that resends with the SAME key**. All three now say so. Left uncorrected, whoever picked up the last cutover gate item would have mispriced it as a one-liner and shipped protection that protects nothing.
- (three lines ADDED, per the same rule) The Try-again control; the pre-existing empty-thread-on-failure bug (narrowed by verification to the FIRST message of a new conversation, and never when the coach is unconfigured); and — flagged for a Kd ruling, nothing built on it — the quota slot spent when the provider never answers, which Groq's 6,000-token/minute free tier makes reachable at roughly two coach questions a minute (measured: ~2,573 tokens per real call, from this project's own `api_cost_events` rows).
- (scope) Records only — no behaviour change on this branch. `coachApi.js` is a comment edit; web suite 130/131 (the 1 = the known syncClient `window is not defined` env quirk, CI-green, DECISIONS 2026-07-15), coachApi.js lint clean.

## Coach retry protection — OWED additions from the API card's round-2 review (2026-07-21)
- (added to the Try-again OWED line, because it is the SAME catch block) The API card's second review found that `Coach.jsx` maps EVERY 429 to the quota copy ("You've used all your coach questions… or upgrade for more"), while the API now also returns 429 with `error: 'rate_limited'` for the new 10-per-minute burst cap. Consequence on the SHIPPED screen, reachable today and not waiting on any other card: a free user with four questions left who sends quickly is told to UPGRADE. The API is right and says the two facts must not be confused; the client confuses them. Fix is one branch on `err.response?.data?.error`, folded into the Try-again card rather than done as a drive-by (R1.1) — they edit the same lines.
- (SMOKE ownership, recorded so it cannot be lost between the two halves) The API half ships three new client-visible responses (429 `rate_limited`, 409 `request_in_flight`, 400 `idempotency_key_mismatch`) but the current client sends no Idempotency-Key, so only the cap's 429 is reachable from a browser — the replay and mismatch paths cannot be smoked until the Try-again control exists. Per CLAUDE.md Part I §2 ("a card whose UI cannot be reached states that explicitly"), the API card records that, and the full browser smoke is owed WITH this web card and listed on its OWED line.

## Coach chat retry protection — Idempotency-Key + short-window cap (2026-07-21)
- (THE OWED ITEM) DECISIONS 2026-07-12 (P2.5b T3 minor) recorded that `/v1/coach/chat` had no per-route rate limit and accepted no Idempotency-Key, and Kd's D1(b) ruling 2026-07-16 tied the fix to "when the client is wired" — web Card 4 wired it, so the API side came due. 🔴 in OWED.md and a blocking checkbox in RUNBOOK/cutover.md. NO migration, NO new dependency, NOTHING added to `packages/shared` (it carries BODY contracts; this travels as a request header — verified by grep: the word "header" appears nowhere in `packages/shared/src`).
- (THE THREE HARMS, each re-verified by command this session rather than taken from the kickoff) (1) **a second THREAD** — `service.chat` calls `repo.createThread` whenever the request omits `threadId`, and `apps/web/src/api/coachApi.js` omits it for the first message of a conversation; (2) **a second QUOTA SLOT** — `requireQuota` performs the INCREMENT itself and runs as a preHandler, i.e. before the handler and before the answer cache is consulted, and free coach is 5 questions/MONTH (seed.ts); (3) **DUPLICATE MESSAGES** — the answer-cache hit path calls `repo.appendExchange` too. **NOT true, and the kickoff's earlier claim was corrected before code:** a retry usually costs no second Groq call — the exact-match answer cache absorbs an identical question for 24h, and a second provider call happens only inside the in-flight window (the cache is written after the reply). This is a DATA-INTEGRITY card, not a money card.
- (PLACEMENT IS THE CARD) Both guards are preHandlers sitting AFTER `validateChatBody` and BEFORE `requireQuota`. Anywhere later and a replay still burns a quota slot while every obvious test passes. **Verified, not assumed:** a throwaway Fastify 5 probe confirmed that a preHandler which sends a reply short-circuits the REMAINING preHandlers and the handler (neither ran), that `onSend` still fires on the error path with the mapped status, that the `onSend` payload is a string, and that a preHandler throw reaches the central error mapper. The probe was deleted; the behaviours it proved are what the design rests on.
- (KD-APPROVED NUMBERS, not spec values — R0.2, the onboarding-bounds precedent) Burst cap **10 messages / 60 s per user**; replay window **10 minutes**; a recognised replay carries an **`Idempotent-Replay: true`** response header. The cap number was chosen so that a FREE user meets their monthly limit first: with 5 questions/month and a cap of 10/minute, questions 1-5 answer and the 6th reports `quota_exceeded` — the true reason — where a 5/minute cap would have said "slow down" at exactly that request. **CORRECTED at T3 (R11.3): the original wording here claimed the cap "can never fire BEFORE the quota limit", which is FALSE TWICE and the repo contradicts it both ways.** Pro coach is `{window:"day", limit:30}` (seed.ts), so a pro user sending 11 in a minute IS capped having used 11 of 30; and this card's own test asserts that a free user ALREADY out of quota gets `rate_limited` on an 11th rapid request. The mechanism is right; the sentence was a universal claim written from the one case in front of me — the same "verify the class, not the case" failure this file has recorded three times.
- (THE DESIGN CORRECTION THAT MAKES THE CARD WORK) The plan originally released the key on failure so a retry could proceed. That is self-defeating here: `createThread` commits on its own BEFORE the provider is called, so a failed first message already leaves an empty thread behind, and a released key means the retry opens a SECOND one — harm 1, in the exact "message fails, user sends again" scenario the card exists for. Instead the failed attempt's thread id is remembered (`coach:idem:t:*`) and the retry CONTINUES IN IT. `service.chat` gained one optional `onThreadOpened` hook, fired the instant the row commits, so the guard learns the id even when the request later throws — no error-class surgery and it covers every failure type, not just `CoachError`.
- (KEY LAYOUT, and why three keys) `coach:idem:r:{userId}:{sha256(key)}` stores the completed answer (10 min); `coach:idem:c:*` is the atomic in-flight claim (**120 s only**); `coach:idem:t:*` holds a failed attempt's thread. The record is read BEFORE the claim, so a process that died mid-request can never shadow a genuinely stored answer, and the short claim TTL means such a crash strands the key for two minutes rather than the full replay window. `incrWithTtl` is the atomic primitive (real Redis = a Lua INCR+EXPIRE script; the in-memory impl is synchronous JS). All keys are userId-scoped (R3.2) — a stranger's key can never resolve to another person's answer or thread, pinned by a cross-user route test.
- (ALL BOOKKEEPING IN ONE `onSend` HOOK, deliberately) A request can be turned away by a LATER preHandler — the cap, or `requireQuota`'s 429 — without the handler ever running, and that claim still has to be released or an honest retry meets a 409 instead of the real reason it failed. A hook is the only place that sees every outcome. Success stores the answer and drops the orphan-thread key; any other status releases the claim and, if a thread was opened, remembers it.
- (FAIL-OPEN, per the coach doctrine) Redis down → `incrWithTtl` returns null → the question proceeds WITHOUT dedupe and the skip is logged (`coach.idempotency_skipped_redis_down`). Coach quota already fails open (quotas/service.ts `FAIL_OPEN.coach`); refusing a legitimate first question because Redis blipped is worse than a rare duplicate. Never silent — the log line is the signal.
- (an unreadable stored record: THIS attempt refuses, the record is DELETED) Re-running immediately would duplicate, which is the one thing the file exists to prevent — so the attempt 409s. But the record is dropped first, so the NEXT attempt under the same key re-runs cleanly. **AMENDED at T3 round 2 (R11.3):** as first written this line justified the 409 with "the user can always send under a fresh key, so nothing is truly stranded" — false for the client this card is built for, which retries with the SAME key by design, and the code was changed at round 1 (F9) to stop relying on it. The rationale is corrected here rather than left describing a design that no longer exists.
- (NOT the auth module's `createDualRateLimit`, and why) That limiter also counts a per-IP dimension, which on a gym's shared connection would throttle everyone behind one NAT address — wrong for a gym-focused product. Same fixed-window mechanics, one dimension (per user). Recorded because the obvious reviewer question is "why not reuse it".
- (HEADER IS OPTIONAL, Kd-agreed) Mandating it would break the client shipped by web Card 4, which sends none. Keyless requests behave exactly as before and are protected by the cap alone — pinned by a test asserting two identical keyless sends still produce two threads.
- (CORRECTION TO THE CARD BRIEF — the web half is NOT one line) The brief (and `coachApi.js`'s own note) said the client change is one header line in `sendMessage`. Verified false: `Coach.jsx` CLEARS the message box on send and never restores it on failure, and there is NO retry affordance anywhere — so a fresh id minted inside `sendMessage` would differ on every attempt and dedupe nothing, while a user's only route back is retyping, which is a new composition. The client half therefore needs a real **"Try again"** control on the failed reply that resends with the SAME key. Kd ruled to do it properly rather than ship the ineffective one-liner; it is its own commit on `web-repoint`. **Now recorded on `web-repoint:OWED.md` (commit c488dda, 2026-07-21) — see the OWED-BRANCH note at the end of this section for why it could not land in this commit.**
- (PRE-EXISTING BUG FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, reported not fixed — R1.1) A coach message that fails AFTER the thread is created leaves an EMPTY conversation in the sidebar even when the user never retries (thread committed before the provider call; messages only appended on success). Narrower than first stated: only for the FIRST message of a new conversation, and not at all when the coach is unconfigured (that 503 precedes creation). This card's retry path now REUSES such a thread, but a never-retried failure still leaves one. Fixing it properly means creating the thread in the same transaction as the exchange, which touches the ledger transaction and is not this card's scope. **Now recorded on `web-repoint:OWED.md` (commit c488dda, 2026-07-21) — see the OWED-BRANCH note at the end of this section for why it could not land in this commit.**
- (TEST-INFRASTRUCTURE FINDING, recorded because the next suite will hit it) The first red run failed 5 of 7 new tests at REGISTRATION with `rate_limited`, not on the feature: the auth limiter allows 20 register+login attempts per HOUR per IP (DECISIONS 2026-07-11 GAP-4) and every `inject()` shares 127.0.0.1 — the existing file already sat at ~16. The limiter was NOT cleared or weakened; each fixture user now registers from its own `remoteAddress`, exactly as nine real users would. A suite that disarms a production control to make itself pass proves less than it appears to.
- (the concurrency test asserts the INVARIANTS, not a winner) Two simultaneous sends can legitimately end either way — the loser is turned away as `request_in_flight`, or it is fast enough to be served the stored reply. Asserting one of those would be flaky, so the test accepts both and pins what the guard actually promises: ONE provider call, ONE thread, ONE exchange.
- (RESIDUAL, honest — RESTATED IN FULL at T3 round 2, R11.3: the first version recorded only half of it) A crash between the claim and `onSend` does more than strand the key for the 120 s in-flight TTL. **`onSend` never runs at all in that window, so nothing is written either:** no answer record, and no thread memory. Consequences after the TTL expires: the retry opens a SECOND thread (harm 1 reopens), and if the crash landed after `appendExchange` had already committed, that retry also writes a SECOND exchange (harm 3 reopens). Bounded to a process death inside a single request — the ordinary failure paths all run `onSend` and are covered — but it is a real hole and the earlier one-clause version understated it. The other residual stands as written: if the answer fails to store (a Redis blip at write time) the claim is released anyway, so the retry re-runs rather than meeting a 409 it could never satisfy — losing dedupe beats a lockout. Both paths are logged. Closing the crash window properly needs the claim and the record to be one durable write (an outbox, or moving idempotency into Postgres) — the same architectural answer already owed for the ledger (OWED.md, "Outbox for external-call ledger writes"), and not this card's scope.
- (CURL DRIVE, end-to-end against the real Groq before any handover) Fresh account → send with `Idempotency-Key` → 200 → **replay of the same key returned a BYTE-IDENTICAL body with `idempotent-replay: true`**, one thread, two messages. Then four more distinct questions and a sixth: the sixth answered `quota_exceeded`, which is the harm-2 proof — had either replay metered, the monthly limit would have arrived one request EARLIER.
- (AN OPERATIONAL FINDING THE DRIVE FORCED OUT, pre-existing and NOT caused by this card — but it changes how the coach behaves in a smoke) Two drive questions returned 503. Chased rather than waved through: the api log showed `coach.provider_failed / errName:"groq"`, which is thrown ONLY at service.ts's provider call — a path this card does not touch (a keyless request runs one `incrWithTtl` in the cap and nothing else; the onSend hook returns immediately when no key is present). Direct curl to Groq with the same key and model answered 200 three times, so it is neither a dead key nor the decommissioned model. **The cause is Groq's FREE-TIER TOKEN CEILING: `x-ratelimit-limit-tokens: 6000` per minute** (read from a live response header), while a real coach call costs **~2,573 tokens** — measured exactly, from this card's own `api_cost_events` rows (2893, 2778, 2580, 2031, 2585). **That is roughly TWO coach questions a minute before Groq refuses and the app returns its clean 503.** Consequences: (a) a browser smoke MUST pace questions or it will see "Sorry, I ran into an error" and read it as a bug in this card; (b) the "message fails, user sends again" scenario this card was written for is far more common on the free tier than the brief assumed, which raises the card's value rather than lowering it; (c) the Groq model-migration card (OWED, hard date 2026-08-16) should re-read this header for whatever model replaces `llama-3.1-8b-instant`, since the ceiling is per-tier and per-model.
- (INTERACTION WORTH A KD RULING, raised NOT actioned — S4) Because `requireQuota` increments BEFORE the handler runs (the ported quotas.py:43-46 doctrine, "a request that fails later still consumed a slot"), a Groq TPM refusal SPENDS one of a free user's five monthly coach questions and returns no answer — and with this card a same-key retry spends another, because it does real work. So on the free tier a user can burn their whole month on failures without ever receiving an answer. That is PRE-EXISTING and deliberately ported, so it is not changed here (R0.2/R1.1); but the 6000-token ceiling above makes it reachable in ordinary use rather than theoretical. Refunding the slot when the provider never answered is a small, contained change to the coach route's failure path, and it is Kd's call, not a chat's. **Now recorded on `web-repoint:OWED.md` (commit c488dda, 2026-07-21) — see the OWED-BRANCH note below.**

## Coach chat retry protection — T3 resolution (2026-07-21; fresh chat run by Kd)
Nine findings, ALL real, ALL fixed. Each was re-verified against the artifact before fixing — a finding is a claim and inherits V1 too.
- (T3 F1, R9.2 — the worst, and my fix for it was ALSO wrong) The remembered thread was cleared only on a 200, and `repo.deleteThread` is a HARD delete. So a user who tidied away the empty conversation a failed attempt left behind had every retry under that key inject a dead id → `service.chat` threw 404 "thread not found" for a request that never mentioned a thread — for the full replay window. The reviewer's suggested fix (also clear the key on a 404) was implemented first and the new TEST CAUGHT IT AS INSUFFICIENT: it heals the attempt AFTER the failure, so the user's first "Try again" still 404s **and still spends a quota slot**, because the handler runs after the meter. REAL FIX: the remembered thread is no longer written into the parsed body at all. It travels as `hooks.resumeThreadId`, and `service.chat` resumes it ONLY if `repo.getThread` (userId-scoped, R3.2) still finds it, otherwise opens a fresh one. The distinction is now semantic and correct: a client-supplied `threadId` that is missing is the CLIENT being wrong and must 404; a remembered one that is missing just means the user deleted it. Pinned by a test that fails, deletes the orphan, retries, and requires a 200 in a NEW thread.
- (T3 F2, R9.2 — the hook's whole reason for existing was untested) The only release-on-failure test drove a 503 thrown from the HANDLER. The quota 429 is `reply.send()` from a PREHANDLER — a different Fastify path and the one the hook was written for. Added: a user at their monthly limit sends with key K → `quota_exceeded`; the same key again → `quota_exceeded`, NOT `request_in_flight`. Without the release, a user who is merely out of questions would be told their message is in flight for 120 s — the wrong reason on the one screen where the reason is the whole message.
- (T3 F3, R8.1 — an error message describing a check the code does not perform) It promised the key "must be a single non-empty value", but `idempotency-key` is not special-cased by Node, so duplicate headers arrive JOINED as "a, b": one string, under the cap, silently accepted. The array arm is reachable only via `inject()`, never over HTTP. Reworded to drop "single" rather than reject on ", " — a joined duplicate is stable per client, so dedupe still behaves, and rejecting a comma could refuse a legitimate key. The array guard stays as defence and now says so.
- (T3 F4, R11.3 — THREE claims in my own record that were false) This entry asserted "its own OWED line", "Own OWED line" and "Recorded on OWED for Kd". **None of them existed.** Exactly the class this project has recorded repeatedly: a record describing what the author meant to do rather than what the repo contains. They were first rewritten to say "NOT YET"; Kd then ruled to add them, and `web-repoint` commit **c488dda** did so (OWED.md + cutover.md + coachApi.js). **CORRECTED AGAIN at T3 round 2 (R11.3):** the "NOT YET" wording survived the commit that made it false — five separate places still said the lines were missing and that all three files still read "one header line", after c488dda had added them and corrected all three. So the fix for a stale claim went stale itself, inside one card. The standing lesson holds and now has a fourth instance: **when an action makes a record obsolete, the record is part of the action** — this is the same shape as the round-1 F1 fix, whose own comment had to be re-corrected after the redesign.
- (T3 F5, R11.3 — a universal claim the repo contradicts twice) "The cap can never fire BEFORE the quota limit" is false for a pro user (30/day: capped at 11 of 30) and false in this card's own test (a free user already out of quota gets `rate_limited` on the 11th). Narrowed to what is actually true. Written from the single case in front of me — the "mutate the class, not the case" failure again.
- (T3 F6, R0.3 — an unlabelled deviation presented as the rule) R3.3's written order is "… → entitlement → quota → rate limit → handler", and `coachRateCap` is placed BEFORE `requireQuota`, yet the comment cited R3.3 as though it were compliant. **The inversion is DELIBERATE and stands:** `requireQuota` increments the counter itself, so a request refused by the cap would otherwise still spend one of a free user's five monthly questions — the rule's own intent (never charge for work not done) is better served by inverting it. Now labelled as a deviation in both the code comment and here, instead of hiding behind the rule number.
- (T3 F7, R9 — a test that could fail for a reason unrelated to the code) The cap test needed 11 round trips to real Postgres inside a real 60-second window; overrunning it would surface as "quota_exceeded on the 11th", i.e. a red that looks like a code fault. `createMemoryRedis` already accepts an injectable clock and was not being used. The test now runs on a FROZEN clock, and — since time is controlled — additionally asserts the window RESETS after 61 s, which wall-clock testing could not have done at all.
- (T3 F8, R7) `coachIdem` was declared on FastifyRequest but never `decorateRequest`-ed, unlike every other request property in the repo. Added beside `coachChatInput`.
- (T3 F9, R11.3 — "nothing is truly stranded" was true for curl, not for the shipped client) An unreadable stored record threw 409 and never cleared it, locking that key for the full 10 minutes; the escape hatch I claimed ("send under a fresh key") is exactly what the same-key "Try again" control will never do. Now the unreadable record is DELETED before the 409: this attempt still refuses rather than duplicate on a guess, and the next one re-runs cleanly.
- (T3 security pass — clean, and one residual now stated in the code) Tenancy verified sound: all four keys carry the user id, the raw key is SHA-256'd before becoming key material (so a `:`-laden key cannot escape its namespace), and the resumed thread still passes through the userId-scoped `getThread`. The reviewer confirmed the placement genuinely closes harm 2 for a replay, and that atomicity holds. Residual now written into the file's own comment: served replays and rejected keys short-circuit BEFORE the cap, so the cap protects requests that do real work, not every request on the route.
- (OWED-BRANCH PROBLEM — RAISED, RULED, AND ACTIONED 2026-07-21) `OWED.md` does not exist on master at all, and master's `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` lacks the owed-endpoints block that carries the coach checkbox — both live only on `web-repoint` (verified: `git ls-tree origin/master` has no OWED.md; `git show origin/master:RUNBOOK/cutover.md` has no "Coach chat retry" line). So a master-bound card CANNOT satisfy CLAUDE.md's "every deferral gets its OWED line in the same commit" — a structural gap in the rule, not a lapse by any one card, and worth knowing for every future API card while the branches are split. Kd ruled to record them on `web-repoint` immediately rather than wait for the web half: **commit c488dda** adds the three lines (Try-again control · empty-thread-on-failure · the quota-slot-on-provider-failure ruling) AND corrects the "one header line" description in all three places it appeared — `OWED.md`, `RUNBOOK/cutover.md`, and `coachApi.js`'s own header comment — which this card proved false and which would otherwise have led whoever picked up the last cutover gate item to misprice it as a one-liner.

## Coach chat retry protection — T3 round 2 (2026-07-21; fresh chat)
Ten violations, ALL real, ALL fixed. Round 2 was materially stronger than round 1: it found a browser-invisible defect, a silent wrong-answer path, and a Done-gate miss, none of which round 1 reached.
- (R2 F1, R10.1 — the finding a SMOKE would have caught, and the reason the Done gate below is right) The new `rate_limited` 429 renders to the user as **"You've used all your coach questions for this period… or upgrade for more."** `Coach.jsx` maps EVERY 429 to the quota copy (verified: `err.response?.status === 429 ? <quota text> : <generic error>`), and `coachRateCap` needs no key, so the shipped screen reaches it at 11 messages/minute. The file's own comment insists the two facts "must not be confused" — and the client confuses them, telling a free user with four questions left to upgrade. The API is right; the CLIENT must branch on `error === 'rate_limited'`. Recorded as an OWED line on `web-repoint` with the Try-again control, since both are edits to the same catch block.
- (R2 F4, R3/Part IV #6 — invisible in a browser, and no route test could ever have seen it) `Idempotent-Replay` was sent but **unreadable by browser JS**: `@fastify/cors` emits `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` ONLY when `exposedHeaders` is set (verified in the installed `@fastify+cors@11.2.0/index.js:232-237`) and the default is null. The test asserting `res.headers["idempotent-replay"]` passes regardless because `inject()` bypasses CORS entirely — **exactly the Card 4 preflight bug's shape, one layer along**. FIX: `exposedHeaders: ["Idempotent-Replay"]` in app.ts, pinned by an assertion in app.test.ts alongside the existing preflight test, which is the only kind of test that can observe it.
- (R2 F5, R3.5 — a silent wrong answer) The record was keyed on user + key only, with **nothing binding it to the message**. The same key sent with a different question returned the FIRST question's answer, no error, no signal. `workouts/routes.ts` already rejects this class by name. FIX: the stored record is now an envelope `{m: sha256(message), body}`; a mismatch throws 400 `idempotency_key_mismatch`. Test pins that the wrong answer is never returned AND that the original message still replays.
- (R2 F6, R2.4 — two independent facts conflated, reopening harm 3) Success was `statusCode === 200 && typeof payload === "string"`, so a 200 with a non-string payload fell into the FAILURE branch: thread remembered, no record stored → the next retry resumes the thread and appends a SECOND exchange. Unreachable on today's JSON path, but the classification depended on that rather than being correct. Now: branch on status, then warn-and-skip if the payload cannot be stored.
- (R2 F2, R11.3 — a stale-record fix that went stale inside the same card) Five places still said the three deferrals were "NOT YET on OWED.md" and that all three files still read "one header line" — after commit c488dda had added them and corrected all three. **The correction for a false claim became false itself, in one card.** Fixed to cite c488dda. The lesson, fourth instance: when an action makes a record obsolete, updating the record IS part of the action.
- (R2 F3, R11.3) Two places still carried the escape-hatch rationale ("the user can always send under a fresh key", "nothing is truly stranded") that round 1's F9 identified as false for the same-key Try-again client, and that the CODE had already been changed to stop relying on. Round 1 amended two records in place and missed these two. Both now state the delete-then-409 behaviour.
- (R2 F7, R11.3) The crash residual recorded only the 120 s strand and omitted that `onSend` never runs at all in that window — so no record and no thread memory are written, and after the TTL the retry reopens harm 1, plus harm 3 if the crash landed after `appendExchange` committed. Restated in full above, with the outbox pointer.
- (R2 F8, R9.2 — and the test CORRECTED the review's premise) The joined-duplicate-header claim was asserted in a comment and tested nowhere. Writing the test showed the review's companion request — an array-valued key should 400 — asserts a case that **cannot occur**: `light-my-request` joins an explicit `["a","b"]` into the string `"a,b"` (probed), exactly as Node joins duplicate headers. So the `typeof raw !== "string"` arm is TYPE NARROWING, not a runtime path. Documented as such rather than "tested" against a fiction; the joined case IS now tested, and an array-valued header is pinned to take the same ordinary path.
- (R2 F9, R1.1) `routes.ts`'s file header still described the pre-card chain ("authn → entitlement+quota → parse → handler"). Pre-existing, made more wrong by this card. Corrected, and it now names the R3.3 deviation instead of implying compliance.
- (R2 F10, R9) The cap test hard-coded 11 and 61_000 instead of importing `COACH_CAP_MAX` / `COACH_CAP_WINDOW_S`, so changing a constant would have left it green against the old number. Now imported and derived.
- (R2 security pass — clean, second consecutive round) The reviewer independently re-verified resume tenancy (double-scoped: user-scoped Redis key AND the userId-scoped `getThread`), that a client-supplied missing threadId still 404s while a remembered one does not, that `onSend` releases on every non-200 once won, the Lua atomicity, cross-user isolation, fail-open, and that the raw key is hashed before becoming key material and never logged.
- (DONE GATE — the reviewer is RIGHT and this is now recorded as owed, not waved) CLAUDE.md Part I §2 requires a browser SMOKE for any card changing "API responses a screen consumes". This card adds THREE new client-visible responses (429 `rate_limited`, 409 `request_in_flight`, 400 `idempotency_key_mismatch`) to a screen that is already shipped and reachable, and the record documented a curl drive only — which is PROVE evidence, never a smoke (the standing distinction from the nutrition-targets card). F1 is precisely what that smoke would have caught. **The smoke cannot run against this branch** — the client sends no key, so the replay and mismatch paths are unreachable from the browser today, and only the cap is. So the honest position, per the same rule's "a card whose UI cannot be reached states that explicitly": the cap's 429 IS smokeable now and its wrong copy is the F1 OWED line; the replay and mismatch paths become smokeable only with the Try-again control, and the smoke is owed WITH that card, listed on its OWED line.

## Coach chat retry protection — T3 round 3 (2026-07-21; fresh chat)
Four violations, ALL real, ALL fixed. Round 3 probed the running code on Neon rather than reading it, and its F1 is the same fault this card has now committed three times.
- (R3 F1, R3.5 — THE FINDING, and the card's recurring fault in its purest form) Round 2's F5 fix bound the message fingerprint to the stored ANSWER. **It did not bind it to the remembered THREAD.** Two paths honour the key; only one was fixed. Consequence, reproduced by the reviewer on Neon and now by a regression test: send message ONE under key K → it FAILS, leaving a thread titled "MESSAGE ONE about squats"; send message TWO under the same K → **200, silently**, appending question two's exchange into question one's thread. The identical client bug that F5 rejects loudly after a success was accepted quietly after a failure. FIX: the thread memory is now the same envelope shape `{m, t}`, parsed and fingerprint-checked before any resume; an unreadable or legacy bare-id value is discarded and a fresh thread opened rather than resumed on faith. **MUTATION-VERIFIED** (the lesson of rounds 3-5 on the targets card, applied to my own fix this time): disabling the new check turns the regression test red with `expected 200 to be 400` — the reviewer's exact probe — and restoring it turns it green. THE PATTERN, stated plainly because it is now three-for-three on this card: round 1 fixed the deleted-thread case and not the class; round 2 fixed the record binding and not the class; round 3 fixed the class only because a reviewer probed the OTHER path. A guard is not done when the named case passes — it is done when every path carrying the same guarantee is checked.
- (R3 F2, R2.5/R8 — and it caught me applying two standards inside one edit) The envelope fix introduced `JSON.parse(payload)` INSIDE the onSend hook, above the unconditional `del(counterKey)`. Every other Redis call in that hook is total (setex→false, get→null, del→void); a throw there maps to 500 AND skips the release, so a request whose exchange had already committed would answer 500 and lock the key for the full 120 s in-flight TTL. Unreachable today because Fastify serialises this route to JSON — **which is exactly the argument the branch eleven lines above refuses to accept** ("Unreachable on today's JSON path; the classification is fixed rather than left to depend on that"). The same commit applied the opposite standard to the same class of risk. FIX: `parseUnknown` — a JSON.parse that returns null instead of throwing — so the write-back path is total end to end; an unparseable payload is warn-logged and simply not recorded, and both deletes still run.
- (R3 F3, R2.3) The new binding read `req.coachChatInput?.message ?? ""`. A silent default is precisely what would defeat it: the day this guard stopped following `validateChatBody`, every message under a key would hash to the same constant and the mismatch check would pass for ANY question — the binding defeated by the ordering assumption it depends on. The neighbouring `authedUserId` throws for the identical assumption. Now `validatedMessage(req)` throws the same way.
- (R3 F4, R8.1 — the round-1 F3 class, again, in code I wrote after learning it) The unreadable-record path threw `request_in_flight` / "That message is already being sent" **one line after deleting the record**, so nothing was in flight and the message asserted a situation that was not happening. New code `retry_not_replayable` with honest wording ("We couldn't recover the earlier reply to this message. Please send it again."), pinned by a test that corrupts a stored record through the module's own key builder and asserts both the code and the absence of the old phrasing.
- (test infrastructure) `coachIdempotencyKeys(userId, rawKey)` is now exported so the corruption test reaches a stored record through the real recipe instead of a copy of it — a duplicated key recipe in a test drifts silently the day the real one changes.
- (R3's four re-verification targets, independently confirmed by the reviewer) (a) a LEGACY pre-envelope record is handled safely — seeded live, it 409s rather than 500s, is deleted, and the same key then re-runs 200; (b) `exposedHeaders` is MUTATION-verified — removing it turns app.test.ts red at the new assertion and restoring it turns it green, with the package citations checked; (c) the 200-with-non-string-payload branch structurally cannot record a failure (the `else if` is the only writer of the thread key and the non-string case sits inside the `if`); (d) no stale round-2 comment or DECISIONS line survives — c488dda exists as claimed, the cap test imports its constants, and the routes.ts header names the deviation.
- (RESIDUAL surfaced by (b), reported not fixed — R1.1) `etag` (exercises routes) and `@fastify/rate-limit`'s `x-ratelimit-*` / `retry-after` are also unexposed and non-safelisted. Nothing is broken today — no client reads them from JS, and the browser's own cache handles `If-None-Match` without help — but a future client written to do conditional requests or to show a retry countdown will hit the same wall this card just hit. Recorded here rather than widened silently: adding headers to `exposedHeaders` is a policy choice about what the API publishes, not a drive-by.
- (Done gate, unchanged) The SMOKE position from round 2 stands and F1 does not alter it — the failed-attempt-then-different-message path also needs the Try-again control before a browser can reach it. Both remain on the web card's OWED line.
- (PROVE, post-round-3) api **309/309 on Neon** (293 baseline + 16 this card); typecheck + lint clean; red-flag greps clean. NB the first attempt at this run reported 3 failures — TWO of them in `nutrition`, a module this card never touches. Not waved away: classified before re-running, and the evidence said network, not code — **zero** assertion failures, all three durations pinned exactly at their timeout ceilings (30017/30009/60018 ms), and the Neon host resolving fine on a direct lookup afterwards. The recorded signature (HANDOFF: "an ENOTFOUND flood with ZERO assertion failures is the network, not the code"). The clean re-run is the evidence, not the assumption.

## Coach chat retry protection — T3 round 4 (2026-07-21; fresh chat)
Four findings; F1 blocked merge and is fixed, F2/F4 folded into the same commit, F3 was a no-harm note. The fault was the SAME one for the FOURTH round running — and this time the fix closes the CLASS, not the case.
- (R4 F1, R3.5 — the fourth instance, BLOCKING, probed on Neon) The fingerprint bound the MESSAGE, but a coach request's identity is `(message, threadId)`. Consequence, reproduced by the reviewer and now by a regression test: send "ok thanks" in thread T1 under key K → 200, recorded; send "ok thanks" in thread T2 under the SAME key → 200, `Idempotent-Replay: true`, body threadId = T1, and **the client's write into T2 silently never happened**. The round-2 harm shape (silent wrong answer under a reused key) reached through the one request field nobody had bound. "ok thanks" / "why?" / "more" is exactly the repeated short text a chat UI produces across threads.
- (THE FIX CLOSES THE CLASS, deliberately NOT the reviewer's one-liner) The review offered `${threadId}\0${message}`, which fixes THIS case. Rounds 2, 3 and 4 each bound the field the previous round named (record → thread → threadId) and a fresh reviewer found the next gap each time — so binding one more field invites a fifth round. Instead the fingerprint is now `sha256(canonical(whole validated body))`: every field the client sent, order-independent (keys sorted at each level), so **the next field added to `coachChatRequestSchema` is covered on the day it is added**, with nobody needing to remember this guard exists. `messageHash` → `requestHash` throughout; the message-only helper is gone. This is the first fix on this card written against the class rather than the case that motivated it.
- (VERIFICATION matched the fix's ambition — TWO layers, both mutation-checked) (a) an integration test in the reviewer's exact probe shape: same text, different thread, same key → 400 `idempotency_key_mismatch`, T2 untouched (still just its own opening exchange), and T1's own request still replays. MUTATION-VERIFIED: reverting `requestFingerprint` to hash `{message}` only turns it red with `expected 200 to be 400`. (b) a pure unit test (`coach.idempotency.unit.test.ts`, no DB) pinning the CLASS-level property of `canonical`: order-insensitive, distinguishes every field, treats absent-vs-explicit-undefined as one request, and does not confuse nesting/types. Honest note recorded: the message-only mutant was caught by the INTEGRATION test, not the unit test (the unit test pins `canonical`'s own correctness; the integration test pins that `requestFingerprint` feeds it the whole body) — two layers, each pinning its own concern, is why both exist.
- (R4 F2, R8.1 — the in-flight 409 asserted a specific message that was not the one running) A different request arriving under a key mid-flight got "That message is already being sent", which was false — a DIFFERENT message was. Round 3's F4 rule ("an error must not assert a situation that is not happening") applied to the case round 3 named and not to the class. The claim carries no payload to compare, so rather than invent one the wording is now request-neutral: "A send under this Idempotency-Key is already in progress." The precise mismatch is reported by the record check once the first attempt lands. No data harm either way (it self-corrects), so 409 is kept over 400.
- (R4 F4 — the comment said "Two paths honour the key". THREE do) The stale count is the case-level framing that produced F1 in the first place. Corrected to name all three (record, thread memory, in-flight claim) and to say which compare the fingerprint and why the claim cannot.
- (R4 F3, R9.2 — NO-HARM, recorded not fixed) The record/thread mismatch check sits inside the `threadId === undefined` branch, so a key reused for a different request WHILE supplying a threadId skips the mismatch check on the resume path. Reachable only when nothing is resumed (the resume path requires `threadId === undefined`), so no wrong answer is served and no write is misdirected — it simply falls through to a normal metered request. The reviewer classed it non-blocking; recorded as the same asymmetry family, closed in spirit by the whole-body fingerprint (every honouring path now compares `requestHash`).
- (Done gate — the reviewer's merge-ordering caveat, already covered) Merging the API half alone leaves `Coach.jsx` mapping the cap's `rate_limited` 429 to the upgrade copy — reachable today because the cap needs no key. This is the FOLD IN already on `web-repoint`'s OWED Try-again line (commit df3fe5b). It is a merge-ordering condition (do not deploy the API 429 to users before the client copy fix), NOT a defect in this diff, and it is recorded where the web work will see it.
- (security pass — clean, FOURTH consecutive round) Reviewer re-verified by probe: tenancy holds (F1 was self-scoped confusion, never cross-tenant — all keys are `${userId}:${sha256}`), records are Zod-re-parsed on read, logs carry event+userId only, the onSend hook is total, and `validatedMessage`/`authedUserId`/`requestFingerprint` throw rather than 500 a legitimate request (probed with empty/missing/non-JSON bodies → 400 every time).
- (PROVE, post-round-4) api **314/314 on Neon**, first run, no flakes (293 baseline + 21 this card, incl. the new `coach.idempotency.unit.test.ts`); typecheck + lint clean; the F1 fix mutation-verified red→green.

## Coach chat retry protection — T3 round 5 (2026-07-21; fresh chat) — READY TO MERGE
- (VERDICT: MERGE) Round 5 attacked the round-4 whole-body fingerprint in both directions and could not break it: no two distinct requests collide (key order sorted; a crafted message cannot forge a `"threadId":"…"` structure because the value goes through JSON.stringify; absent-vs-undefined filtered to one form) and no honest retry spuriously 400s (the parsed `.strict()` `.trim()`ed body is deterministic for the same raw input). The reviewer mutation-tested `canonical` independently (removed `.sort()` and the undefined filter → the exact unit assertions went red) and re-verified rounds 1-3's fixes hold under the refactor. Security pass clean for the FIFTH consecutive round; tenancy, total onSend hook, and `retry_not_replayable` all re-confirmed on Neon. No blocking violation in behaviour, security, or the Done gate.
- (R5 note 1 — applied, R11.3) The reviewer flagged the unstated flip side of "the next field is covered automatically": a future NON-identity field (client nonce, UI locale, regenerated timestamp) would OVER-BIND and 400 an honest retry. Added to the `requestFingerprint` doc — over-binding fails SAFE (a 400 mismatch, never a wrong replay), and `.strict()` makes any such field a deliberate addition, so the guidance is "omit it from `input` here if ever added". One-line-class comment, no code change owed.
- (R5 note 2 — applied, style) `value.map(canonical)` → `value.map((el) => canonical(el))`: the bare reference passed `(el, index, array)` to a single-arg function (harmless, since the extra args were ignored, but implicit). Explicit now. Both notes are NON-BEHAVIOURAL; re-verified typecheck + lint clean and coach chat + unit + app suites green (39/39) on the edited bytes.
- (DEPLOY-ORDERING CAVEAT restated by the reviewer, already on file) The cap's `rate_limited` 429 is reachable without a key, and today's `Coach.jsx` maps every 429 to the upgrade copy — so the API's 429 must NOT reach the current web client before the copy fix ships. It is the FOLD IN on `web-repoint`'s OWED Try-again line (df3fe5b), a merge-ordering condition, not a defect in this diff. Merging the API half to master ships nothing user-facing on its own (master's web is the pre-repoint app; the repoint lands at P2.8).
- (READY TO MERGE, V7) CI green is the closing gate; every finding across five rounds is resolved. api 314/314 on Neon on the pre-note bytes; typecheck + lint clean and the touched surface (39/39) green after the two non-behavioural notes.

## Groq COACH_MODEL migration — llama-3.1-8b-instant → openai/gpt-oss-20b (2026-07-22)
- (PROCESS) Same-chat continuation after the coach-idempotency card, by Kd instruction (the standing Part I §1 deviation). Re-grounded on the specific files before planning.
- (THE DEADLINE) Groq deprecated `llama-3.1-8b-instant` and DECOMMISSIONS it 2026-08-16 — after that date requests are not served and the coach goes dark. Confirmed by the Groq email Kd forwarded (free/developer tier affected; enterprise committed-spend exempt) and matching DECISIONS 2026-07-16. Date-gated, not launch-gated.
- (THE MODEL + PRICES — Kd read them from the live Groq site, Part 0 rule 4) Replacement `openai/gpt-oss-20b` (Groq's own recommendation; exact id from the GroqCloud supported-models page). Prices re-quoted from groq.com/pricing 2026-07-22: **$0.075/1M input, $0.30/1M output** → integer micro-USD constants **75_000 / 300_000** (×1e6), superseding the P2.5b GAP-5 llama constants (50_000 / 80_000). The discounted $0.0375/1M CACHED-input rate is deliberately NOT used — the ledger prices every real call at the full input rate (R6.1: never under-count) and our 24h answer cache already skips the provider on a hit. NOT guessed — Kd supplied all three from the authoritative live source.
- (FILES) config.ts COACH_MODEL default flipped (+ comment citing the migration); service.ts price constants re-quoted (+ comment: source, the cached-rate note, and the OpenRouter-fallback-shares-these-constants approximation carried from DECISIONS 2026-07-12); test cost assertions recomputed 21→68 (coach.chat.test.ts ×2, coach.llm.unit.test.ts ×1) — new math (100·75000+200·300000+500000)/1e6 = 68 (67.5 round-half-up).
- (CORRECTION to my own plan claim — a test caught it, the recurring class-not-case fault) I asserted "flipping COACH_MODEL breaks no test", verified by grepping for tests that READ the config. WRONG: the service WRITES `cached:${deps.model}` (= config.COACH_MODEL) onto a cache-hit row, and a test asserts that string. The cache test went red (`cached:openai/gpt-oss-20b` vs the old expected). Fix made the fixtures COHERENT: all coach test fakes now return `openai/gpt-oss-20b` (sed over both coach test files), so no decommissioned model id lingers and the cached/non-cached model strings agree. Lesson logged: before claiming a value change is inert, grep every place it is WRITTEN/stamped, not just read.
- (NO migration, NO new dependency, NOTHING in packages/shared) The model is free-text in `coach_messages.model` (no CHECK); the model is part of the answer-cache key, so the swap auto-invalidates old-model cache entries with no manual bust; PROMPT_VERSION unchanged (the prompt did not change).
- (LIVE DRIVE against real Groq, before any handover — this is where the real findings came from) Registered → sent real coach questions to gpt-oss-20b on Kd's key. Results: (1) **NO reasoning-dump** — GPT OSS 20B is a reasoning model and the risk flagged at plan time (the Qwen-vision precedent) did NOT materialize; answers arrive clean in `content`, no `<think>` leakage, so NO adapter change was needed. (2) stored model = `openai/gpt-oss-20b#p2`, ledger row prices correctly (406 micro-USD, provider groq) — pricing works end-to-end. (3) real cost ≈ $0.0004/question (2.1× the old model, still a fraction of a cent; a free user ≤$0.0015/month).
- (COACH_MAX_TOKENS 800→1200 — Kd-ruled, folded into this card) The live drive found the pre-existing 800-output-token cap (llm.adapter.ts, ported from coach.py:96-97) now TRUNCATES answers mid-sentence: the old llama averaged ~500 output tokens and rarely hit it, but gpt-oss-20b is markedly more verbose (tables, worked examples) and hit exactly 800 on every substantive question. Presented as a decision (raise / leave+owe / prompt-for-concise); Kd chose RAISE. Folded into THIS card rather than done as a drive-by because the model swap is what made the cap bite (R1.1 respected — reported, ruled, then changed). Verified live at 1200: the same question that truncated at 800 now uses 967 output tokens and COMPLETES NATURALLY (finish is the model's own stop, not the cap). Cost impact ~$0.0001/question, negligible. No test pins the cap.

## Groq COACH_MODEL migration — T3 resolution (2026-07-22; fresh-chat review run by Kd) — READY TO MERGE
- (VERDICT: MERGE after one comment fix) The reviewer verified the prices against the LIVE Groq source (per-token $0.0000000750 in / $0.0000003000 out = $0.075/$0.30 per 1M; the "$0.07" in aggregator summaries is 2-dp rounding of $0.075, corroborated by the $0.0375 cached rate being exactly half). Confirmed: integer micro-USD, no float touches money, the recomputed 68 is right, the cached discount is genuinely unused (a cache hit is costMicro 0n with NO ledger row — a real free hit, not an under-count), the model-id swap leaves no active llama reference, the cache-write string is coherent (the "inert" trap genuinely closed), and COACH_MAX_TOKENS 800→1200 pins no test and is R1.1-documented. Security pass clean (no new surface; model id is server-controlled free text, provider response still Zod-parsed).
