- (THE ONE FINDING — R11.4 stale comment, the exact class this card polices) llm.adapter.ts's `createOpenRouterProvider` docstring still read "OpenRouter serves the same Llama family under its own model id" — false after the swap, since the primary is now gpt-oss-20b and "the same" lost its referent. FIXED in the same commit: the comment now states the fallback serves a DIFFERENT family (meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b-instruct) from the gpt-oss-20b primary, priced at the primary's constants as the conservative approximation (DECISIONS 2026-07-12). Grep confirmed no other stale llama comment remains (the rest are intended migration/superseded references). Comment-only change; the 314/314 Neon run stands on functionally identical bytes; typecheck + lint clean.
- (PROCESS SLIP, self-reported) This card's changes were initially made on `web-repoint` rather than a fresh branch off master. Caught before any commit, moved cleanly to `groq-coach-model` off origin/master (code files via stash-checkout; the DECISIONS entry appended to MASTER's copy, not web-repoint's, to avoid the cross-branch record divergence this project has hit before); web-repoint restored to its committed state. Full suite re-run 314/314 on the new branch to prove the move was clean. Recorded rather than quietly corrected.
- (READY TO MERGE, V7) The single finding is fixed and the branch is green: api 314/314 on Neon, typecheck + lint clean, live-driven end-to-end against real Groq (clean answers, correct pricing, natural completion under the raised cap).

## Coach markdown tables — the model-swap smoke's second finding (2026-07-22, web-repoint)
- (THE FINDING — a browser smoke caught what curl could not, for the SECOND time on this card) After the gpt-oss-20b swap merged, Kd's browser smoke showed coach answers as a run-on paragraph of `|` pipes and `---` dividers. Root cause, predicted before the smoke and confirmed by it: `Coach.jsx` renders replies with `react-markdown` ^10, which does NOT support GitHub-flavoured tables without `remark-gfm` — and no plugin was installed (verified by grep: zero remark/rehype deps) and no table CSS existed. The OLD llama model answered mostly in prose so tables were rare; gpt-oss-20b answers macro questions with tables as a matter of course, so the swap turned a latent gap into every substantive answer. Same shape as the COACH_MAX_TOKENS finding: the model change made a pre-existing weakness start to bite. THE API WAS NEVER WRONG — the model returned correct, well-formed markdown; only the drawing was broken.
- (R1.4 NEW DEPENDENCY, Kd-approved after being presented with the alternative) `remark-gfm` ^4 added to apps/web. It is the official companion plugin to the `react-markdown` already in use (same unified/remark family, the standard way to get GFM tables). Kd was given both options — (A) render tables properly, (B) instruct the model to avoid tables — and chose A. Recorded rationale: B relies on the MODEL OBEYING an instruction, which it will not always do, so the ugly rendering would return at random; A is robust regardless of what the model emits, and tables are genuinely the right format for meal/protein/carbs/fat data.
- (IMPLEMENTATION, and a deliberate simplification) `remarkPlugins={[remarkGfm]}` on the ReactMarkdown call, plus table CSS in the existing `.coach-markdown` block (orange headers matching the theme, hairline borders, even-row shading). Wide tables scroll SIDEWAYS INSIDE the bubble via `display:block; overflow-x:auto` ON THE TABLE ITSELF rather than a JSX wrapper component — the first attempt used a `components={{ table: ... }}` override, which tripped the web lint's no-unused-vars on the discarded `node` prop; the CSS-only route removes the override entirely (less code, nothing to keep in sync) and keeps the chat from ever widening the page on a phone.
- (PROVE) web 130/131 (the 1 = the known syncClient VITE_API_URL env quirk, CI-green, DECISIONS 2026-07-15) · `vite build` ✓ · lint **1 error on Coach.jsx = its exact pre-existing baseline** (the untouched hoisting complaint; the transient 2nd error from the wrapper attempt is gone). **SMOKE PASSED** (Kd, 2026-07-22): the same question that rendered as pipe soup now shows proper boxed tables.
- (T3 SKIPPED — Kd-ruled, recorded so the skip is not silent) The standing rule (DECISIONS 2026-07-11 P2.2) is that EVERY card gets a fresh-chat T3. Kd ruled this one exempt: it is display-only (one import, one plugin prop, CSS), adds no logic, and touches nothing about money, auth, tenancy or data — and the browser smoke he ran IS the proof, since the defect and the fix are both purely visual. Recorded as an explicit exemption rather than an omission; the rule stands for everything else.

## web repoint — Coach "Try again" control + honest 429 copy (2026-07-22)
- (SCOPE) The CLIENT half of the coach retry protection whose API half merged as PR #43 (`facc804`, verified an ancestor of this branch). `Coach.jsx` cleared the message box on send and never restored it, and there was NO retry affordance — so the user's only route back from a failure was retyping, which the server correctly sees as a NEW question (new thread, another question spent), and a key minted inside `sendMessage` would have differed on every attempt and deduped nothing. Web-only, no API change, no migration, no new dependency; commits to `web-repoint` per the 2026-07-15 branch-strategy ruling.
- (THE MECHANISM) The key is minted ONCE PER COMPOSED MESSAGE (`newIdempotencyKey`, `crypto.randomUUID` — the ActiveWorkout.jsx secure-context precedent) and stored ON the failed message with the text and the compose-time `threadId`; "Try again" replays exactly that triple. `threadId` is captured at compose time and NEVER re-read from `activeId` at click time, so the body is identical by construction — the server fingerprints the WHOLE validated body (`requestFingerprint` → `canonical(coachChatInput)`), and any difference is correctly a 400 mismatch rather than a recognised repeat. The input box is deliberately NOT restored: the button is the retry path.
- (BRANCH ON THE NAME, NOT THE STATUS — the fold-in delivered) `coachErrorInfo` is a PURE exported mapper over `err.response?.data?.error`. Two different 429s mean opposite things (monthly quota vs the 10/minute burst cap) and two different 409s give opposite advice (`request_in_flight` = wait, `retry_not_replayable` = send it again), so the old status-only branch told a free user with four questions left to UPGRADE. `quota_exceeded`, `not_found` and `validation_error` offer NO button — retrying cannot help and an offer would be a lie; the two key-errors retry with a FRESH key, because a mismatch proves no stored answer is bound to this body (so a new key is a genuine first attempt and cannot duplicate) while the same key would 400 for the full 10-minute window.
- (STATUS AS A FALLBACK — a deliberate exception to "never branch on status", verified not assumed) An UNRECOGNISED 429 maps to the "too quickly" copy. `@fastify/rate-limit@11.1.0` builds a bare `new Error()` with `statusCode` and no `code` (index.js:31-33), so app.ts's typed-error allowlist (`err.code.startsWith("FST_")`) does not claim it and the global limiter's 429 reaches the client as `{error:"request_error"}`. The name is then the wrong instrument and the status is the only signal left; what matters is that this path can never produce the upgrade copy. The T3 was asked to judge whether this was a relapse and confirmed it sound.
- (T3 — FRESH CHAT, run by Kd; ONE BLOCKING finding, and the extracted-mapper tests were structurally unable to see it) **V1:** the button was addressed PER MESSAGE while `performSend` writes to the TAIL — two addressing schemes for one action. Reachable: send A → fails (bubble keeps a live button, and `streaming` goes false so the composer re-enables) → send B → succeeds → click A's "Try again" ⇒ **B's reply is overwritten with A's answer** while A's failed bubble stays armed. The right key delivered into the wrong bubble, corrupting the visible transcript. **Closed at BOTH layers rather than the one path the finding named** (the "mutate the class, not the case" lesson this file has now recorded five times): `appendOutgoing` withdraws every earlier retry offer when a new message is composed, so state stays truthful; and the button cannot RENDER off-tail (`isTail`), so the offer and the write target agree by construction whatever a future edit does to the list. MUTATION-VERIFIED: deleting the strip turns the new test red with exactly the V1 symptom, restoring it turns it green.
- (T3 V3, fixed) `replaceTailAssistant` was pure, was exactly where V1 lived, and was neither exported nor tested — the extraction standard had been applied to the mapper and not to the sequencer. Both helpers now live beside `coachErrorInfo` in `coachApi.js` and are unit-tested (tail-is-assistant → replaced · tail-is-user → untouched, so a send can never rewrite a user's words · empty list safe · `appendOutgoing` does not mutate its input). Placement follows the established repo pattern: `userApi.js` carries `syncTimezone` for exactly this reason (DECISIONS 2026-07-21, that card's T3 F2 — "the only thing preventing a test was that it lived in the JSX file").
- (T3 V4, observed not fixed, on the reviewer's explicit advice) `if (streaming) return` reads closure state, so two clicks inside one frame both dispatch. Server-safe — the second loses the atomic `incrWithTtl` claim and gets a 409 `request_in_flight`, which itself offers a same-key retry that then replays — but the resulting bubble is resolution-order dependent. Stated in the smoke rather than fixed blind.
- (T3 V5 → an OWED line, reported not fixed per R1.1) The composer sets no `maxLength` while the contract caps a message at 2000 chars, so an over-long message is rejected AFTER the box is cleared and correctly offers no retry — leaving the text recoverable only from the user bubble. Pre-existing; needs a length affordance, not a change to the error mapping.
- (R3.10, in-scope improvement to a line being rewritten) `console.error('Coach chat error:', err)` logged the whole axios error, whose `config.data` carries the user's own question. Now `err?.message` only — the Card-6 console-leak precedent.
- (RECORDS CORRECTED IN THE SAME COMMIT — T3 V2, and the branch-topology problem again) `OWED.md` and `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` still said the API half was "on branch `coach-idempotency`, NOT yet merged"; it merged as PR #43. Both ticked. The **Groq COACH_MODEL** line was also still open though PR #44 merged on 2026-07-22 — neither file exists in a form a master-bound card can edit (OWED.md is not on master at all), which is the structural gap recorded on 2026-07-21. Ticked here, and the **GROQ_API_KEY rotation** that card was expected to carry but did not perform is now its OWN open line rather than a sentence buried inside a ticked entry — a `[ ]` grep must be able to find it. cutover.md's `limitedToDays` checkbox was likewise stale (done 2026-07-21) and is ticked.
- (T3 DIFF SCOPE — the handover instruction was wrong and was corrected) The card brief said to produce the review diff with `git diff origin/master -- apps/web`. On this branch that is **4,537 insertions across 36 files** — every previous web-repoint card — which would have buried this card's 358 lines. The T3 diff was taken against the branch HEAD instead (3 files, 520 diff lines), matching the scope of every previous card's `t3-*.diff`.
- (PROVE) Tests RED before code, twice (13 failed / 4 passed for the mapper + key work; 3 failed for the V1 sequencing tests). Final: web **147/148** — the 1 is the known `syncClient` VITE_API_URL env quirk (`window is not defined` in node, CI-green, DECISIONS 2026-07-15) — up from the 130/131 baseline, i.e. **+17 tests and nothing else disturbed**; `vite build` ✓; lint **1 error on Coach.jsx = its exact pre-existing baseline** (the untouched hoisting complaint), `coachApi.js` + its test clean.
- (LIVE DRIVE, before handover — the mechanism verified rather than inherited from the API card's record) Fresh account → send with key K → 200; **replay of K with the same body returned a BYTE-IDENTICAL body with `idempotent-replay: true`**; K with a DIFFERENT message returned **400 `idempotency_key_mismatch`**; the account held **ONE** thread after all three requests. The CORS preflight was re-checked first, because a custom request header forces one and a refusal would kill the coach in the browser entirely (the Card-4 bug class): `access-control-allow-headers: content-type,idempotency-key` and `access-control-expose-headers: Idempotent-Replay`.
- (SMOKE — OWED, and three precision points recorded BEFORE it runs so a correct behaviour is not reported as a bug) (a) On a FREE account, rapid sends 6-10 legitimately show the QUOTA copy — the free tier is 5 questions/MONTH — and only the **11th** trips the 10/minute cap and says "too quickly"; the cap sits before the meter, so it fires even for a user already out of quota (the API card's own test pins this). (b) Groq's free tier allows ~6,000 tokens/minute against ~2,573 per call, i.e. about **two questions a minute**, so rapid sends will also produce 503s — these now render "The coach is briefly unavailable" WITH a Try-again button, which is the honest outcome, not a fault. (c) "ONE question spent" holds for the devtools-block method because the blocked request never reaches the server; a failure AFTER the meter spends a second question on retry, which is the still-unruled OWED item, not a defect in this card.
- (SMOKE-FOLDED UI FIX — Kd ask, 2026-07-22, recorded per the established pattern; web-only, no API change, no logic) Kd's smoke showed coach answers being CUT OFF: a markdown table ran past the bubble edge and had to be scrolled sideways to read, on a 1920px screen. Two constraints were stacked — the conversation column was `max-w-3xl` (768px) regardless of window width, and every bubble was 75% OF THAT, so an answer table got ~576px. FIXED: the column (and the input bar and empty state, so they stay aligned) is now `max-w-5xl` (1024px), and the ANSWER bubble is `max-w-[92%]` while the QUESTION bubble stays at 75% — a question is a line or two, an answer from gpt-oss-20b is routinely a table. Usable table width ~576px → ~942px. Kd's framing honoured exactly: the table's own `overflow-x:auto` (DECISIONS 2026-07-22, the remark-gfm card) is KEPT, so a genuinely wide table still scrolls inside the bubble — the fix stops the COMMON case needing it rather than removing the scroll. Same class as the two previous model-swap findings: the model returned correct markdown and only the drawing was wrong.
- (RELATED, REPORTED NOT FIXED — R1.1, seen in the same screenshot) The model emitted a literal `<br>` inside a table cell and it renders as visible text ("→ 90-120 g <br> Carbs:"). That is RAW HTML, not markdown: `react-markdown` ignores raw HTML by design and renders it as text, and enabling it needs `rehype-raw` — a NEW DEPENDENCY (R1.4) and a decision about allowing model-authored HTML into the DOM, which is Kd's call and not a smoke drive-by. Not part of the Try-again card. On OWED.md as its own line.
- (SMOKE — PASSED, Kd 2026-07-22; the card is DONE and the API half's owed smoke is closed with it) All three steps passed in the browser: (1) the blocked-request failure showed the error WITH a "Try again" button, unblocking and clicking it produced a real answer, and the sidebar held ONE conversation — not the two that retyping would have created; (2) the T3 V1 case — fail a message, then send a DIFFERENT one that succeeds — left the stale button gone and the newer answer intact, i.e. the blocking defect is confirmed absent on the exact path that would have shipped it; (3) ~11 rapid sends ended in the burst-cap copy ("sending too quickly") rather than the upgrade copy, which is the fold-in this card owed. Recorded honestly: step 3 was reported by Kd as passing without a screenshot, and steps 6-10 of that sequence legitimately show the quota copy (5 free questions/month), which the pre-recorded precision notes above existed to prevent being misread as a failure.
- (SMOKE METHOD, one more for the file) Kd could not find the `chat` row in devtools because the Network panel's FILTER BOX still held `users/me` from an earlier session — the request list read "0 / 4 requests" and looked empty. Clearing the filter (or typing `chat` into it) is the fix. Worth recording beside the two method corrections the nutrition-targets card logged: three of the last four smokes lost time to the instrument, not the code.

## DPDP Day-14 hard-delete worker (2026-07-22; branch `dpdp-day14-purge`)
- (SCOPE, Kd-ruled) §5.2 has TWO halves and this card builds ONE: the Day-14 hard delete. The JSON export is split to its own next card because none of its infrastructure exists — command-verified: no R2/S3 client in any package.json, no bucket or signing keys among config.ts's 17 env vars, no zip library, and no Part 4 table to track an export job. Bundling it would have meant new credentials plus at least 2 new dependencies in a card that already carries the irreversible half, against Part I §7b's one-thing-per-chat ceiling. `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` now carries the gate as two checkboxes, so nothing is lost — the export still blocks P2.8.
- (THE MECHANISM, and why the list is the only one) §5.2 ANONYMIZES the users row rather than deleting it ("row kept so FKs from audit/invoices resolve"), so `ON DELETE CASCADE` from users NEVER fires on account deletion — the correction recorded at DECISIONS 2026-07-15, whose plan had assumed the cascade collected everything for free. `modules/privacy/tables.ts` is therefore the compliance artifact: it is meant to be read top-to-bottom against the spec, and it carries the §5.2 sentence verbatim above the lists.
- (A PLAN DEFECT THE VERIFICATION CAUGHT BEFORE ANY CODE) The approved plan described 17 tables deleted by `WHERE user_id = $1`. **Two of them have no user_id at all:** `meal_log_corrections` (cascades from meal_logs) and `coach_messages` (cascades from coach_threads); `workout_sets` likewise cascades from workouts, which is what §5.2's "(+sets CASCADE)" means. So the real shape is 15 direct DELETEs and 18 tables ASSERTED empty — two lists, not one. (Counts re-CORRECTED at T3 round 3: round 2 said 16/18 after `push_tokens` was added; round 3 moved `workout_sets` OUT of the direct list to cascade-collected (F6), so it is now 15 direct + 3 cascade = 18. Measured from code each time now: 15 `await tx\`DELETE FROM\`` statements, `DIRECT_DELETE_TABLES` 15, `CASCADE_COLLECTED_TABLES` 3, `PII_TABLES` 18. Three different count-claims across three rounds is exactly why they are computed, not recalled.) The assertion list is the one that proves the cascades actually fired.
- (DELETE ORDER IS UNCONSTRAINED — verified, not assumed) All 38 FK constraints in the migrations were parsed: nothing outside the list references anything inside it, and the only intra-list links are the three ON DELETE CASCADE parent-to-child ones. No statement can be blocked by a referrer, in any order. The order in repo.ts simply mirrors the spec sentence so the two read alike.
- (LITERAL SQL, NOT A LOOP) The deletes are 15 explicit statements (round 3 dropped the 16th, the cross-tenant workout_sets delete — F6). A table name is an identifier and cannot be parameterised, and a grep proved this repo has NO dynamic-identifier precedent anywhere in `apps/api/src`. Drift between the lists and the statements is caught by the test asserting every listed table ends empty.
- (ONE NAMED CONSTANT — and the plan UNDERCOUNTED it) `src/retention.ts` holds `DPDP_RETENTION_DAYS = 14`, per the build note recorded with Kd's open privacy-scope question (DECISIONS 2026-07-21: building now boxes nothing in PROVIDED the window is one constant). The plan claimed ONE live hard-coded 14; there were **FOUR, in three files across two modules** — `auth/service.ts`'s `RESTORE_TTL_MS`, `users/repo.ts`'s `interval '14 days'` in `restoreUser`, and the TWO user-facing strings in `users/routes.ts`'s `DELETE /v1/users/me` handler. (Line numbers deliberately omitted: this card's own edits moved all three, and the first version of this bullet cited the PRE-EDIT positions — T3 F2. The CITATION DISCIPLINE rule of 2026-07-20 says quote text, not positions; this is the first entry to actually obey it.) The copy is the one that matters: had only the code read the constant, widening the window to GDPR's 30 would leave the API telling users "you have 14 days" while purging at 30 — the app lying about its own deletion behaviour, produced by the change meant to keep it lawful. All four now derive from the constant. Verified by grep that no existing test asserted any of them, so the swap is genuinely inert (the Groq card's lesson: grep every place a value is WRITTEN, not just read).
- (PURGE MARKER = an audit_log row, Kd-ruled) `action='user.purged'` rather than a new `users.purged_at` column: NO migration, and §5.2 keeps audit_log precisely so a tombstoned user stays narratable (Part 8). Verified audit_log has NO foreign keys and no CHECK on `action`, so the row is free to write. The `NOT EXISTS` is bounded by `at >= u.deleted_at` — not decoration: audit_log has no index on action/target_id (only `(gym_id, at)` btree plus a BRIN on `at`), so an unbounded probe would seq-scan the fastest-growing table in the schema, and a purge can never predate its own deletion. **Proven on real data:** the dev branch holds 9 due accounts, 8 carry markers, and the dry run correctly scanned only the 1 unmarked one.
- (THE REAL BUG THIS CARD SHIPPED AND CAUGHT — a SILENT wrong answer) The leaderboard scrub first used `JSON.stringify(value)` cast to jsonb. postgres-js sends a plain JS string as a TEXT parameter, so the cast yields a jsonb STRING SCALAR, not an array — and `array @> string` is simply FALSE, raising nothing. The UPDATE matched zero rows, the transaction committed happily, and a purged user's display name would have stayed on every leaderboard forever. Probed both forms directly: the JSON.stringify form returned false, the `sql.json(...)` form returned true. Both interpolations in the function were wrong the same way. Caught ONLY because the test asserts the scrubbed VALUE; an assertion that the statement "ran" would have passed. Red-to-green on the identical test is the mutation proof.
- (A TEST TIMEOUT WAS HIDING IT) That scrub test was the only one without an explicit 60s timeout, so over a remote Neon connection it died at the 5s default and read as an infrastructure flake. The assertion failure only appeared once the timeout was raised. A timeout is not a result — a suite where one test times out is a suite with one untested claim.
- (SCRUB VALUE — recorded inference) §5.2 says "scrub display_name" without saying to what. It is REPLACED with the same `Deleted user` tombstone the users row gets, rather than the key being dropped, so the two representations of a purged person agree and readers keep a well-formed shape. `WITH ORDINALITY` preserves entry order; `jsonb_typeof = 'array'` guards a malformed value that would otherwise raise inside an otherwise-good purge.
- (WORKER = BullMQ, and it CANNOT reuse the Redis seam) New dependency `bullmq@5.80.10` approved (R1.4): 7,384,354 downloads for 2026-07-15 to 07-21, verified from npm rather than recalled — the plan's first figure was "~2M" from memory and wrong by 3.7x. It is the stack the spec fixed (R1.4's own list; v1 §6 "two modes, same image"; Part 4 §5.1 "jobs live in the BullMQ rollups queue"; Part 8 alerts on queue age). **DELIBERATE EXCEPTION, declared:** `src/redis.ts` says modules must never import ioredis directly, and `worker.ts` does. `createIoRedis` sets `maxRetriesPerRequest: 1` and `enableOfflineQueue: false`, while BullMQ REQUIRES `maxRetriesPerRequest: null` for its blocking commands, and a blocking worker monopolises its connection. worker.ts is an ENTRYPOINT (same tier as index.ts), not a module, so the rule's subject does not cover it.
- (THE JOB BODY IS A PLAIN FUNCTION, deliberately) BullMQ only CALLS `purgeDueUsers`; the scheduler holds no logic. That is why 10 tests exercise the real behaviour against real Postgres with no queue, no Redis and no worker running — and why the choice of scheduler was never the risky part of this card.
- (INJECTED CLOCK) `purgeDueUsers({now})` — R6.4's billing doctrine applied to the other place where a date decides whether data lives. The window tests move time; they do not wait 14 days.
- (FAILURE ISOLATION) One transaction per user (§5.2 verbatim), each wrapped so a failing user is logged and counted while the batch continues (the migrate-mongo T3-2 precedent). A failed user is simply not marked, so the next run retries them.
- (DRY RUN IS THE DEFAULT) `tools/dpdp-purge.ts` reports and writes nothing unless `--apply` is passed, modelled on tools/migrate-mongo/run.ts — the only irreversible action in the product should not be one keystroke away. It parses ONLY `DATABASE_URL` (R2.3, the migrate-mongo precedent) rather than `loadConfig()`, which would demand WEB_ORIGIN and JWT_SECRET: an operator running a deletion sweep should not need the app's HTTP secrets.
- (SIDE EFFECT OF TESTING A GLOBAL SWEEP, reported not hidden) `purgeDueUsers` selects EVERY due account — the product behaviour, not a test shortcut — so running this suite purges any due soft-deleted row in the target DB, not only its own fixtures. The first green run purged 8 pre-existing soft-deleted rows on the dev Neon branch, all example.com fixtures left by earlier suites. Harmless there and irrelevant on prod (which starts EMPTY, and where the worker does this on a schedule anyway), but a suite that destroys data outside its own fixtures must say so: a loud warning now heads the test file.
- (CACHES — considered and dismissed WITH evidence, so a reviewer need not re-derive it) No Redis purge step is needed. Every user-linked Redis holding is TTL-bounded at 24h or less and none links user-authored text to an identity: `coach:idem:*` is 10min/120s and stores an envelope `{m: requestHash, body: <the reply>}` keyed by the raw userId — i.e. the REPLY TEXT is in Redis, and what is hashed is the request, not the record (CORRECTED at T3 F3; the original claim here said it stored `sha256(message)` and NOT the message, which was false — the conclusion survives on the 10-minute TTL, but it was resting on a premise I had not checked); the answer cache is 24h and deliberately not keyed to identity (DECISIONS 2026-07-12 T3 finding 2 removed displayName for exactly this reason); entitlements 60s and quota counters are plan data and integers. PostHog's `capture()` seam has ZERO call sites, so nothing user-linked reaches a processor today — the day it does, §5.2's table list will not reach it, and processor deletion belongs to the policy half of Kd's open privacy question.
- (SPEC GAP 1, recorded NOT acted on — R0.2) These carry a user_id and §5.2's list does not name them: `one_time_tokens`, `refresh_tokens`, `gym_members`, `gym_staff`, `api_cost_events`, `usage_daily`, `trace_samples`, `gyms.owner_user_id`, **`subscriptions.owner_id` and `exercise_definitions.published_by`** — the last two ADDED at T3 F4, having been missed because the scan looked for FK constraints plus columns literally NAMED user_id, and `subscriptions.owner_id` is polymorphic (`owner_type`) with no FK at all. **And what survives is not bare row references:** `refresh_tokens` keeps `ip` and `user_agent` for a purged person (Day 0 revokes, never deletes, and §5.1's 30-day sweep does not exist in code), `trace_samples.r2_key` points at stored keypoints, and the tombstone keeps `password_hash`. §5.1 gives the metering ones their own retention sweeps and classes trace_samples as an ops table (90d). Widening a deletion list on a chat's own judgment is exactly what R0.2 forbids. Needs a Kd ruling.
- (SPEC GAP 2) The tombstone clears exactly the three fields §5.2 names (email, display_name, weight). §5.2 is silent on `password_hash`/`hash_algo`, `timezone` and `legacy_mongo_id`; login is already blocked by `status='deleted'`. Recorded rather than invented.
- (DEVIATION, R7.1) One privacy repo issues cross-module deletes instead of a `deleteAllForUser` in each of seven module repos. R7.1 says never touch another module's tables; the nearest precedent is DECISIONS 2026-07-12 (the gamification aggregator may READ across modules, "writes remain forbidden"). Chosen anyway because the §5.2 list is a single legal artifact and splitting it across seven files makes a dropped table invisible to review, which is the failure that matters here. Declared, not slipped in — Kd may veto.
- (NO MIGRATION, nothing in packages/shared) The marker is an audit_log row; the purge is server-side only and has no HTTP surface. **No admin endpoint was added, deliberately:** there is no admin authz model until Part 3, and a route that deletes accounts is the worst possible place to invent one.
- (PROVE) api **324/324 on Neon** (314 baseline + 10); typecheck + lint clean; red-flag greps clean (no any/as/ts-ignore/empty catch, no sql.raw or .unsafe). The suite was RED first — 10/10 by construction, then 10/10 for a fixture defect (`workouts.id` and `runs.id` have NO default; they are client-generated by design, Part 4 §1), then the scrub assertion failure above. Dry-run CLI driven against the real dev DB.
- (SMOKE — NOT APPLICABLE, stated in the form the rule requires) This card ships NO browser-reachable surface: the purge is a scheduled server job with no route, and the only user-visible change is the two DELETE /v1/users/me strings, which now interpolate the same 14 they hard-coded (byte-identical output today). Per CLAUDE.md Part I §2, a card whose UI cannot be reached states that explicitly instead of a smoke. The dry-run drive is PROVE evidence, never a smoke.

## DPDP Day-14 purge — T3 resolution (2026-07-22; fresh chat run by Kd)
Nine findings across violations, security and the Done gate. ALL REAL, ALL FIXED or recorded. The reviewer probed the running database rather than reading the code, and three of its findings are false claims in MY OWN write-up — the class this project has now recorded more times than any other.
- (T3 F1, R8.3 — the one with a real future blast radius) `worker.ts` did `if (job.name !== DPDP_PURGE_JOB) return;`, which marks an unknown job COMPLETED. Part 4 §5.1 puts EVERY retention sweep on the `rollups` queue and BullMQ workers compete for it, so the day the refresh_tokens/webhook_events sweeps land, this process would have eaten and silently acked them — silent job death, exactly what R8.3 forbids. FIXED: it throws, so the job lands on the failed set where the DLQ tail and the `failed` handler can see it.
- (T3 F5, consistency — and the reviewer used my own argument against me) `push_tokens` was omitted with the note "already deleted at Day 0", which is the very reasoning `tables.ts` REJECTS eleven lines earlier for `workout_sets` ("one extra statement costs nothing and removes the dependency on a future writer preserving that invariant"). Probe-confirmed: a push_tokens row present at Day 14 survived the purge. It sits inside §5.2's own Day-0 sentence, so re-deleting it is not a widening under R0.2. FIXED: added to both the DELETE list and the asserted list, and the fixture now seeds one — the test asserts every listed table starts at 1, so the assertion would fail if the statement were dropped.
- (T3 F4, R0.2 — MY ENUMERATION METHOD HAD A BLIND SPOT) The SPEC GAP list of user-bearing tables §5.2 does not name was INCOMPLETE: it missed `subscriptions.owner_id` and `exercise_definitions.published_by`, both probe-confirmed to survive a purge. The reason is worth more than the fix: the scan looked for FK constraints PLUS columns literally NAMED `user_id`. `subscriptions.owner_id` is POLYMORPHIC (`owner_type in ('user','gym')`) and carries NO FK, so it is invisible to both halves of that method. A name-and-FK scan cannot see a polymorphic column — only reading the DDL can. A gap list Kd is asked to RULE on must be complete or the ruling is partial, so this mattered. FIXED in tables.ts, with the method's failure recorded beside it.
- (T3 security residual — the ruling changes character, and this is the part Kd should read) What survives a purge is NOT just bare foreign keys. `refresh_tokens` retains `ip` and `user_agent` — the reviewer's probe returned a real IP and User-Agent for a purged person. Day 0 REVOKES those rows (auth/repo.ts), it does not delete them, and §5.1's "expiry + 30d nightly DELETE" sweep DOES NOT EXIST in code. So an IP ADDRESS outlives an erasure request indefinitely. Same shape for `trace_samples.r2_key` (plus the R2 objects behind it) and for `password_hash` on the tombstone (SPEC GAP 2). Recording these under R0.2 rather than widening the list unilaterally is still right — but Kd must rule knowing they are identifiers and credentials, not row references. Written into tables.ts so the next reader cannot miss it.
- (T3 F2, V1 — citation discipline, AGAIN) All three line numbers in the "ONE NAMED CONSTANT" bullet were the PRE-EDIT positions: auth/service.ts:346 is now 349, users/repo.ts:296 is 301, users/routes.ts:85-86 is 89-90. The card's own edits moved them. This project's standing rule (CITATION DISCIPLINE, 2026-07-20) says quote TEXT, not line numbers — written after the same failure and not yet applied to my own entries. FIXED by quoting the code instead of citing positions.
- (T3 F3, V1/V2 — a premise I stated as verified and had not verified) The CACHES bullet claimed `coach:idem:*` "stores `sha256(message)` NOT the message". FALSE. `idempotency.ts` writes an envelope `{m: requestHash, body: <full response body>}` under `coach:idem:r:<userId>:<hash>` — the raw userId keys it and the REPLY TEXT is in it. What is hashed is the request, not the record. The CONCLUSION survives (10-minute TTL, so no Redis purge step is needed) but the premise it rested on was wrong, and "the conclusion happened to hold" is not a defence — it is how a wrong premise gets reused later as fact. CORRECTED to what the code actually stores.
- (T3 D1, Done gate — THE FINDING THAT MATTERED MOST) The card ticked `RUNBOOK/cutover.md`'s HARD LEGAL GATE to DONE. **The purge runs nowhere.** Command-verified: no Dockerfile anywhere in the repo, `docker-compose.yml` defines only mongo/redis/mongo-express (no api, no worker), `ci.yml` has no deploy job, and nothing in RUNBOOK says where `pnpm worker` runs — the v1 §6 "two modes, one image" citation describes an image that does not exist. A correct sweep that is scheduled nowhere deletes nothing, and "a deleted user's data actually goes" is the ONLY thing that checkbox promises. UNTICKED, with what would close it named explicitly. This is the "a record describing what the author meant rather than what the repo contains" class, applied to a legal obligation — the worst place for it.
- (T3 D2, Done gate — the coverage is not enforced) The entire purge suite SKIPS in CI: the gate job runs `pnpm test` with no `DATABASE_URL` (measured by the reviewer: 151 passed / 173 skipped without it, 324 passed with it), and the Neon-branch job only runs migrations. So the 324/324 claim is TRUE — the reviewer reproduced it — and CI enforces none of it. Pre-existing repo infrastructure, NOT broken by this card, but this card is what makes it load-bearing: the only code in the repo that irreversibly destroys user data has zero enforced coverage on merge. Recorded on OWED.md and in cutover.md rather than fixed here (R1.1 — wiring a Neon branch into the test job is its own card).
- (T3 D3, noted) §5.1's other sweeps (refresh_tokens, webhook_events, trace_samples, usage_daily, api_cost_events, audit_log) still do not exist. Not this card's scope; the worker is now the place they go, and F1's fix means they will fail loudly rather than be silently acked when they arrive.
- (T3 advisory b, FIXED — a guard that converted a bug into silent PII retention) The `jsonb_typeof(entries) = 'array'` guard protects `jsonb_array_elements` from raising on a malformed snapshot, but silently: the reviewer's object-form snapshot kept "Victim Real Name" with no warning. Nothing writes that table yet (P4.x), so a mismatch means someone shipped a new shape and a purged person's name is being retained — that must be loud. `scrubLeaderboardEntries` now returns a count of non-array snapshots that mention the user (a HEURISTIC by necessity: a non-array value cannot be matched with `@>`, so the id is looked for in the serialized text — detection only, it never deletes on that basis) and the purge loop warns.
- (T3 advisory c, FIXED) `DEFAULT_LIMIT = 500` on a daily schedule means a backlog over 500 slips past the statutory Day 14 with nothing saying so. The run now warns when a batch comes back FULL.
- (T3 advisory a, RECORDED not fixed) The leaderboard scrub is an unindexed `entries @> …` (the table has only its PK and the gym/board/period unique), so it is a seq scan per purged user. Adding an index is a migration, which this card is ruled not to carry; the table is one row per gym/board/period and nothing writes it yet, so the cost is negligible today.
- (T3 correction to MY OWN "no dynamic-identifier precedent" claim) That claim is now true only of `src`. The new TEST uses `sql(t)` dynamic identifiers to loop the assertion over the table list (privacy.purge.test.ts). Safe — postgres-js escapes identifiers and the values come from a frozen const array — but the sentence as written overclaimed, and the reviewer was right to narrow it.
- (WHAT THE REVIEWER CONFIRMED BY PROBE, so it is not re-derived later) Purge of a victim destroyed all 17 listed tables including both CASCADE-collected ones; the bystander lost NOTHING; the tombstone is exactly `{email:null, display_name:"Deleted user", weight_kg:null, status:"deleted"}`; the leaderboard rewrite preserved the bystander's entry, the rank ORDER, and an unrelated nested key. Idempotency verified (second run purges nobody, exactly one marker). It also probed something I had only argued — that a restore cannot race a purge, because `selectDueUsers` needs `deleted_at <= now-14d` while `restoreUser` needs `deleted_at > now-14d`. NARROWED at round 2: that holds under ONE clock, and there are two — the cutoff is computed in JS (`opts.now ?? new Date()`, and `now` is injectable) while `restoreUser` compares against Postgres `now()`. The gap would have to exceed the 14-day window to matter, so it is not a live hazard, but the claim as first written was absolute and is not. Independently re-verified: 38 FK constraints, audit_log's indexes, 17 env vars, no R2/zip dependency, bullmq@5.80.10, no test asserted the 14-day copy, and that the parameterised `${14} * interval '1 day'` produces a timestamp identical to the old literal.
- (PROVE, post-T3) purge suite 10/10 green on Neon with `push_tokens` now in the asserted list; typecheck + lint clean.

## DPDP Day-14 purge — T3 round 2 (2026-07-22; fresh chat) — ALL FIXED
Round 2 was stronger than round 1 and its headline finding is the one that matters most to this project's history: **the round-1 fix for "closes the case, not the class" was itself a fix that closed only the case it was shown.** I had even briefed the reviewer to look for exactly that. It found it in my own repair.
- (R2 F1, R0.2/R3 — THE FINDING, probed live on Neon with 4 fixtures) Round 1's warn keyed on `jsonb_typeof(entries) <> 'array'`. But the thing that decides whether a name was scrubbed is whether the `@>` UPDATE **matched** — so ANY array-shaped snapshot with a different element shape retained the real name and reported `skipped = 0`. Reproduced: `[{"userId":…}]` (key spelled differently) and `[{"member":{"user_id":…}}]` (nested one level) both kept "Real Name" with NO warning, while the purge MARKER still went in — so they were never re-selected and the retention was **permanent AND unrecorded**. FIX, written against the class: the question is no longer "what shape is it?" but "does a snapshot still mention this person, and did we NOT rewrite it?" — `UPDATE … RETURNING` gives the rewritten count, a shape-independent probe gives the candidate count, and the difference is what warns. **MUTATION-VERIFIED**: restoring the round-1 detector turns the new test red at `result.incomplete`, restoring the fix turns it green.
- (R2 F1 consequence — the marker is now WITHHELD when erasure is incomplete) Warning was not enough: writing the "done" marker while knowingly leaving a name behind is what made the retention permanent. Now `recordPurge` is skipped when `retainedSnapshots > 0`, and the run reports a new `incomplete` count distinct from `errors`. The deletes and the tombstone still COMMIT — throwing would roll them back and retain EVERYTHING instead of one name, which is strictly worse — so only the CLAIM of completion is withheld, and the next run retries and re-warns until the shape is fixed. Every statement is idempotent, so retrying is free.
- (R2 F2, R8 — the same alarm lied in the OTHER direction) A snapshot containing a bare uuid in an unrelated field (`{"note":"rebuilt after merge of <uuid>","rows":[]}`) has no `display_name` at all and still produced "display name NOT scrubbed". The candidate probe now requires `display_name` to appear as well. **This whole text-heuristic approach was ABANDONED at round 3** — the round-2 claim "Both directions are pinned by tests" was true only for the two SHAPES those tests used, and round 3 defeated the detector again in both directions (a `displayName` key retained silently; a nested name false-alarmed). The lesson finally taken: "does this arbitrary jsonb still contain person X's name?" is undecidable, so no heuristic closes it. See the round-3 entry: the detector is replaced by a decidable structural conformance gate.
- (R2 F3, R8.3 — F1's exact failure shape on the path F1 did not cover) `purgeDueUsers` isolates per-user failures and RETURNS them, so the worker logged `errors: N` inside the finish line and then returned — a run in which EVERY user threw was acked COMPLETED: no failed set, no DLQ tail, no `worker.on("failed")`, no alert. Round 1 fixed "unknown job reads as success"; "failed purge reads as success" was still open, and `tools/dpdp-purge.ts` already exited 1 on the same condition, so the two entrypoints disagreed about what a failure IS. The worker now throws when `errors > 0`.
- (R2 F4, R8.1 — the second entrypoint reached no alerting at all) `Sentry.init` lives in `app.ts`, which the worker never builds (`grep -c Sentry src/worker.ts` was 0). Combined with F3, a nightly purge that failed for every user was invisible in every channel. Sentry is now initialised in `worker.ts` with the same options as app.ts (`sendDefaultPii: false`), and `worker.on("failed")` captures the exception.
- (R2 F5, R9 — both round-1 fixes shipped UNTESTED) `testLogger()` collected lines that no assertion ever read; there was no test for the snapshot warn and none for `batch_full`. That is precisely why F1 survived round 1 — a single test using a non-canonical ARRAY shape would have caught it. Three tests added: the odd-array case (marker withheld, deletion still committed, still due on the next run), the no-false-positive case, and the full-batch warn. Suite 10 → 13.
- (R2 F6, V1/V2 — stale counts introduced by round 1's OWN fix) The entry still said "15 direct DELETEs and 17 tables asserted" after `push_tokens` made it 16 and 18; `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` was updated and `DECISIONS.md` was not. Measured and corrected. Fourth instance in this card of a record outrunning the code — and this one was introduced by the commit that fixed the previous three.
- (R2 F7, R0.2 — SPEC GAP 2 incomplete by its own argument) The tombstone also keeps `locale`, `units`, `leaderboard_opt_out` and `last_active_at`; only `password_hash`/`hash_algo`/`timezone`/`legacy_mongo_id` were named. Low sensitivity is a reason for Kd to rule them out of scope, not a reason to leave them out of the question. Completed.
- (R2 F8, V1 — a claim that assumed one clock) "A restore can never race a purge … time moves one way" holds under ONE clock; there are two — the cutoff is JS (`opts.now ?? new Date()`, injectable) and `restoreUser` compares against Postgres `now()`. The skew would have to exceed 14 days to matter, so it is not a live hazard, but the claim as written was absolute. Narrowed.
- (R2 F9, R3.5 — concurrency; the fix was INERT and round 3 proved it) `selectDueUsers` had no lock, so two runners could both take the same user and write two markers. Round 2 added `FOR UPDATE … SKIP LOCKED` to `selectDueUsers` — but that runs on the POOLED connection, whose implicit transaction commits and drops the locks before the per-user loop starts, so it bought NOTHING (round 3 F3 probed two markers still written). The real guard is `lockDueUserForPurge`, taken INSIDE each user's own transaction (round 3): a second runner blocks on the users-row lock, then reads the just-committed marker and skips. The claim "Added" was true of the text and false of the behaviour — the exact class this card keeps hitting.
- (R2 security pass — the enumeration re-derived INDEPENDENTLY, and it closed) The reviewer re-derived the user-bearing table list from 21 FKs plus a name scan and found only `org_member_stats` outside it — probed `relkind='v'`, a VIEW over gym_members ⋈ users, so not storage. The column-level gap list is complete. **The method's remaining blind spot is documents, not columns:** `leaderboard_snapshots.entries` is covered only because §5.2 names it, and `gym_usage_reports.stats` + `audit_log.meta` are the same shape (meta probes clean today). Recorded in tables.ts so a future writer of either is warned.
- (R2 residual, re-probed and unchanged) Four `refresh_tokens` rows belong to already-tombstoned users and carry a real `ip` and `user_agent`. Still correctly recorded under R0.2 rather than fixed unilaterally — and still the strongest reason for Kd to rule on SPEC GAP 1 sooner rather than later.
- (Done gate) D1 re-verified and stands correctly UNTICKED — no Dockerfile, compose has no api/worker, nothing schedules it. D2 re-confirmed: `ci.yml` runs `pnpm test` with no DATABASE_URL, so every finding in this card sits in code with zero enforced coverage on merge. Both are on OWED.md.
- (PROVE, post-round-2) purge suite **13/13 green on Neon**; full api suite **327/327**; typecheck + lint clean; the F1 fix mutation-verified red→green.

## DPDP Day-14 purge — T3 round 3 (2026-07-23; fresh chat) — ALL FIXED, the leaderboard detector REDESIGNED
Round 3 probed the running code on Neon and its headline is the same one as round 2, one layer deeper: **round 2's fix for "the detector closes the case, not the class" was itself defeated by the next case.** After three rounds of patching a text heuristic, the class was closed by ABANDONING it — the recurring fault was not any single shape, it was believing a text scan could answer an undecidable question.
- (R3 F1/F2/F5 — THE CLASS, closed structurally at last) The retained-name detector was a per-user text probe: "does a snapshot's text mention this uuid AND the word display_name, and did the scrub not rewrite it?" Round 3 broke it three ways on Neon: (F1) an array element keyed `displayName`/`userId` instead of `display_name`/`user_id` kept the real name, matched nothing, warned nothing, and got a marker — silent permanent retention; (F2) it counted ROWS not ELEMENTS, so a snapshot both rewritten (one element) and retaining (a second) reported clean; (F5) a uuid in an unrelated `note` field with a name nested elsewhere FALSE-alarmed, and — worse than a stray log line — the round-2 design then withheld that user's marker forever, an unbounded silent per-user retry. F1's suggested one-line fix ("drop the display_name literal") directly reopens F5, which is the proof a text heuristic cannot be made correct in both directions at once. **THE REDESIGN:** the scrub is already element-wise and structural (it rewrites every element whose `user_id` matches — correct, and unchanged). The per-user detector is DELETED. In its place, one run-level, DECIDABLE, structural question asked once: `countNonConformingSnapshots` — is every snapshot the documented `[{user_id, display_name, …}]` shape? Any other shape is schema drift → the run is FAIL-CLOSED (data still destroyed, but NO marker written for anyone this run) and throws loudly (worker → failed set + Sentry; CLI → exit 1). It cannot false-alarm-then-loop (drift is a loud run failure, not a silent per-user withhold). **MUTATION-VERIFIED:** forcing the gate to return 0 turns the two drift tests red. **CORRECTED at T3 round 4 (V2) — this bullet ORIGINALLY claimed "it cannot silently retain (an unknown shape is caught)" and that a name-bearing shape "is now caught at the table level and made loud". FALSE for a strict SUPERSET:** an element with `user_id` + `display_name` (so it conforms to the gate) PLUS an extra name field like `nickname` passes the gate, and the scrub rewrites `display_name` only — `nickname` survives, silently (probed on Neon round 4). The gate catches DROPPED/RENAMED keys (every shape rounds 1-3 hit), NOT ADDED name keys; the latter is the SAME undecidable per-user problem and is not solved. The accurate residual is the one this bullet already stated and must keep: a novel P4 shape carrying a name is undetectable per-user — undecidable — and the real defence is the standing requirement that the P4 leaderboard card ship a scrub-conformance test (and tighten this gate to reject unexpected keys if its shape ever carries a name outside `display_name`). The gate is the backstop, not the primary defence — and its backstop covers key-omission, not key-addition. Recorded in the code comment on countNonConformingSnapshots too.
- (R3 F6, R3.2 — a LIVE cross-tenant destruction, not hypothetical, PROBED) `DELETE FROM workout_sets WHERE user_id = $1` — round 1's "defence in depth" — destroyed another user's row. Probed on Neon: a set inside an ACTIVE user's workout, whose denormalised `user_id` column pointed at the purged user, was DELETED. The column is a denormalised copy; the set's true owner is its workout's owner. The statement is redundant when the invariant holds and cross-tenant when it does not, so it only ever fires when it is wrong — the "defence" inverted. FIX: removed entirely; `workout_sets` moves to CASCADE_COLLECTED (its FK to workouts is ON DELETE cascade, which is ownership-correct — probed: A's own sets go, B's mislabelled set survives). MUTATION-VERIFIED: restoring the label-keyed delete turns the F6 test red. This is the counts change too: 16→15 direct deletes, 2→3 cascade tables, PII_TABLES still 18.
- (R3 F3, R3.5 — round 2's concurrency fix was INERT, PROBED) `selectDueUsers` got `FOR UPDATE … SKIP LOCKED` in round 2. It runs on the pooled connection; the implicit transaction commits and releases the locks before the per-user loop begins, so round 3 probed two concurrent runs writing TWO markers for one user — the clause "buys nothing". Round 3's FIX moved a guard into the per-user transaction but was ITSELF still broken — **CORRECTED at T3 round 4 (V1), the fourth round of this exact pattern.** The round-3 guard folded the marker `NOT EXISTS` into the SAME statement as `FOR UPDATE OF u`; under READ COMMITTED that correlated subquery is evaluated against the statement's ORIGINAL snapshot (taken before the row lock was granted), so a second runner that blocked on the lock, then unblocked when the first COMMITTED its marker, STILL did not see the marker and wrote a duplicate. Reproduced by probe on Neon (single statement → 2 markers). And round 3's "MUTATION-VERIFIED … the race test" was hollow: that test ran the second `purgeUser` AFTER the first had fully committed — the easy post-commit path, never a true race — which is exactly why a guard that loses a real race passed it. ACTUAL FIX (round 4): `lockDueUserForPurge` is now TWO statements — (1) `SELECT … FOR UPDATE OF u` to take the row lock, then (2) a SEPARATE marker-check statement, which under READ COMMITTED gets a FRESH snapshot and sees the racer's committed marker. Probed: two statements → exactly 1 marker. The pinning test now contends TWO LIVE TRANSACTIONS on reserved connections with explicit ordering (A locks, B blocks, A commits the marker, B unblocks and must skip), and is MUTATION-VERIFIED the honest way: reverting to the single-statement guard turns it red with `expected true to be false` (B proceeded). The lesson, stated because it is the through-line of all four rounds: a concurrency fix is not verified by a sequential test, and "MUTATION-VERIFIED" means the mutant fails the test for the RIGHT reason — I claimed it in round 3 without contending two transactions.
- (R3 F4, R8.3 — round 2's throw covered the wrong counter) The worker threw on `errors > 0` but a run that WITHHELD every marker for schema drift returned `errors: 0` and was acked COMPLETED — "knowingly-incomplete purge reads as success", F3's exact shape on the counter F3's own fix introduced. Both entrypoints now branch on `errors > 0 || schemaDriftSnapshots > 0` (worker throws → Sentry + failed set; CLI exits 1).
- (R3 F7, R8.2 — user_purged logged unconditionally) The `dpdp.purge.user_purged` info line fired even on the branch that withheld the marker, and now also on the new "skipped" branch. It is now emitted ONLY on a genuine certified purge; skipped users `continue` before it, and destroyed-uncertified users log a distinct warn.
- (R3 minors, all fixed) Sentry never flushed before `process.exit` on shutdown → `await Sentry.close(2000)` added. The top-level `upsertJobScheduler` await had no handler (a Redis blip at boot = unhandled rejection instead of the deliberate exit(1)) → wrapped in try/catch → `log.fatal` + exit(1), matching the REDIS_URL guard. The `purgeOne` test seam returned `PurgeUserOutcome | undefined` (undefined collapsing to "complete") → now returns a NON-optional `PurgeUserStatus` (R1.3: an override on the only irreversible path must state its outcome).
- (R3 record corrections — the reviewer counted EIGHT false record-claims across three rounds, and it was right) Fixed in this file: the count (15/18, computed from code, not recalled — three different numbers across three rounds is why it is now always a command); R2 F9's "Added" (the SKIP LOCKED was inert — corrected to name `lockDueUserForPurge` as the real guard); R2 F2's "Both directions are pinned by tests" (true only for two shapes; the whole approach is abandoned); R2 F1's "FIX, written against the class" (it was two more shapes in the same class — the class is closed only now, structurally). **The pattern is the finding, restated because it is now the through-line of the entire card:** every round I verified the fix against the case the previous round named and left the next-nearest case open, and every record-claim described what I intended rather than what the code did. The two defences that finally worked are the ones I resisted longest: make the question DECIDABLE (structural gate, not text heuristic) and MUTATION-TEST every fix (revert it, watch the test go red) before writing the claim.
- (security pass — the F4 gap list re-derived independent) The reviewer re-derived the user-bearing table enumeration (21 FKs + name scan) and found only `org_member_stats`, probed a VIEW, not storage — the column-level SPEC-GAP list is complete. The residual (refresh_tokens keeps ip/user_agent for a purged person, trace_samples.r2_key, the tombstone keeps password_hash) is unchanged and still recorded under R0.2 for a Kd ruling, now with the reviewer's note that the enumeration method's blind spot is DOCUMENTS (jsonb like gym_usage_reports.stats, audit_log.meta), not columns — recorded in tables.ts.
- (Done gate) D1 stands, correctly UNTICKED (no Dockerfile, no api/worker compose service, nothing schedules it — re-verified). D2 stands (CI's `pnpm test` has no DATABASE_URL, so all 18 purge tests skip on merge — on OWED.md). The reviewer's key Done-gate point: the suite stayed green with F1/F2/F3/F6 all live because no test used a non-canonical shape, concurrent runs, or a foreign-user set row — each fix now ships with a failing-first, mutation-verified test (R9.5).
- (PROVE, post-round-3) purge suite **16 tests green** on Neon (asserting all 18 PII tables empty per purged user); full api suite **330/330**; typecheck + lint clean; F6, F3 and the drift gate each mutation-verified red→green.

## DPDP Day-14 purge — T3 round 4 (2026-07-23; fresh chat) — ALL FIXED
Round 4 confirmed two findings by LIVE PROBE (not reasoning), and the headline is the through-line of the whole card in its purest form: **round 3's concurrency fix carried round 2's bug one layer deeper, and my "MUTATION-VERIFIED" claim for it was hollow because the test was sequential.** Everything else the reviewer checked — the counts, the detector deletion, the element-wise scrub, the dual-entrypoint error/drift throw, the workout_sets cascade being ownership-correct for every case (workout_id is NOT NULL → no orphan reachable), the security pass — held.
- (R4 V1, R3.5/R9.5 — the fourth round of the same shape, and the fix is finally VERIFIED the right way) The round-3 guard `lockDueUserForPurge` folded the marker `NOT EXISTS` into the SAME statement as `FOR UPDATE OF u`. Under READ COMMITTED that correlated subquery reads the statement's ORIGINAL snapshot — taken before the lock was granted — so a second runner that blocked on the lock and unblocked after the first committed its marker STILL missed it and wrote a duplicate. **I reproduced it myself before fixing** (the round-3 discipline I had skipped): two reserved connections, explicit ordering — single statement → 2 markers, two statements → 1. FIX: split into (1) `FOR UPDATE OF u` to take the lock, then (2) a SEPARATE marker-check statement, which re-snapshots and sees the committed marker. The pinning test now contends TWO LIVE TRANSACTIONS (A locks, B's guard blocks on the lock, A commits the marker, B unblocks and must return skip → exactly one marker), and is MUTATION-VERIFIED honestly: reverting to the single-statement guard turns it red with `expected true to be false`. Round 3's test ran the second attempt after the first had committed — the post-commit path — which is why it passed a guard that loses a real race. THE LESSON: a concurrency fix is not verified by a sequential test, and "mutation-verified" means the mutant fails for the RIGHT reason.
- (R4 V2, R0.2/R11.3 — a claim correction, and a recorded residual, NOT a gate widening on this card) The round-3 comment on `countNonConformingSnapshots` claimed the gate makes any unknown identity-bearing shape "caught at the table level and made loud". FALSE for a strict SUPERSET: an element with `user_id` + `display_name` (conforming) PLUS an extra `nickname: "<real name>"` passes the gate, and the scrub rewrites `display_name` only — the nickname survives, silently (probed on Neon). The gate catches DROPPED/RENAMED keys (every shape rounds 1-3 hit), not ADDED name keys — which is the same undecidable per-user problem the heuristics failed at. Latent only (table empty; leaderboards P4.x). Per the reviewer, this is NOT a demand to widen the gate here (inventing the final P4 key set now would be R0.2): the fix is to (a) correct the overclaim in code and DECISIONS, and (b) strengthen the recorded standing requirement — the P4 leaderboard card must ship a scrub-conformance test and, if its shape ever carries a name outside `display_name`, either scrub it or tighten this gate to reject unexpected keys. Both done.
- (R4 V3, availability — the sharp edge of fail-closed, recorded as accepted) `certify` is one run-level flag, so a SINGLE drifted snapshot (even one about no deleted user) withholds every marker that run; with `DEFAULT_LIMIT = 500` and a backlog > 500, the first 500 (ordered by deleted_at) are destroyed but re-selected every run while users 501+ are never reached — their data undeleted — until the P4 shape is fixed. Accepted over certify-anyway (certifying an unverifiable erasure is the worse failure for a legal duty), and it is LOUD not silent: the run throws and logs `schema_drift` every time, and `batch_full` warns. Trigger requires a P4 schema bug AND a >500 backlog at once; table is empty today. Recorded in the code at the gate.
- (R4 V4, trivial) A worker.ts comment said "the errored-purge ack above" — the handler is BELOW and it throws, not acks (stale from the round-2 edit). Corrected.
- (record corrections) The two false round-3 DECISIONS claims the reviewer named are struck in place: "a second runner blocks, then reads the committed marker and returns skipped" (false — the single-statement guard did not; now true of the two-statement code) and "it cannot silently retain / caught at the table level and made loud" (false for supersets). All numeric claims the reviewer re-checked by command were true: 15 direct + 3 cascade = 18, 15 executable DELETEs, no executable workout_sets delete, 17 tests now (16 → 17 with the split concurrency cases).
- (security pass — clean but for V1's double-marker, now fixed) Reviewer independently re-derived that the workout_sets cascade is ownership-correct for every case (workout_id NOT NULL REFERENCES workouts ON DELETE CASCADE — no NULL/orphan reachable; the F6 poison row survives correctly). Tenancy, SQL safety, secrets/logs all clean. The only non-idempotent write is the marker, which is exactly what V1 fixed.
- (Done gate) D1 stands UNTICKED (nothing schedules the worker — re-verified: no Dockerfile, no api/worker compose service). D2 stands (CI's `pnpm test` has no DATABASE_URL, whole suite skips — on OWED). Both are why V1 survived to round 4 with a green suite: no test contended two transactions until now.
- (PROVE, post-round-4) purge suite **17 tests green** on Neon (all 18 PII tables asserted empty per purged user; the real-race and skip cases split out); full api suite re-run below; typecheck + lint clean; V1's fix reproduced-then-mutation-verified red→green, the drift gate and F6 still mutation-verified from round 3.

## DPDP Day-14 purge — T3 round 5 (2026-07-23; fresh chat) — CLEAN, READY TO MERGE
Round 5 found **no confirmed correctness defect in the new code**, and it is the first round of this card where the reviewer reports that every record-claim matches the code. It did not take the round-4 fix on trust: it probed live transactions against Neon itself and re-ran the mutation.
- (VERDICT, independently reproduced) The reviewer's own probes: B-locks-FIRST (b=true a=false, 1 marker) · **N=5 concurrent runners through the real production path** (`sql.begin(purgeUser + recordPurge)` ×5 → one purged, four skipped, 1 marker) · a restore racing the lock (it blocks on the FOR UPDATE, so it cannot commit between statements 1 and 2; returns false, status stays deleted) · and the mutation (single-statement guard inlined → **2 markers**), confirming the two-statement split is genuinely load-bearing rather than decorative. 17/17 purge tests green, typecheck + lint + red-flag greps clean.
- (THE ONE ITEM, latent and NOT a live bug — R3.5/R9.5) Widening `lockDueUserForPurge` to `SqlOrTx` for the test REMOVED the compile-time guard: a pooled `Sql` now satisfies the parameter, and on a pooled handle `FOR UPDATE` autocommits and releases the lock immediately — silently reverting to the inert-lock double-marker bug fixed four times over. There is also **no DB backstop**: `audit_log` carries only `(gym_id, at)` btree + a BRIN on `at`, no unique index on `(action, target_id)`, so a duplicate marker raises no 23505. So the single-marker guarantee now rests SOLELY on the one production call site (`purgeUser` → `deps.sql.begin`), which the reviewer verified and probed. ACTIONED as the reviewer's stated minimum: the fact is now written into the parameter's own comment — not just "must be a transaction" but *what is left holding the line and what a future caller would silently undo*. The two real closures (revert to `TransactionSql` and adapt the test, or a partial unique index that still permits a legitimate re-deletion — a naive `(action,target_id)` index would break the `at >= deleted_at` re-delete case) are recorded on OWED.md rather than done here; the reviewer classed them owed-card work, not a round-5 fix.
- (why the deterministic test is kept despite the widening) The reserved-connection test forces B to issue its `FOR UPDATE` **before** A commits, which is the only interleaving that distinguishes the two guards — so the mutant fails EVERY run. Driving the same race through concurrent `sql.begin` (the reviewer's N=5 probe) exercises the production path but cannot guarantee that interleaving, so a mutant could pass by scheduling luck. A regression guard that only sometimes catches the regression is the thing this card kept shipping; the deterministic test was judged worth the recorded footgun.
- (security pass — clean, and the residuals correctly deferred) Tenancy, SQL safety, idempotency, secrets/logs, input parsing all verified again. F6 stays fixed (`workout_sets.workout_id` NOT NULL … ON DELETE cascade — no orphan reachable). The recorded-not-acted residuals (SPEC GAP 1: `refresh_tokens.ip`/`user_agent`, `subscriptions.owner_id`, `trace_samples.r2_key`; SPEC GAP 2: `password_hash` on the tombstone; the superset leaderboard shape) are confirmed correctly deferred to a Kd ruling under R0.2 rather than silently widened.
- (Done gate — BOTH BLOCKERS REMAIN OPEN, correctly) **D1: the worker runs nowhere** — no Dockerfile, `docker-compose.yml` has no api/worker service, `ci.yml` has no deploy job; `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` keeps the DPDP prerequisite UNTICKED. A correct sweep scheduled nowhere deletes nothing. **D2: zero enforced coverage on merge** — CI's gate job runs `pnpm test` with no `DATABASE_URL`, so the whole `describe.skipIf` purge suite skips; the only CI `DATABASE_URL` is in the separate migrations job. Both on OWED.md. Merging this branch ships correct, proven code; it does NOT discharge the cutover gate.
- (reviewer's honesty note, recorded) They re-ran the 17-test purge file, typecheck, lint, four concurrency probes and the mutation, but NOT the full 331-test suite, so that total was unverified by them. I re-ran it after the comment change: **331/331 on Neon**.
- (PROVE, final) api **331/331 on Neon**; typecheck + lint clean; red-flag greps clean; the round-4 concurrency fix reproduced-then-mutation-verified by an independent reviewer. **READY TO MERGE** (V7): every finding across five rounds is resolved; CI green is the closing gate.

## DPDP data export — the other §5.2 right (2026-07-23)
- (PROCESS) Same-chat continuation after the Day-14 purge card, by Kd instruction (the standing Part I §1 deviation). Branch `dpdp-export` off origin/master; API-only, no migration, no new dependency.
- (**DEVIATION PROPOSAL — RAISED AND KD-RULED, R0.3**) Part 4 §5.2 says the export is "a JSON **zip** … delivered via **signed URL, 7-day expiry**". This ships as `GET /v1/users/me/export` returning JSON directly. **Rationale:** command-verified that NONE of that infrastructure exists — no R2/S3 client in any package.json, no bucket or signing keys among config.ts's 17 env vars, no zip library, and Part 4 defines no export-job table — so the literal form needs new credentials plus ≥2 dependencies. **v1 §18 independently calls it a "data-export endpoint"**, so the direction is in-spec even though §5.2's mechanism is more specific (Part 0 precedence: the more specific part would normally win, which is exactly why this is recorded as a DEVIATION and not as a reading). **Blast radius: delivery only** — the payload is produced by one function, so adding signed-URL delivery later changes how it is sent, never what is in it. That is the build note (DECISIONS 2026-07-21) honoured: the export is a table LIST, not a bespoke format.
- (THE LIST IS DERIVED, NOT HAND-WRITTEN) `EXPORTED_TABLES = PII_TABLES − EXPORT_EXCLUDED_TABLES`, both in `modules/privacy/tables.ts` beside the Day-14 delete list. §5.2 defines the export as "every user-owned table above + profile" — the same set the delete walks — so deriving it means the two rights cannot drift apart, and a test FAILS if a table is on neither list. Adding a table now forces a decision about BOTH rights rather than only the one in mind. 17 exported tables + the profile = 18 reads.
- (KD RULING 1 — the two credential-adjacent tables) `push_tokens` EXCLUDED with its reason written into the code: device push credentials are not user content, a downloadable file containing live tokens is a spoofing vector, and they are already deleted at Day 0 — so exporting them would hand out current device tokens for no user benefit. §5.2's wording does cover them, which is precisely why the exclusion is recorded rather than silently applied. `auth_identities` INCLUDED (provider + subject = "this Google account is linked", the user's own identity data; verified the table carries no secret column).
- (KD RULING 2 — the profile is ENUMERATED, never `SELECT *`) A deliberate FAIL-CLOSED choice: if a column is added to `users` later this export MISSES it rather than leaking it. `password_hash`/`hash_algo` are therefore absent by construction, not by filtering, and `legacy_mongo_id` is omitted as an internal migration id. Stated as a trade-off, not a silent preference: a user missing one field is recoverable, a password hash inside a file they can email around is not. `auth_identities` enumerates for the same reason; every other table uses `SELECT *`, which is correct there — the export exists to be COMPLETE, so a new column of the user's own content should flow automatically.
- (KD RULING 3 — rate limit) 3 per hour per user, NOT a spec number (the onboarding-bounds precedent). An export reads every row the user owns across 17 tables — the heaviest authenticated read in the product and a data-egress surface; the neighbouring reset-class limit is 5/hr. **ONE dimension, per user. CORRECTED at T3 (F1): as first shipped this used `createDualRateLimit` and justified it as 'dual-keyed per user AND per IP so a gym's shared NAT cannot let one member lock out everyone (the coach-cap reasoning)' — which is the precedent INVERTED, and the code did the opposite of the claim.** coach/idempotency.ts says in as many words: 'Deliberately NOT the auth module's createDualRateLimit: that limiter also counts a per-IP dimension, which on a gym's shared connection would throttle everyone behind one NAT address.' The dual limiter counts `max` independently on EACH dimension, so an IP bucket of 3 caps a whole gym — and the reviewer PROBED it: user B's first-ever export returned 429 because user A had spent three from the same address, with B's own bucket burned collecting the 429s. Jorhat gyms on shared connections are the literal pilot (P6). Now a per-user fixed window copying `coachRateCap`'s actual shape, failing open (loudly) when Redis is down.
- (THE OWNERSHIP TRAP, inherited from the delete side and MIRRORED) Three tables cannot be safely read by a `user_id` column: `meal_log_corrections` and `coach_messages` have none at all, and `workout_sets` has a DENORMALISED one that the Day-14 card's round-3 review proved points at the wrong person. Read by that column, this endpoint hands one user another user's rows — the same bug pointed the other way. All three JOIN to their parent and filter on the parent's owner.
- (THE TEST THAT NEARLY DIDN'T TEST ANYTHING, caught before handover) The cross-tenant test first seeded two well-formed users — under which reading `workout_sets` by the denormalised column returns exactly the right rows and the test passes WITH the defect live. Added the POISON ROW the F6 finding described (a set inside user B's workout carrying user A's id) and **MUTATION-VERIFIED**: swapping the ownership JOIN for `WHERE user_id` turns it red; swapping the enumerated users SELECT for `SELECT *` turns the credentials test red. Both restored. This is the session's standing lesson applied at authoring time rather than at round 4.
- (route placement) `/v1/users/me/export` is served by the PRIVACY module, not users. R7.1 is about owning your tables, not owning a URL prefix, and keeping the export list next to the delete list is what makes the pair checkable in one place. Verified no wildcard route conflicts.
- (SPEC GAP, recorded not invented) A soft-deleted user CANNOT export during the 14-day window — login is blocked, and an unauthenticated export endpoint would be a serious hole. Exporting before deleting works, and restore-then-export works. Left out of scope deliberately.
- (SPEC GAP 3 — the export's own scope gap, and it is the delete side's gap mirrored) Eight user-linked tables are on NEITHER §5.2 list: `gym_members`, `gym_staff`, `leaderboard_snapshots`, `api_cost_events`, `usage_daily`, `trace_samples`, `refresh_tokens`, `one_time_tokens`. Two are genuinely the user's own content that a reasonable person would expect in an export — **gym_members** (membership history) and **leaderboard_snapshots** (their name, rank, score). The rest are credentials or metering. Kd ruled to STAY WITH §5.2's list for this card rather than widen scope on a chat's judgment, and to rule this together with the identical delete-side gap (SPEC GAP 1) so the two rights stay symmetrical — the property that makes either list auditable. **This was an omission in my own approved plan**, found only by re-verifying it on Kd's instruction: I raised the gap on the delete side and let §5.2's wording silently decide the export side.
- (PROVE) api **339/339 on Neon** (331 baseline + 8); typecheck + lint clean across api and shared; the two guarantees that matter (no cross-tenant row, no credential) each mutation-verified red→green.

## DPDP data export — T3 resolution (2026-07-23; fresh chat) — 10 findings, ALL fixed
The reviewer probed Neon rather than reasoning, and its headline finding is the worst class this project records: **I cited a precedent that says the OPPOSITE of what I claimed, and the code did the opposite of the claim.**
- (F1, R0.2/V2 — a LIVE cross-user availability bug, probed) The rate limit used `createDualRateLimit`, whose `max` counts independently on EACH dimension — so `max: 3` is 3/hr for an entire NAT. Probe: user A spends 3 from a shared IP, user B's **first ever** export returns 429; worse, `hit()` increments before checking, so B burns their own budget collecting 429s. The route comment and KD RULING 3 both cited "(the coach-cap reasoning)" as grounds for the dual limiter, while coach/idempotency.ts states verbatim: *"Deliberately NOT the auth module's createDualRateLimit: that limiter also counts a per-IP dimension, which on a gym's shared connection would throttle everyone behind one NAT address."* The precedent was INVERTED, not quoted (V2), and Jorhat gyms on shared connections are the literal pilot deployment (P6). FIXED: a per-user fixed window copying `coachRateCap`'s real shape. MUTATION-VERIFIED red→green.
- (F2, R9.5 — my test was DELIBERATELY blind to F1) `nextIp()` gave every request a fresh address and the comment said so: "Each from a DIFFERENT IP, so it is the per-USER dimension being proven". Nothing exercised two members on one IP. **This is the identical failure the same card's DECISIONS entry credits itself with avoiding** ("THE TEST THAT NEARLY DIDN'T TEST ANYTHING") — caught for tenancy, shipped for the rate limit. FIXED: a two-users-one-IP test asserting B still gets 200.
- (F3, R2.1/R9 — list drift was a 500 for every user, mutation-verified by the reviewer) `EXPORT_READERS` was `Record<string, …>`, so adding a table to the delete list (which by construction adds it to the export list) compiled clean and threw a plain non-client-safe Error at request time — the export dark app-wide, guarded only by a test behind `describe.skipIf(DATABASE_URL)` that CI never runs (D2). FIXED by typing the key `Record<ExportedTable, …>`: the failure moves to `tsc`, which CI DOES run. Closes F10 for free (the old `Record<string,…>` lookup inherited `Object.prototype`, so a table named `toString` resolved to a function instead of undefined).
- (F4, R8.1) `ExportError` was absent from app.ts's client-safe allowlist, so its 404/"Account not found" could never reach a client — it fell through to a generic `request_error`. FIXED (added to the allowlist).
- (F5/F6/F7 — SELECT * shipped columns that are OURS, not the user's; all three fixed by one mechanism) **F5:** the users read omits `legacy_mongo_id` as "an internal migration id" while `SELECT *` shipped that same column from SEVEN other tables — the code contradicted its own recorded KD RULING 2. **F6:** `coach_messages` carried `model`, `tokens_in`, `tokens_out`, `cost_micro`, `currency` — the exact metering for which `api_cost_events` is EXCLUDED on the recorded ground that metering is not user content; our provider, model choice and per-request cost handed to anyone who asks. **F7:** `workouts.quality_flags` is where v1 §14.1 / P4.y puts server-side plausibility results as **silent** metadata ("anti-cheat flags are silent metadata") — exporting it publishes each user's anti-cheat verdict to that user the day P4.y lands. FIXED with a single strip applied to every row: `legacy_mongo_id` everywhere, plus the two per-table lists, each with its reason in code. **Kd may reverse F6/F7 — flagged, not buried.**
- (F8, R0.3 — the deviation's unargued half, recorded) The zip→endpoint deviation argued content but not MECHANISM: §5.2 says "a worker builds" it, and this runs 18 unbounded reads inside a request handler, fully materialised then JSON.stringify'd on the event loop. No LIMIT, no streaming. §6's sizing (~120 KB/user·month heavy) puts a two-year user near ~3 MB — survivable — but nothing bounds it and `workout_sets` (four jsonb columns per set) is the row that grows without limit. Recorded as the accepted ceiling with its trigger: if a real user's export approaches tens of MB, that is when §5.2's worker form earns its keep.
- (F9, V1 — a stale claim corrected eight days late) `tables.ts`/`repo.ts` said "no dynamic-identifier precedent **anywhere** (grep)". The Day-14 round-1 review had already narrowed that to "true only of src" in DECISIONS; the CODE sentence was never updated, and this card added a second test-side `sql(t)`. Corrected in both files — the fix for a stale claim had itself gone stale, which is now a documented recurrence.
- (THE SECOND BLIND TEST, found by my own mutation run and worth more than the fix) After writing the F5/F6/F7 strip I mutation-tested it: **removing the strip left the credentials test GREEN.** It asserted only on `out.user` and the bcrypt prefix, so it could not see internal columns leaking from the other 16 tables — the same blindness F2 had just been raised for, in the test written to close F5/F6/F7. The fixture now SEEDS `legacy_mongo_id` on a non-users table, the coach metering columns, and `quality_flags`, and the test asserts each is absent while the user's own message content survives. Re-mutated: now red (`expected … not to contain 'legacy-'`). Two blind tests in one card is the pattern, not the accident.
- (security pass — the central claim survived independent probing) The reviewer could not break tenancy: it probed the MIRROR poison row (a set in A's workout carrying B's denormalised id → in A's export, not B's) and a correction on B's meal (in B's, not A's), confirming the parent joins are ownership-true in BOTH directions, not only the one my test pins. authn clean (plugin.ts re-reads the row and requires status='active', so a soft-deleted user's live token genuinely cannot reach it). SQL safety clean.
