Recorded, not actioned: an export writes no `audit_log` row — the largest data-egress event a user can trigger currently leaves no trace. v1 §18 mandates audit only on money/admin actions, so this is a note, not a violation.
- (Done gate) **D2 now covers this card**: all 9 export tests sit behind `describe.skipIf(DATABASE_URL)` and CI's gate job supplies none, so the whole suite — including the drift guard that needs no database at all — runs nowhere on merge. Already on OWED. **SMOKE: none is owed and this states so explicitly** per CLAUDE.md Part I §2 — there is NO web surface for `/v1/users/me/export`; nothing in `apps/web` calls it, so no browser click-through exists to run. The web download button is a later card.
- (PROVE, post-T3) api **340/340 on Neon** (339 + the NAT test); typecheck + lint clean; F1 and the internal-column strip each mutation-verified red→green.

## DPDP data export — T3 round 2 (2026-07-23; fresh chat) — 6 findings, 2 blocking, ALL fixed
- (R2 F1, R2.1/R9 — BLOCKING, and it is round 1's F3 RE-CREATED INSIDE THE FIX FOR F5/F6/F7) `INTERNAL_COLUMNS_BY_TABLE` was `Partial<Record<string, …>>` — the exact string-keyed shape F3 was raised against for `EXPORT_READERS`. I fixed the class in one map and reopened it in the other, in the same commit. Probed both ways: renaming the key to `coach_message` left `tsc` at **exit 0** and shipped `model`/`tokens_in`/`tokens_out`/`cost_micro`/`currency` in every user's downloadable file, visible only to a suite CI does not run. FIXED to `Partial<Record<ExportedTable, …>>` (and `stripInternal(table: ExportedTable, …)`); the same typo now fails the build with **TS2561 "did you mean 'coach_messages'?"** — verified by re-running the probe against the fix.
- (R2 F2, R9 — BLOCKING, and the cheapest real win in the card) The one guard that touches NO SQL — "the export list and the delete list cannot drift apart" — sat inside the `describe.skipIf(DATABASE_URL)` block, so it ran NOWHERE: CI's gate job supplies no DATABASE_URL. This is NOT D2 restated; D2 is "the Postgres tests don't run", whereas this one **could run today**. Lifted into its own ungated `describe`. Verified: with no DATABASE_URL the file now reports **1 passed / 8 skipped** instead of 9 skipped — the compliance guard between a drifted list and a broken export executes on every merge.
- (R2 F3 — the accepted ceiling was priced without the connection pool) Round 1's F8 recorded the export's memory and event-loop cost but missed that `app.ts` builds `postgres(url, { max: 1 })`: ONE connection per API process. So an export SERIALISES the process's only connection for all 18 round trips and every other in-flight request queues behind it. Amended in the code with an explicit trigger (a real export approaching tens of MB, OR the pool staying at max:1 once the API serves concurrent traffic). Recorded, not silently changed — giving the export its own pool is Kd's call.
- (R2 F4, R10/Part IV #6) `Content-Disposition` is set but absent from app.ts's `exposedHeaders`, so browser JS reading the response cross-origin cannot see it — the Card-4 header class, which app.ts's own comment documents verbatim. NOT a live break: the natural download path (a link/navigation) has the browser honour the header itself. Recorded on the route for the web download card, which must add it to `exposedHeaders` if it wants the filename from a `fetch()`. Publishing a header is a policy choice, not a drive-by (the coach card's precedent).
- (R2 F5, V1) Round 1's "closes F10 for free" was true of `EXPORT_READERS` only. `EXPORTED_TABLES`' filter still used `t in EXPORT_EXCLUDED_TABLES`, which walks `Object.prototype` — a table named `toString` would test as excluded and silently vanish from the export. Unreachable today; FIXED with `Object.hasOwn`, and F1's typed key closes the other half.
- (R2 F6, V1) The `now` seam's doc claimed "so `exportedAt` is assertable" while no caller and no test ever passed one — a claim with nothing behind it, the class this project keeps recording. Now asserted with a fixed clock, so the sentence is true.
- (security pass — clean, and the reviewer re-probed rather than inheriting) It independently re-verified tenancy by MUTATION (swapping the `workout_sets` ownership join for `WHERE s.user_id` turns the cross-tenant test red), confirmed the soft-delete claim against `auth/plugin.ts`, and confirmed the new cap is neither bypassable nor cross-user: keying the counter on `req.ip` turns BOTH new tests red in opposite directions, and a capped request costs no DB read (4 requests → 3 handler executions). v1 §18's per-IP concern is still covered by the global 300/min limiter.
- (residual worth having in writing, from the reviewer) The strip is COLUMN-level and cannot see inside jsonb. `workout_sets` already carries `fault_counts`, `rom_stats`, `calibration`; if P4.y's plausibility verdicts ever land in a jsonb rather than `workouts.quality_flags`, F7's protection fails silently and nothing fails. The P4.y card should read this line.
- (Done gate) **SMOKE: none owed, and the claim was verified** — `grep -rn "me/export" apps/web/src` returns nothing, so there is no browser surface to click. D2 still swallows the 8 database-backed export tests; the 9th now runs in CI. D1 (worker deployment) is unrelated to this endpoint, which the api serves.
- (PROVE, post-round-2) api **340/340 on Neon**; typecheck + lint clean; the F1 fix verified by re-running the reviewer's own probe (TS2561), and F2 verified by running the file with no DATABASE_URL.

## DPDP data export — T3 round 3 (2026-07-23) — the blocker fixed, READY TO MERGE
- (R3-F1, R2.1/R9 — BLOCKING, and it is the SAME defect a THIRD time, one level down each round) Round 1 typed `EXPORT_READERS`' key. Round 2 typed `INTERNAL_COLUMNS_BY_TABLE`'s key. Round 3 found its **VALUES** still bare strings — and the values are the half a person edits when adding a column to strip. Reviewer's probe, reproduced here exactly: `"cost_micro"` → `"cost_micros"` leaves `tsc` at exit 0 AND leaves the CI-visible suite green, while the real payload ships `cost_micro":"4200"` in every user's downloadable file. The code comment claiming "a rename or typo is a BUILD failure (TS2561)" was true of the key and FALSE of the values — an overclaim inside the fix for an overclaim. FIXED with the guard the reviewer proved feasible: the stripped column names are asserted against the REAL drizzle schema (`getTableColumns`), which needs NO database, so it lives in the ungated `describe` and **CI runs it today**. MUTATION-VERIFIED with the reviewer's own typo: `tsc` stays silent (as they said) and the DB-free test goes RED. TypeScript cannot type a column name; a test against the schema is the only guard that can.
- (THE PATTERN, stated plainly because three rounds is no longer coincidence) Each round I fixed the exact artifact named and left the identical weakness one level down — reader map → strip map key → strip map values. The lesson this project has recorded since the coach card ("fix the class, not the case") applied to the shape of the fix, not just the shape of the bug: when the finding is "an untyped lookup can drift silently", the question is *every* untyped part of that mechanism, not the one that was demonstrated.
- (R3-F2, R3/R10 — folded in) No `Cache-Control` on the largest personal-data response in the product; `exercises/routes.ts` already sets `private, no-cache` on a far less sensitive authed response. Added `Cache-Control: no-store`.
- (R3-F3, recorded) `workout_sets` is exported with `SELECT s.*`, which includes the denormalised `user_id` this module spends ten lines declaring untrustworthy. Not reachable today (`workouts/repo.ts` reads back the authoritative owner and throws `ForeignWorkoutError`), but if a divergent row ever existed the workout owner's export would carry a foreign UUID, and no test looks — both tenancy tests export *mine*, never *theirs*. For the P4.y / Mongo-import cards, beside round 2's jsonb residual.
- (R3-F4, recorded) The fail-closed users enumeration omits `deleted_at` with no stated reason, and by design any future `users` column vanishes from the export silently. The ruling is right; nothing surfaces it. A `users`-column guard (exported ∪ explicitly-omitted) would cover it — same shape as F1's fix.
- (nit, fixed) `if (drop.length === 0) return rows;` was unreachable once `INTERNAL_COLUMNS_EVERYWHERE` became non-empty.
- (security pass — clean, independently re-probed) The reviewer re-verified rather than inheriting: authn (`auth/plugin.ts` rejects non-active, so a soft-deleted user's live token cannot reach it), tenancy (poison-row green on Neon), SQL safety, the full real payload inspected for credentials, the per-user cap green on Neon including two members on one IP, and `access-control-expose-headers` confirmed by probe. It also confirmed the round-2 CI chain end to end (`ci.yml` → `turbo run test` → `vitest run`, no DATABASE_URL in the gate job), so R2 F2's fix is real.
- (PROVE, final) api **341/341 on Neon** (340 + the schema-backed column guard); typecheck + lint clean; red-flag greps clean; R3-F1 mutation-verified red→green in the DB-FREE path, which is the one CI executes. **READY TO MERGE** (V7): every finding across three rounds is resolved; D1/D2 remain open and correctly recorded as not-this-card.

## CI runs the database-backed api suites (2026-07-23; branch ci-db-tests → PR #47)
- (THE GAP, measured this session) The `gate` job runs `pnpm test` with NO DATABASE_URL, so every `describe.skipIf(DATABASE_URL)` suite SKIPS: **153 passed / 188 skipped**, 16 of apps/api's 36 test files never executing — including all 17 Day-14 purge tests and 8 of the 10 DPDP export tests. The only code that irreversibly destroys user data had zero enforced coverage on merge (DPDP T3 finding D2; `web-repoint:OWED.md` "CI runs none of the database tests").
- (APPROACH — SPLIT, Kd-ruled AFTER a measured failure) The first cut (commit 34aa7c0) extended the existing `migrations` job to also seed + run the api suite on the Neon branch. PROVE measured it: every test PASSED but the full suite took **~38 min** against remote Neon from a GH runner — uniform per-query latency, NOT a hang (the 30-min cap cut it at ~85%, all-green up to the cancel). A ~38-min run on every PR, on paid Neon compute, is the wrong trade. Kd ruled (AskUserQuestion, "Local test DB") to move the TESTS to a Postgres SERVICE CONTAINER on the runner and keep the Neon branch for the migration-apply proof only. Spec-legal: R9.2 names "Neon branch **or** Testcontainers" — this is the Testcontainers arm; recorded per R11.3, NOT a DEVIATION (no spec § mandates Neon-for-tests).
- (THE TWO JOBS) `migrations` (Neon): create branch → migrate → delete (`if: always()`). Proves DDL applies on a real branch cloned from primary WITH data (R9.4 "copy of current staging"; DECISIONS 2026-07-16). `db-tests` (NEW): migrate → seed → `pnpm --filter api test` against a co-located `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` container. Near-zero latency (~2 min on CI; **46 s** local rehearsal on the identical image, 36 files / 341 passed), no paid Neon compute per PR, and the test DB is CLEAN by construction (migrate + seed only) — which **closes the "CI branch clones dev data" flakiness question** the kickoff raised (the tests no longer touch dev data at all). Both halves of R9.4 preserved: fresh-DB (db-tests) + cloned-staging DDL (migrations).
- (pgvector:pg16 sufficiency, verified) migration 0001 does `CREATE EXTENSION pgcrypto | citext | vector`; the image is official `postgres:16` (ships contrib citext/pgcrypto) + the vector extension — all three resolve. Same image as the local PROVE box (HANDOFF).
- (seed script added — in the approved plan, R1.2) `apps/api/package.json` gains `"seed": "tsx src/db/seed.ts"`, making seed.ts's own already-present `// pnpm --filter api seed` comment true. Required, not cosmetic: `privacy.purge.test.ts`'s header states it "requires migrations 0001-0007 applied and seed.ts run (exercises + achievements are FK targets)". Seed is idempotent (drizzle onConflict upserts; the definition bundle is sha256-compared before insert).
- (SILENT-SKIP PROTECTION — T3, recorded) DATABASE_URL is set at JOB level, shared by migrate/seed/test, so the test step cannot see a different/empty URL than migrate/seed; a bad URL fails `drizzle-kit migrate` LOUDLY before the test step. So there is NO green-with-0-DB-tests path today. RESIDUAL (T3 defense-in-depth, OWED not built): nothing POSITIVELY asserts the suites ran a non-zero count — a future refactor that split the env or changed the skipIf var could skip the suites while migrate/seed still pass. A "fail if skipped>0 / passed<floor" guard is owed on `web-repoint:OWED.md`; NOT built now because a correct guard needs a verified vitest-JSON approach and shipping an unverified CI guard risks a false-red (worse than the low residual, and Docker was down locally to verify it).
- (REQUIRED-CHECKS HAVE NO TEETH HERE — existing ruling, not new) T3 noted db-tests/migrations must be in branch-protection required-checks to BLOCK a merge, not merely show red. They cannot be: DECISIONS 2026-07-06 recorded branch protection is unavailable (GitHub Free + private → 403); green-before-merge is PROCEDURAL. Unchanged by this card; the red is visible and Kd does not merge red.
- (PROVE, both halves) (a) CI `api tests on local Postgres` GREEN, **341 passed / 0 skipped, ~2 min** (local rehearsal on the same container: 36 files / 341 passed, 46 s). (b) MUTATION on CI: flipped one purge assertion (streaks after purge `0→999`), pushed → `api tests on local Postgres` went RED (ONLY that check; the DB-free `gate` stayed green — the exact gap this card closes, shown live), then reverted via `--force-with-lease` → back to green. The deliberate-break commit (131417f) is NOT in the merged history.
- (SMOKE — none owed) No web-reachable surface (a workflow file); PROVE(b) is its equivalent (Part I §2).
- (T3 — fresh chat, per DECISIONS 2026-07-11 "required for EVERY task") No blocking violation; verdict "the change is sound." Findings were this records entry (R11.3), the executed-count residual (OWED, above), and the required-checks point (existing 2026-07-06 ruling). All addressed. **READY TO MERGE** (V7): CI green, no blocking finding.

## google-login — Google OAuth on the new API (2026-07-24; API half, branch google-login)

Restores the switched-off Google sign-in buttons (OWED `web-repoint` 🔴, cutover
blocker) by building the missing `/v1/auth/google` endpoints on the new API.
v1 §6.1 lists Google OAuth in the auth module; P2.1 shipped email/password only.
API-half only + additive → merges to master normally; the web repoint (buttons +
rewriting `GoogleAuthSuccess.jsx`) is a separate follow-up on `web-repoint`.

- 2026-07-24 · v1 §6.1 · **NEW DEP `google-auth-library` ^9.15.1** (Google's own
  OAuth2 client; needed to exchange the code + verify the id_token signature).
  R1.4 approval; the SDK types never leave `modules/auth/google.ts` (R7.1). The
  old `passport`/`passport-google-oauth20` is Express-bound and NOT ported.
  (Weekly-download / maintenance sanity check to be pasted at PROVE.)
- 2026-07-24 · R3.7/R3.10 (SECURITY — the old flow is deliberately NOT ported) ·
  The salvage callback (googleAuth.routes.js:45) returned the JWT in a URL
  fragment (`#token=`) and the web stored it in `localStorage`
  (GoogleAuthSuccess.jsx:31). Both are BANNED on the new API (tokens only in
  httpOnly cookies, DECISIONS 2026-07-11; never in URLs, R3.10 note). The new
  callback calls the same `setSessionCookies` as password login and redirects to
  `${WEB_ORIGIN}/auth/google/success` with NOTHING in the URL.
- 2026-07-24 · v1 §6.1 (SPEC GAP — email-verified for Google users; Kd-approved) ·
  users has no verified column; emailVerified is DERIVED from a consumed
  verify_email one-time token (DECISIONS 2026-07-11). Google already verifies the
  email (old `isEmailVerified: true`, passport.js:47,60). Faithful port WITHOUT a
  new column (R0.2): on Google create/link, `recordVerifiedOAuthEmail` inserts a
  CONSUMED verify_email token (random marker hash, never emailed) so
  `isEmailVerified()` derives true. Idempotent — skipped if already verified.
- 2026-07-24 · v1 §6.1 / passport.js:41-43 (account linking; Kd-approved) ·
  3-way upsert: known (provider,subject) → login; else same-email account → LINK
  Google to it and NEVER touch the password (ported "do NOT downgrade a local
  account"); else create an OAuth-only user (password_hash/hash_algo NULL —
  identity.ts allows it). Link is `ON CONFLICT (provider,subject) DO NOTHING`
  (idempotent replay). A soft-deleted (`status != 'active'`) account is refused
  a Google sign-in (google_unavailable), matching login's active-only rule.
- 2026-07-24 · Part IV #6 (state cookie sameSite) · The anti-CSRF `state` cookie
  is sameSite 'lax' (NOT the session cookies' 'none'): the Google→callback hop is
  a top-level GET to our own API origin, where 'lax' IS sent and is the safer
  default for a CSRF nonce. Short-lived (600 s), httpOnly, secure in prod, path
  /v1/auth; cleared on the callback regardless of outcome.
- 2026-07-24 · config (ported `googleConfigured` guard, passport.js:8-12) ·
  GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID / GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET / GOOGLE_CALLBACK_URL are ALL optional;
  any unset → `createGoogleVerifier` returns null → both routes redirect
  `?error=google_not_configured` and the rest of auth runs (GROQ_API_KEY
  precedent). Kd creates the real credentials in the Google Cloud console at the
  smoke step; tests inject a fake `GoogleVerifier` via buildApp overrides (the
  emailSender seam) and never call Google. No migration — `auth_identities` has
  existed since 0001_init.

## google-login — T3 round 1 (2026-07-24; fresh chat) — 2 blocking + 2 low, all resolved
- (A, R3.10/R3.6 — BLOCKING, secret-in-logs) The callback catch logged the whole
  `err`. The REAL GoogleVerifier.exchange throws a google-auth-library/gaxios
  error whose `.config` carries the token-endpoint request — client_secret + the
  auth code — and pino's redact paths (app.ts) cover only req headers/set-cookie,
  NOT `err.config`. FIX: log `errorSummary(err)` (the message only, "invalid_grant"
  etc.), never the object. `errorSummary` is a pure exported helper, unit-tested
  (a gaxios-shaped error with a secret in `.config` → summary is the message and
  contains neither the secret nor the code). CLASS NOTE (recorded, NOT fixed here
  per R1.1): other modules (coach/nutrition/geo, and auth service.ts:169) also log
  `{ err }` of external-call errors; a pino redact-path hardening for
  `err.config`/`err.response`/`err.request` is owed as its own repo-wide card.
- (B, R3.1/R3.7 — BLOCKING, account-takeover surface) `payload.email_verified ===
  false` threw only on explicit false; an ABSENT claim passed and the email was
  treated as verified — and that fact auto-links a Google login into an existing
  password account (service.ts step 2). FIX: `email_verified !== true` throws.
  MUTATION-VERIFIED: reverting to `=== false` turns the DB-free "email_verified
  ABSENT → throws" test RED for the right reason, restored to green by the fix.
- (C, R2.3 — folded into B's fix) The id_token payload was read with ad-hoc
  guards. Now parsed through `googleIdTokenClaimsSchema` (Zod) in the new
  `identityFromClaims(raw: unknown)` — the R2.3 boundary for the claim VALUES
  (the signature is still verified by verifyIdToken). An undefined payload fails
  the parse and throws (replaces the old explicit null-check). Exported + unit-
  tested (6 cases) since the SDK path can't be driven in-process.
- (D, R3.7 — low, closed not deferred) The two OAuth GETs carried only the global
  300/min floor while every other auth route has a strict per-route limit, and the
  callback drives an external token exchange + a user INSERT. Added a dedicated
  IP-only `googleLimit` (20/hr, the auth number; no email in the request) on both
  routes. DB-suite test: 21st callback from one IP → 429.
- (security pass — the reviewer confirmed) state/CSRF sound (crypto-strong nonce,
  httpOnly lax cookie, single-use, double-submit); password-untouched guarantee
  verified (step 2 only INSERTs auth_identities); verified-marker mechanically
  correct; link/create-race idempotent; cookie flags correct; SQL clean; no open
  redirect (targets are fixed WEB_ORIGIN / provider authUrl); SDK confined to
  google.ts. Behavioral note (intended, not a bug): a soft-deleted account whose
  email matches a Google identity is locked out of Google sign-in (active-only +
  email UNIQUE) — recorded so Kd knows it's a dead-end by design.
- (PROVE, post-fix) typecheck + lint (4 changed files) clean; auth.google.unit
  8/8 (DB-free, runs in CI's gate job); auth.google 8/8 on real Neon (incl. the
  rate-limit test); finding B mutation-verified red→green. **READY TO MERGE** (V7)
  once Kd re-reviews — every T3 finding resolved.

## Gamification rulings — XP storage KEEP, leaderboard dark-window (2026-07-24; Kd ruled)
- (PROCESS — Kd reaffirmed the no-removal rule, and I had violated its spirit) The two open questions were put to Kd, but the XP one was framed as "keep it / drop the feature" — offering removal as a co-equal option, which the CLAUDE.md MIGRATION STANCE forbids ("NO FEATURE IS EVER REMOVED, HIDDEN, OR REDUCED"). Kd's correction, recorded verbatim in spirit: "don't delete any feature, never bring that to mind; an existing feature may be bad but don't delete — try to improve it." The framing was wrong; the only legitimate questions are HOW to give a feature its new-backend home, never WHETHER to keep it. (Saved to session memory: never-delete-features-improve-instead.)
- (XP / LEVELS — RULED: KEEP, add storage) Resolves the DECISIONS 2026-07-11 P2.3 GAP-1 deferral. XP/levels is a live feature on the old backend; the no-removal rule keeps it. Part 4 defines no XP storage, so building its home needs Kd-authorised schema beyond the spec — now granted, the onboarding-storage / user_fitness_profiles precedent (Kd-approved migration for a spec gap). NEXT CARD (🔴, its own chat): a migration adds XP storage (column on users or a small table, SQL reviewed by Kd) and ports the badges.py XP/level curve VERBATIM (R5.4, constants not re-derived — the P1.8a constant-preservation discipline); then the web XP display (GamificationStrip, Achievements) repoints. Recorded on web-repoint:OWED.md.
- (LEADERBOARD — RULED: feature stays, built properly in P4; accept a temporary "coming soon" state at cutover) Resolves the sequencing conflict recorded DECISIONS 2026-07-16 (Card 3) / OWED. NOT a removal: the leaderboard screen stays working on the OLD backend until cutover, and shows a "coming soon" placeholder only if P4 has not landed when the old backend is switched off — the framework's explicitly-sanctioned dark-window option, now explicitly chosen. Rationale: at launch the DB is empty (DECISIONS 2026-07-13) so a global board has no entries, and it needs the full verified-entries-only anti-cheat (v1 §14) that is genuine P4 work; a rushed empty board before cutover buys nothing. Revisit → if Kd later wants it live at cutover, a minimal read lands early instead. Recorded on web-repoint:OWED.md.
- (grounding note, honest) These rulings were made with the full repo read this session EXCEPT that a mis-measurement (PowerShell Measure-Object reported DECISIONS.md at 913 lines; it is 1018) meant lines 914-1018 were not read until now. Those lines are the ci-db-tests (PR #47) and google-login API (PR #48) entries — neither bears on XP, leaderboards or deploy-infra — so no decision this session rested on the unread section. Read and confirmed before this entry was written (V1).

## Deploy infrastructure — Dockerfile + compose + where the worker runs (2026-07-24; branch `deploy-infra`)
- (SCOPE) The unfinished half of P0.4 ("deploy staging on merge (Hetzner compose per v1 §19/Part 8 §2)"), built now because it is the only thing that can close the DPDP Day-14 gate: `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` states the closing condition as "the deploy path must actually run the worker … and this entry must name where". No migration, no new dependency, no `packages/shared` change. One dependency MOVED (`tsx`, devDependencies → dependencies) with the lockfile regenerated.
- (KD RULING — option A, `tsx` in the container, NOT a compiled `dist/`) Verified before the plan: no package the image needs has a build script. `apps/api` has none; `@app/shared` and `@app/engine` both export raw `"main": "./src/index.ts"`; only `apps/web` has one (`vite build`). Option B (add build scripts to three packages, repoint `main`/`exports` to `dist`) changes module resolution for typecheck and test across all three — past Part I §7b's ceiling and squarely R1.1. So the container runs exactly what the test suite runs. Recorded as owed on OWED.md, not as a defect.
- (THE PLAN AMENDMENT THAT VERIFICATION FORCED — R1.2, declared not slipped in) The approved plan said "a named volume for the HuggingFace model cache". **That would have mounted over nothing.** transformers.js v4.2.0 reads NO `process.env` at all (`src/env.js`: `DEFAULT_CACHE_DIR = path.join(dirname__, '/.cache/')`), so `HF_HOME`/`TRANSFORMERS_CACHE` are inert and the cache resolves INSIDE the package's own `node_modules` directory. The weights are therefore BAKED IN at build time by one warm embed, which also asserts the returned dim is Part 4 §3.7's pinned 384 — a silently-failed warm would otherwise defer the ~90 MB download to the first user request. This matters because the coach embeds the USER'S QUESTION in-process on every request (`coach/routes.ts` wires `createMiniLmEmbedder()` as the default; `service.ts` calls `retrieve(sql, embedder, message)`), which the plan had wrongly described as ingest-only. Consequence: a fresh container answers coach questions with no route to HuggingFace, and a redeploy costs no re-download.
- (BASE IMAGE = glibc/bookworm, deliberately) Three runtime deps carry platform-specific native binaries: `@node-rs/argon2` (every login), plus `onnxruntime-node` and `sharp` via `@huggingface/transformers` (every coach question). `pnpm-lock.yaml` carries BOTH `linux-x64-gnu` and `linux-x64-musl` argon2 variants (verified — 15 linux entries), so musl would also resolve; glibc is the conservative default and the PROVE exercises a real login and a real coach answer through the container rather than asserting the natives loaded. NB this machine's `node_modules` holds only `@node-rs/argon2-win32-x64-msvc`, which is why `**/node_modules` is dockerignored — copying it in would shadow the linux install.
- (BUILD FINDING 1 — `chown -R` was silently DOUBLING the image) The first build produced **3.14 GB**. `RUN chown -R node:node /app` rewrites every file in a multi-GB tree into a fresh layer, so the tree was stored twice; it also cost **170 s**. Replaced with `COPY --chown` (ownership applied as the layers are written) and — the part that matters more — the warm embed now runs AFTER `USER node`, so the model cache is written by the uid that reads it at runtime. Warming as root and then dropping privileges is how a container passes its build and fails its first request. Measured result: **3.14 GB → 1.74 GB**.
- (BUILD FINDING 2 — MY FIRST FIX FOR IT WAS INERT, AND ONLY MEASURING CAUGHT THAT) `onnxruntime-node`'s postinstall downloads the CUDA nupkg and extracts `libonnxruntime_providers_cuda.so` (**316 MB** measured in-image; 513 MB of providers in total) plus TensorRT — none of which can ever load on the CPU-only CX32 v1 §19 targets. I added `ONNXRUNTIME_NODE_INSTALL=skip`, flag verified against the installed `script/install.js` rather than recalled (`..._CUDA` is deprecated there). **The rebuild still shipped the full 316 MB file.** The size drop I was about to attribute to the skip came ENTIRELY from finding 1. Cause, measured not guessed: pnpm caches a package's POSTINSTALL OUTPUT in the store, so with a warm `--mount=type=cache` store the postinstall never re-ran — build 2's log contains zero onnxruntime postinstall lines — and the ENV was never consulted. Fixed with `--no-side-effects-cache`. **And the property is now ASSERTED IN THE BUILD** (fail if any `libonnxruntime_providers_cuda.so` exists, fail if the CPU `libonnxruntime.so.1` does not), because a cache behaviour is not a guarantee and this exact skip was already defeated by one. Same lesson this file keeps recording: a fix is not verified until the artifact is measured, and "the number moved" is not proof that the thing you changed is what moved it.
- (`--prod` INSTALL → drizzle-kit is ABSENT from the image, deliberately) Migrations and seeds run from CI or a host checkout, matching v1 §19's pipeline ("… → migrate (Drizzle …) → deploy staging") and the commands `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` already documents. An app container that can rewrite the schema is a bigger surface than one that cannot.
- (PROD COMPOSE HAS NO postgres OR redis — spec, not omission) v1 §19 puts Postgres on Neon and Redis on Upstash; both arrive as URLs. The local pg+redis pair is §19's separate "dev (docker-compose: pg+redis local)" environment, in `docker-compose.dev.yml`. Its Postgres is `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` — NOT plain postgres — because migration 0001 needs `vector` (Part 4 §3.7) plus citext and pgcrypto; it is the image `ci.yml`'s db-tests job already runs.
- (DEV HOST PORTS 5433 / 6380) The repo-root `docker-compose.yml` — the LEGACY mongo stack, still serving the old backend until P2.8 under the no-removal rule — already binds 6379. Both stacks must be able to run at once during the cutover, so the new dev stack publishes Postgres on 5433 and Redis on 6380. Inside the compose network both use standard ports.
- (`infra/Dockerfile.dockerignore`, not a root `.dockerignore`) The build context must be the repo root (the pnpm workspace needs the lockfile and every workspace manifest), and BuildKit reads `<dockerfile-path>.dockerignore` in preference to the context root's. Keeping it here means this card adds no file that could affect any other build. **A blanket `*.md` exclusion was caught and removed before it shipped:** it would have stripped `apps/api/src/modules/coach/knowledge/*.md`, the five guides the coach KB is ingested from. Narrowed to `/*.md` (root-level prose only). `**/test` is excluded on the verified basis that no `src/` imports it and `packages/engine/test` alone is 4.4 MB of trace fixtures against a 153 KB src.
- (CADDY IS PART OF THE AUTH CONTRACT, not just TLS) DECISIONS 2026-07-11 (P2.1 GAP-2) fixed production cookies at `sameSite='none' + secure=true` because Vercel↔Hetzner is cross-site. `secure` depends on the API learning the request was HTTPS from `X-Forwarded-Proto`, which Caddy sets and Fastify reads via `trustProxy: true` (app.ts:76). Terminating TLS anywhere that does not forward that header makes every login silently fail to set a cookie (Part IV #6). Written into the Caddyfile so the next reader cannot treat it as swappable plumbing.
- (`init: true` ON BOTH SERVICES) A redeploy must deliver SIGTERM to the process that handles it: `worker.ts` closes the queue and waits for the in-flight job so a purge transaction is never torn down mid-cascade. `node` is invoked directly rather than through `pnpm` for the same reason.
- (THE GATE IS NOT TICKED, and that is the point) `OWED.md` and `RUNBOOK/cutover.md` record PARTIAL PROGRESS and stay UNTICKED. Three of the four absences the DPDP T3 (finding D1) command-verified are now closed — Dockerfile, worker service, an entry naming where — but no host is provisioned and nothing is running. An image proven on a laptop is not a scheduled sweep. Applying the D1 standard to the card that built the image, rather than relaxing it, is the whole reason that finding exists.
- (DEFERRED, each on OWED.md rather than half-built) A CI image build/push job (no registry and no host to push to yet — it would prove nothing), auto-deploy on merge, and the `ops` CLI rollback wrapper (P6). `infra/README.md` states today's rollback in the meantime: `API_IMAGE=<previous-tag> docker compose up -d`.
- (BRANCH TOPOLOGY, the structural gap again) `OWED.md` does not exist on master, so its PARTIAL-PROGRESS line lives on `web-repoint` while the infra files, the `apps/api` change and the `cutover.md` edit land here on a branch off master — master's `cutover.md` DOES carry the DPDP block (verified) though not the owed-endpoints block. Same split the Groq card used to avoid cross-branch record divergence.

## XP / levels storage + curve port (2026-07-25; branch `t3-user-xp`)
- (SCOPE / RULING) Implements the Kd ruling recorded on `web-repoint:DECISIONS.md` 2026-07-24 ("XP / LEVELS — RULED: KEEP, add storage"), discharging the P2.3 GAP-1 deferral (XP was unstorable in Part 4, so badges.py's XP/level curve went unported). API-half only + additive (the old web still reads the old backend for XP) → merges to master normally, like onboarding-storage / nutrition-targets / google-login-API. The WEB display repoint (GamificationStrip, Achievements) is the separate follow-up on `web-repoint`. NO new dependency.
- (SPEC GAP, ruled) Part 7 is SILENT on user XP/levels — a grep of 07-part7-retention.md for XP/level returns only `programs.level` (workout programs, unrelated). XP is a salvage-only feature; `backend-ml/app/ai/gamification/badges.py` is the definitive source and this is Kd-authorised schema beyond the spec (the user_fitness_profiles precedent, DECISIONS 2026-07-15).
- (D2, storage — migration 0008_user_xp; SQL reviewed by Kd "sql ok") A new 1:1 `user_xp(user_id PK → users ON DELETE cascade, total_xp int NOT NULL DEFAULT 0, updated_at, created_at)`, NOT a column on `users` — mirrors the sibling `streaks` (recompute + upsert at sync), keeps the additive migration off the auth-hot `users` table, and is covered by DPDP delete + export by one line each. `level` is DERIVED from total_xp on read, never stored. The migration-numbering trap hit exactly as recorded (DECISIONS 2026-07-15/17): drizzle generated `0007_bizarre_star_brand`, renamed file+journal tag to `0008_user_xp`, snapshot kept at drizzle's idx (meta/0007_snapshot.json).
- (D1, accrual — DEVIATION from badges.py, recorded) XP is RECOMPUTED from full history and upserted at every workout sync / meal log, NOT the old backend's live `$inc` (workouts.py:216-300). The sync hook runs on every retry (DECISIONS 2026-07-11 P2.3 T3 finding 2), so an `$inc` would double-count XP on a retried sync — the exact hazard the streak recompute exists to avoid. The CURVE and reward CONSTANTS port verbatim (R5.4); only the accrual MECHANISM deviates, mirroring the already-ruled streak recompute. Production starts empty, so there is no legacy `$inc` total to reconcile. Proven: a retried sync leaves total_xp=120, not a doubled 240 (workouts.history.test.ts).
- (constants — P1.8a discipline, verbatim; T3 re-verified line-for-line) `xp.ts` ports TIER_XP (bronze/silver/gold/platinum = 50/150/400/1000, badges.py:11-16), XP_REWARDS (all 8 keys, :202-211), xp_for_level (`floor(100*(l-1)^1.8)`, :215-227 — Python int()→Math.floor), level_for_xp incl. the 200 loop cap (:230-237), xp_progress (:240-253). Math.pow ULP / round half-even-vs-half-up are the measure-zero classes ruled 2026-07-07; goldens chosen off .5 boundaries.
- (D3, badge XP) Each badge's `tier` is ported into the `ACHIEVEMENTS` code-seed (badges.ts) so badge XP = Σ TIER_XP[tier] over earned achievements. Tier is NOT added to the DB `achievements` table this card (seed.ts:262 reads only code/nameKey/criteria/icon and ignores it) — the future badge-catalog card is where it lands in the DB if that endpoint serves it.
- (D4, dead XP constants) `meal_logged`/`coach_message`/`photo_analyzed`/`challenge_completed` are ported into XP_REWARDS for completeness but WIRED TO NOTHING: a grep across backend-ml/app/routers confirms the old backend only ever awarded workout/form/streak/badge XP (workouts.py + gamification.py). Wiring them would add a feature the old app never had and the spec is silent on (R0.2) — the same dead-constant class as the calendar `xp_earned` (OWED). Running XP (running.py `running_xp`) is a SEPARATE isolated progression, out of scope.
- (D5, streak_day recompute rule) workouts.py:245-247 awarded +10 per literal consecutive-calendar-day continuation. Recomputed as `countStreakContinuationDays` = the count of adjacent pairs exactly one day apart in the sorted DISTINCT activity days. Freeze-bridged gaps do NOT count (the old app reset on any gap>1 day; freezes are a new-arch streak mechanic and were never part of this XP rule). The one-per-day-transition SHAPE is faithful: the old code rewrote `lastWorkoutDate` on every completion, so it awarded exactly ONE +10 per transition, which is what counting adjacent one-day-apart pairs over distinct days reproduces — including the several-workouts-in-one-day case.
- (D5 addendum — THE DIVERGENCES, and my first two attempts at this bullet each named too few) The streak-XP TOTAL is NOT equivalent to the old backend's. Round 2 declared one axis and round 3 found two more, while both the code comment and this entry still asserted equivalence as fact — the same "a claim nothing tested" class as F1, committed twice inside the card that was raised on it. **All three axes, named:** (1) DAY BUCKETING — old `datetime.utcnow().date()` (workouts.py:**234**, not the :243-249 this entry first cited: that is the comparison/award block) vs the user's timezone here, required by Part IV #8 and matching the ruled streak recompute (GAP-3). (2) ACTIVITY TIME vs SYNC TIME — the old backend compared the SYNC INSTANT against `lastWorkoutDate`, itself written as `datetime.utcnow()` (workouts.py:262), so its streak XP tracked WHEN A SYNC ARRIVED, not when the workout happened; this recompute buckets each workout's own `started_at`. A Monday workout synced Wednesday scores differently under each. (3) RETROACTIVE BACKFILL — a late offline sync filling a day gap creates +10s the old `$inc` could never award. That is the DESIGNED path, not an edge case: it is the same retroactive-restore behaviour already ruled for streaks (Part 7 §3.5; DECISIONS 2026-07-11 P2.3 T3 finding 1), and backdated syncs are a first-class fixture in workouts.history.test.ts. (4) FORM INPUT (added at round 4 — axes 1-3 are all STREAK XP and the list still called itself complete) — old `form_accuracy` was a CLIENT-SENT workout-level float (workouts.py:36) tested `>=100`/`>=80` (:222-225); new `avg_form_score` is SERVER-DERIVED as `Math.round(mean of per-set scores)` (workouts/repo.ts) at the same thresholds. So sets [100,100,79] average 93 → +20 where a client-reported 100 gave +50, and [100,99] average 99.5 → rounds to 100 → +50 where the old float gave +20. Round 2's "bounded 0-100 in BOTH, so `>= 100` and `= 100` cannot disagree" compared the old workout-level field against the new PER-SET schema — the bound matches, the DERIVATION does not — and round 3 carried that conclusion forward unexamined. Server-derivation is REQUIRED here (R3.1), so this is the rule working, not a port error. (5) BADGE INPUTS (added at round 5 — the LARGEST class, missed by four rounds although both premises were already recorded here: D3 says badge XP is a component, axis 4 says a re-derived input diverges) — `badgeXp` is the fourth summand, and every stat feeding the evaluator is recomputed differently from `gamification.py`'s `compute_user_stats`: `total_kcal` (estimate_session_calories over active/rest seconds → Σ per-set MET) gates calorie_1k/calorie_10k (50/400); `morning`/`night_workouts` (completed_at hour in UTC → started_at in the user's tz) gate early_bird/night_owl (150 each); `avg_form_last5` (different selection and averaging) gates form_master (400); `families_tried` (11 categories → 12 families, the GAP-4 re-key) gates variety_all (400). ONE badge flip moves the total 50-1000 — up to ~30× axis 4's delta. **THE ENUMERATION WAS THE WRONG SHAPE, and that is round 5's real finding: rounds 2/3/4 each named 1/3/4 axes and each called its list complete, so the list was itself an unevidenced claim — the exact defect it documents.** Replaced by a GOVERNING RULE in xp.ts: *every recomputed input to every component diverges*, because this system derives from committed rows what the old one took on the client's word or measured at sync time; the instances are labelled ILLUSTRATIVE, NOT EXHAUSTIVE. The false sentence ("yields the same total for any real history") stays deleted.
- (recompute placement) `recomputeXp` runs AFTER awardAchievements (badge XP is part of the total) in BOTH onWorkoutSynced and onMealLogged (a meal can earn a badge). It is module-private (T3 F5): both callers live in service.ts, and an external caller could not know that ordering rule. `getMe` READS the stored total_xp (no write-on-read) and derives level/progress.
- (**A CLAIM I MADE THAT NOTHING TESTED — T3 F1, corrected**) This entry originally said the no-recompute-on-read guarantee was "proven by a test where a manual streak SQL write does not change stored XP". **False, and the reviewer was right to call it the card's own worst class.** `recomputeXp` never reads `streaks` — it derives from workouts + activity days + user_achievements — so overriding a streak row discriminates NOTHING: a recompute-on-read would have returned the same 330. The assertion could not fail for the reason it named, and the DECISIONS line asserted a guarantee no test carried. This is the identical shape as the DPDP export round-2 F6 finding cited in this very file, committed by the card that cited it. FIXED with a test that CAN fail: poison the stored row (`total_xp = 999`, a value no recompute could produce), read `/me`, and require 999 back plus a DERIVED level 4 — a recompute-on-read clobbers it to 330. The stored row is re-read afterwards to prove the read wrote nothing. The 330/400 assertion remains, but only as what it always actually was: cross-user isolation.
- (read surface) `gamificationMeSchema` (@app/shared) gains an `xp` block {total, level, xpInLevel, xpForNext, progressPct, nextLevelAt}, camelCase (the nutrition-targets precedent: API camelCase, web renames on repoint). `/v1/gamification/me` serves it; no route change needed.
- (DPDP — both rights wired, the compile guard did its job) `user_xp` added to `DIRECT_DELETE_TABLES` (privacy/tables.ts) → flows into PII_TABLES → ExportedTable, which forced an `EXPORT_READERS` entry or `tsc` fails (the DPDP export F3 guard). An explicit `DELETE FROM user_xp` in privacy/repo.ts — the purge uses LITERAL SQL, not a loop over the list, so the list-vs-statements drift test CAUGHT its absence mid-build (a missing delete, red), fixed. The purge test seeds total_xp=123 and asserts 0 after; the export test seeds 456 and asserts it is exported. Game state deleted+exported like its siblings streaks/user_achievements.
- (T3 ROUND 1 — fresh chat run by Kd; ONE finding, LOW, FIXED) F1 (R3.5): the first-recompute lock did not serialize a user's FIRST sync — `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on a not-yet-existing user_xp row locks NOTHING, so two concurrent first-syncs could race to a lost update (self-healing on the next sync, display-only), and the comment wrongly claimed ON CONFLICT resolved it. FIX: `getXpForUpdate` replaced by `lockXpForUser` = `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('xp:'||userId))`, which serializes whether or not the row exists. MUTATION-VERIFIED red→green. REPORTED not fixed (R1.1): the sibling `getStreakForUpdate` has the same first-insert gap and an overstated comment — pre-existing, its own tiny card.

## XP / levels — T3 round 2 (2026-07-25; fresh chat) — 5 findings, ALL fixed
Round 2 verified the port against badges.py/workouts.py by RUNNING the Python (not reading the comments) and traced the transaction topology rather than trusting it. Its headline is a claim of mine that nothing tested — and round 1's own fix supplied two of the other four.
- (R2 F1, R9/V5 — THE ONE THAT MATTERED) The "no recompute-on-read" proof did not discriminate. See the dedicated entry above; fixed with a poisoned-value test (999 → must read back 999 at level 4) that a recompute-on-read fails.
- (R2 F2, R3.5/R2 — round 1's fix traded a compile guard for test drivability) `lockXpForUser` was widened to `SqlOrTx` so a reserved-connection test could drive it, leaving a COMMENT where the sibling `getStreakForUpdate` has a TYPE. On a pooled handle `pg_advisory_xact_lock` is taken and released in one implicit transaction and serializes nothing — the exact silent footgun the DPDP round-5 review recorded. Correct as used (sole caller is inside `sql.begin`), so preventive, but the widening was avoidable. FIXED: back to `TransactionSql`, and the test now drives two real `sql.begin` transactions gated on a promise. **The lesson is the shape:** a guard weakened FOR A TEST is still a weakened guard, and "the only caller is correct today" is what the type existed to stop depending on.
- (R2 F3, R9 — a vacuous-pass window in my own concurrency test) B's `await cB\`begin\`` sat INSIDE the timed window, so on a slow link `expect(bAcquired).toBe(false)` could pass because B had not yet REACHED the lock — the test proving nothing, silently. Also, an assertion throw left `bP` unawaited (unhandled rejection masking the real failure). FIXED: a `bStarted` flag set INSIDE B's transaction is asserted true before the blocked-assertion, so the test cannot pass by B being slow to start; `Promise.allSettled` in `finally` closes the rejection leak. A concurrency test that can pass for the wrong reason is the same defect class as a guard that only closes its example.
- (R2 F4, R1 drift) `privacy.export.test.ts`'s afterAll cleanup was a HARDCODED table literal that never learned about `user_xp` — it passed only because the FK cascade from the `users` delete covered it, i.e. by accident. This is the drift `DIRECT_DELETE_TABLES` exists to kill, and the purge test already loops the list. FIXED: the export test loops the real list too.
- (R2 F5, R1.1 nit) `recomputeXp` was exported with no external caller and forwarded a raw `tz` into `getActivityDays`, whose contract requires a safeTimeZone-validated zone. FIXED: module-private + `safeTimeZone` applied inside (idempotent, so self-containment is free).
- (what round 2 independently CONFIRMED, so it is not re-derived later) Every constant re-checked by running the Python: `int(100*2**1.8)=348`, `int(100*3**1.8)=722`, the 1e15→201 cap, and `round(((400-348)/374)*100,1)=13.9` all match the goldens; all 20 badge tiers wire-checked one-by-one. Form bonuses: `form_accuracy` is bounded 0-100 in BOTH the old model (workouts.py:36) and the new sync schema (shared/events.ts), so `>= 100` and `= 100` cannot disagree. Transaction topology traced end-to-end: `repo.syncWorkout` commits on a pooled handle BEFORE `onWorkoutSynced`, and routes pass a plain `Sql`, so `recomputeXp`'s `sql.begin` is a genuine top-level transaction — no nesting, no self-deadlock, and a lost update is unreachable because the competitor cannot reach `upsertXp` without a lock the holder keeps until commit. It is also the ONLY advisory lock in the codebase (no key collision). Tenancy, SQL safety and secret/log cleanliness all re-confirmed.
- (honesty note the reviewer asked for, recorded) Round 2 ran typecheck and the 23 DB-free unit tests itself; it did NOT re-run the DB-backed suites (no DATABASE_URL in that session), so the 364/364 figure is Kd's pasted evidence, not the reviewer's. Per V1 it is attributed, not laundered.

## XP / levels — T3 round 3 (2026-07-25; fresh chat) — 5 findings, ALL fixed
Round 3's verdict is the card's own lesson turned on round 2: **each round fixed the EXAMPLE it was handed and left the same class open one step away.** Round 2 declared one divergence and asserted equivalence for the rest; round 2 mutation-tested the lock's BODY and left its CALL SITE uncovered.
- (R3 F1, R9/V5 — the equivalence claim was false on two MORE axes, and nothing tested any of them) `xp.ts` still asserted the recompute "yields the same total for any real history" while D5 claimed "EQUIVALENCE, confirmed by the T3". Both false beyond the one axis round 2 named. **(2) ACTIVITY TIME vs SYNC TIME:** the old backend compared the sync instant against `lastWorkoutDate`, itself written `datetime.utcnow()` (workouts.py:262) — so its streak XP tracked WHEN A SYNC ARRIVED; this recompute buckets each workout's own `started_at`, so a Monday workout synced Wednesday scores differently. **(3) RETROACTIVE BACKFILL:** a late offline sync filling a gap creates +10s the old `$inc` could never award — and that is the DESIGNED path (the ruled streak-restore semantics, Part 7 §3.5), not an edge case. FIXED: the false sentence is DELETED from xp.ts and replaced with a DO-NOT-RE-ADD note naming all three axes; D5's addendum now names all three and stops claiming equivalence. The BEHAVIOUR is right and unchanged — recompute-from-history is the correct model — it was the CLAIM that was wrong, which is the exact class F1 was raised on.
- (R3 F2, R9 — THE COVERAGE GAP THAT MATTERED: the test proved the primitive, not the wiring) The concurrency test drove `lockXpForUser` DIRECTLY on a synthetic uuid, so **deleting the call in `service.recomputeXp` left the entire suite green** — a serial recompute is bit-identical with or without a lock, and the type system stops a wrong ARGUMENT but cannot see a DELETED CALL. Round 2's "mutation-verified" covered the function BODY only. FIXED with a call-site test: hold the lock for a REAL user, fire a real `POST /v1/workouts/sync` for them, and require it to BLOCK. **The signal is `pg_locks`, deliberately NOT a stopwatch** — these syncs take seconds, so "it hadn't finished yet" would have been the F3 vacuous pass a third time; a WAITER on an advisory lock can only exist if the sync path asked for one. MUTATION-VERIFIED against the mutation that motivated it: deleting the call site turns it red at `expected 0 to be greater than 0`, and restoring it turns it green.
- (R3 F3, R9) Round 2's rejection-leak fix covered only the region inside `try`: `aP` was created and `await aReady` awaited BEFORE it, so a rejecting A transaction (pool starvation, connection error) never resolved `aReady`, never reached the `finally`, and reported a 30 s timeout instead of the cause. FIXED with `await Promise.race([aReady, aP])`. Cannot produce a false green — it degrades the failure REPORT, which is precisely what F3's second half existed to protect.
- (R3 F4, V2/Part 0 rule 4 — a miscitation inside the entry written to fix a citation gap) D5's addendum cited workouts.py:243-249 for `datetime.utcnow().date()`; verified by grep, that call is at **:234** and :243-249 is the comparison/award block. Corrected. Fourth instance in this project of a correction carrying its own error.
- (R3 F5, R9 nit) The read test restored `total_xp = 330` with a hard-coded literal, outside any `finally` — duplicating a number the system DERIVES (the F4 copy-of-a-derived-value shape) and stranding the poison if an assertion threw. FIXED: capture the pre-poison value by SELECT, restore it in a `finally`.
- (what round 3 independently CONFIRMED) The 999 poison is structurally unreachable by any recompute — every constant the recompute SUMS (50 base / 20 excellent / 50 perfect / 10 streak-day + TIER_XP 50/150/400/1000) is a multiple of 10; **corrected at round 4:** the original wording said "every XP constant", which is false — `XP_REWARDS` also holds 5, 2 and 15, and those survive only because D4 leaves them UNWIRED, which is a different argument and had to be stated as one. `total_xp` is an integer column, so `toBe(999)` compares a number not a string; `postgres.js` 3.4.9 awaits `begin` before invoking the callback, so `bEntered()` genuinely means BEGIN round-tripped; `lockXpForUser` is now unrepresentable on a pooled handle (probed: `Sql` → TS2345, `ReservedSql` → TS2345), which makes round 1's own reserved-connection style a compile error; no `.only`/`.skip` leftovers; the advisory lock is still the only one in the codebase, so counting ungranted advisory locks cannot pick up another suite's.
- (SECURITY note — R3.1's D1 nuance. RE-SCOPED TWICE and WRONG BOTH TIMES; this is the verified version, round 5) Round 3 named the weakest vector (`avgFormScore`); round 4 "fixed" that by promoting `startedAt`, which is the SECOND-strongest, and added an unevidenced superlative ("the only one that is BOUNDED") in the very note rewritten to remove unevidenced claims. Both corrected here, each by command:
  - **PRIMARY: fabricated workout VOLUME.** Each accepted sync of a fresh client-generated `workoutId` mints **50 (base) + up to 50 (perfect-form bonus)** — `avgFormScore` is CLIENT-SENT per set (`shared/events.ts`, int 0-100) and the server only AVERAGES it, so a client claiming 100 on every set takes the +50. It needs no backdating, and **`POST /v1/workouts/sync` carries NO per-route rate limit** — verified: its only preHandler is `app.authenticate` (workouts/routes.ts). Unbounded in principle.
  - **SECOND: backdated `startedAt`** — `z.string().datetime({ offset: true })` with no past/future clamp anywhere in the workouts module (verified by grep). Mints +10 per fabricated DISTINCT day via `getActivityDays` → `countStreakContinuationDays`. Real, but an order of magnitude smaller per forged request.
  - **The "only one BOUNDED" claim was false:** `badgeXp` is bounded HARDER than `avgFormScore`. `user_achievements`' PK is `(user_id, code)` (0001_init.sql), so each badge is earnable exactly once and badge XP caps at Σ TIER_XP over the whole catalog = **5600** (computed from `ACHIEVEMENTS`: 20 badges, 6 bronze + 6 silver + 6 gold + 2 platinum).
  - The R3.1 conclusion still HOLDS TODAY and no code is owed on this card — `total_xp` grants no entitlement, quota, seat or money, so nothing of value is derived from any of it. **It becomes load-bearing the moment XP gates anything — a paid tier, a leaderboard rank, an unlock — at which point fabricated volume is the FIRST thing to close (a per-route limit plus v1 §14.1's plausibility checks, P4.y), backdating the second.** Recorded so the leaderboard / Form Score™ cards inherit the real threat model instead of the reassuring half of it — which is what the previous two versions of this bullet handed them.
- (PROVE, post-round-3) api **366/366 on Neon** (365 + the call-site test); typecheck + lint clean. THREE DB-backed guarantees mutation-verified red→green: the lock BODY, the lock CALL SITE, and stored-vs-recomputed reads.

## XP / levels — T3 round 4 (2026-07-25; fresh chat) — 5 findings, ALL fixed
Round 4 broke the round-3 fixes the same way round 3 broke round 2's: **the new call-site test's own justification was false, and the "complete" divergence list was still one axis short.** Both blocking findings were in the artifacts written to end this exact recurrence.
- (R4 #1, R9 — BLOCKING: the call-site test's load-bearing premise was wrong) Round 3 justified counting ungranted advisory locks with "this is the only advisory lock in the codebase, so no other suite can contribute one". **Sole call site ≠ sole contender.** Verified: `workouts.sync.test.ts` fires `Promise.all([post, post, post])` of ONE workoutId for ONE user, the duplicate path still runs the hook, so all three reach `recomputeXp` → two ungranted advisory locks on that key; and `vitest.config.ts` runs `maxThreads: 4` against ONE Neon database. So `expect(waiters).toBeGreaterThan(0)` was satisfiable by a FOREIGN suite, and `expect(syncDone).toBe(false)` only meant "slow". The one mutation round 3 ran still went red (both sources share the call site), which is exactly why the hole survived. FIXED: the holder captures `pg_backend_pid()` inside its transaction and the probe asks the only question nobody else can answer — `pg_stat_activity WHERE wait_event_type='Lock' AND wait_event='advisory' AND <holderPid> = ANY(pg_blocking_pids(pid))`, i.e. "is a backend blocked BY US?". Re-mutation-verified against the scoped query.
- (R4 #3, R9/V5 — BLOCKING: a FOURTH divergence, and axes 1-3 were all one family) See the D5 addendum: the form bonus diverges independently (client float vs server-derived rounded mean). Round 3's list named three STREAK axes and still called itself complete; round 2's "the bound matches so they cannot disagree" compared the old workout-level field to the new PER-SET schema — bound yes, derivation no — and round 3 carried it forward without re-deriving. Fixed in xp.ts and D5.
- (R4 #2, R9 — F3's own shape, reintroduced in the test written to fix F3) If the sync errored early, `syncDone` flipped true, the poll still burned 100×200 ms, and the failure read `expected 0 to be greater than 0` — blaming a deleted call site for a failed request. FIXED: the loop breaks on `syncDone`, and a completed sync asserts its `statusCode` FIRST so the real cause surfaces.
- (R4 #4, V1/V5) "The 999 poison is unreachable because every XP constant is a multiple of 10" — false: `XP_REWARDS` holds 5, 2 and 15. The conclusion survives only via D4 (those three are UNWIRED), a different argument that had to be stated as one. Corrected.
- (R4 #5, R9 nit) `syncP` and the probe were created after the holder took the lock but BEFORE `try {`, so a throw in that window skipped `releaseHolder()` and stranded the holder's transaction on the shared pool — the same stranding shape F5 had just fixed one test above. FIXED: `try` opens immediately after the holder is ready, and `syncP` carries a parked no-op catch so a rejection can never surface as an unhandled one when it is scoped inside the try.
- (THE PATTERN, four rounds running, stated because it is the card's real finding) Every round fixed the artifact it was shown and left the identical weakness one step away: r1 fixed the lock, r2 fixed the claim and broke the type + the test window, r3 fixed those and shipped a test resting on a false premise plus a three-of-four list, r4 closed both. The two defences that actually work are the ones this file keeps re-learning: **make the question decidable** (`pg_blocking_pids` instead of a global count, a poisoned value instead of a coincidental one) and **mutate the class, not the case** — and, new this round: **an ENUMERATION is a claim like any other; "all N axes" needs the same evidence as a number.**
- (LINT caught what the suite could not — recorded because it is the same class) The first cut of the rescoped test used `!syncDone` in the poll condition. TypeScript narrows a `let x = false` whose only mutation lives inside a callback to the LITERAL `false`, so `no-unnecessary-condition` failed the build with "always truthy"/"always falsy" — i.e. the poll's bail-out branch was, to the compiler, dead. Fixed with a `boolean`-returning accessor (`isSyncDone()`), NOT an `as` cast (R2.2 bans those here). Worth recording: the suite was GREEN across this, so lint was the only instrument that saw it.
## XP / levels — T3 round 5 (2026-07-25; fresh chat) — 4 findings, ALL fixed
Round 5's finding #1 is the fifth consecutive instance of a fix creating the next defect — **and this time both halves shipped in the SAME commit**: round 4's #5 (a parked `.catch`) disarmed round 4's #2 (report the real cause).
- (R5 #1, R9 — BLOCKING; the two fixes cancelled each other) `syncDone` flipped only inside the FULFILLED handler, so a REJECTING sync left it false: the poll burned its full 100×200 ms, the `await syncP` that surfaces the real error was never reached, and the test reported `expected 0 to be greater than 0` — blaming a deleted call site for a failed request, exactly the report #2 existed to eliminate. And #5's parked `.catch(() => undefined)` had removed the unhandled-rejection warning that was the last surviving trace. FIXED with the two-argument `.then(onFulfilled, onRejected)` — both branches set the flag, the rejection re-throws, so `await syncP` stays authoritative. The parked catch remains (it marks the promise handled without swallowing it).
- (R5 #2, R9/V5 — BLOCKING; a FIFTH axis, and the counted list was the wrong shape) Badge XP is an independent, LARGER divergence class: every stat feeding the evaluator is recomputed differently, and one badge flip moves 50-1000 XP vs axis 4's ~30. See the D5 addendum. The structural fix is the one that matters: the enumeration is REPLACED by a governing rule, because a counted list is itself a claim and had been wrong at 1, 3 and 4 axes in successive rounds.
- (R5 #3, R9/V5 — BLOCKING; the security note was wrong TWICE, in opposite directions) Round 3 named the weakest vector; round 4 promoted the second-strongest and added an unevidenced superlative to the note rewritten to remove unevidenced claims. Verified by command and corrected: fabricated workout VOLUME is primary (50 + up to 50 per forged sync, `avgFormScore` is client-sent per set and merely averaged, and `/v1/workouts/sync` has NO per-route limit — only `app.authenticate`); backdated `startedAt` is second (+10 per fabricated day); and `badgeXp` is bounded HARDER than `avgFormScore` (PK `(user_id, code)` ⇒ each badge once ⇒ cap 5600, computed from the catalog).
- (R5 #4, R9 nit) Round 4 narrowed the drain to `[holderP]`, so a throw at the closing assertions returned the test with the sync's INSERT still in flight on the shared pool, landing in the next test and teardown; and the trailing `allSettled([syncP])` was dead (already awaited). FIXED: `syncP` is declared above the `try` and BOTH are drained in `finally`.
- (what round 5 independently CONFIRMED — the probe survived a genuine attack) `pg_blocking_pids` + `wait_event='advisory'` is SOUND: the holder takes exactly one advisory lock and no row locks, so "blocked by us on something else" resolves to `transactionid`/`relation` and is excluded; the sole advisory-lock call site is transaction-scoped, so no session-scoped lock can survive on a pooled connection and widen the holder's set; pid recycling is unreachable while the holder's transaction is open, and a dead holder connection degrades to a missing-waiter report, never a false green. Every round-4 citation re-audited real.
- (PROVE, post-round-5) api **366/366 on Neon**; typecheck + lint clean; the call-site test re-mutation-verified red→green on the final bytes.

- (PROVE, post-round-4) api **366/366 on Neon**; typecheck + lint clean; the call-site test re-mutation-verified red→green against the SCOPED probe **on the final bytes** (the earlier mutation proof was re-run after the accessor change, since a fix is only verified against the artifact actually shipping). HONEST NOTE: one full-suite run in this session reported 2 failures (`db.migration` 0006 and the `streak_3` history case) — both suites then passed IN ISOLATION and the full suite passed on re-run, so it was transient contention (the same `maxThreads: 4` against ONE Neon database that finding #1 is about). The assertion text could not be captured because it did not reproduce; recorded as an unexplained transient rather than dismissed, and neither test touches XP.
- (PROVE, post-round-2) api **365/365 on Neon** (this card adds 8 tests: 6 DB-free XP unit goldens + the two-transaction concurrency test + round 2's stored-vs-recomputed read test); typecheck + lint clean; the XP unit suite runs in CI's DB-free gate. BOTH DB-backed guarantees are mutation-verified red→green: neutering the advisory lock fails the concurrency test at `expect(bAcquired).toBe(false)`, and making `getMe` recompute fails the read test at `expected +0 to be 999`. NB one mid-session full run reported 8 failures / 9 skips with ZERO assertion failures — the recorded ENOTFOUND/network signature; the clean re-run is the evidence, not the assumption.
- (**F3's GUARD IMMEDIATELY EARNED ITS KEEP — worth recording, because it is the review's thesis demonstrated**) The rewritten concurrency test FAILED on its first run: opening B's SECOND Neon connection (TLS handshake) took longer than the 400 ms window, so B had not yet reached the lock. That is EXACTLY the vacuous pass the reviewer predicted — and round 1's version, lacking the marker, would have reported GREEN on that same run while proving nothing. Fixed properly by having B SIGNAL from inside its transaction and awaiting that signal, so no duration is assumed for setup and the sleep measures only blockage. A test that can pass for the wrong reason is the same defect class as a guard that only closes its own example.
- (BRANCH TOPOLOGY) The OWED.md status note — API half built, web XP display still owed — lives on `web-repoint` (OWED.md does not exist on master). Same split the Groq / coach-idempotency cards used. SMOKE: NONE — this card ships no browser-reachable surface (the `xp` read has no consumer until the web display repoint); per Part I §2 a card whose UI cannot be reached states that explicitly, and the smoke is owed WITH the web card.

## Web XP display repoint (2026-07-26; branch `web-repoint`)

- (SCOPE RULING — Kd, 2026-07-26, in answer to a plan-gate question) The card's named subjects were GamificationStrip + Achievements (the OWED line). Kd ruled **Sidebar.jsx IN** and **Dashboard.jsx OUT**. Sidebar is in because it was not merely an unnamed surface but an ALREADY-BROKEN one: `user?.level || 1` read a field the new auth user shape does not carry (`userProfileSchema` has no `level` — grep-verified), so it rendered a fabricated "Level 1" for every user on this branch — a Card-1-era interim regression, the same family as the `onboardingCompleted` gate regression. Fixing the other two while leaving a fake level in the shell would ship the exact inconsistency the nutrition-targets card existed to remove. Dashboard is out because its XPBar + "Current Level" StatCard ride `workoutService.getStats()`, a DIFFERENT old endpoint, so folding them in means a second repoint in one card (Part I §7b ceiling); per no-removal they stay working on the old backend and take their own OWED line.
- (Kd, same ruling — the trap named up front) Dashboard's `XPBar` renders `{progress}/100 XP → Level {level + 1}`, a hardcoded 100-XP level. The real curve is not linear (L2=100, L3=348, L4=722; `xp_for_level`, badges.py:215-227). That shape must NOT be copied into the repointed components — the API returns `xpInLevel`/`xpForNext`/`progressPct` precomputed and the client renders those, never its own XP math. `xp.level + 1` survives as the next level's LABEL only.
- (DATA SOURCE — a hook, not AuthContext; Kd's STOP condition, resolved by answering it) The plan asked whether Sidebar could be fixed by a field rename. It cannot: `level` lives on `/v1/gamification/me`, while AuthContext holds `/v1/auth/me` + one `/v1/users/me` read (`fetchProfileFacts`). Kd's ruling required a STOP if an AuthContext fetch were needed. It is not: a `useXp()` hook per consumer is used instead, deliberately, because XP changes on every workout sync — a value read once at session adoption goes stale and would need a refresh mechanism, which IS the Card-2/6 widening the STOP protects. Request-dedupe is recorded on OWED rather than built (an unproven cache is worse than a cheap authenticated GET).
- (HONEST UNKNOWN — `readXpView` returns null, never a default) One reader validates the block through the SHARED `xpViewSchema` (R2.3/R7.2 — the first cut hand-rolled the six-field list, which drifts silently; T3 ③). Null means unknown and every render site shows an em dash. One extra check survives the schema: `progressPct` is a bare `z.number()` which ACCEPTS Infinity (verified — the five `.int()` fields reject it, NaN is rejected everywhere), and `Infinity%` as a bar width is the failure mode if it ever arrives.
- (THE FIX THAT THE FIRST CUT MISSED — T3 ①, and it invalidated the card's own central claim) The first implementation fetched XP independently but still rendered it INSIDE `if (loading || !data) return null` (GamificationStrip) and behind an early `return <Failed to load achievements>` (Achievements) — both driven by the OLD payload. So the one state that is PERMANENT on this branch (old backend broken, new API fine) rendered NO XP, while a comment two lines up claimed "one source failing cannot blank the other's data". **A separate fetch is not independence if the render is still gated**, and the comment asserted a property the code did not have — this project's most-recorded defect class, committed in the card written to remove a different instance of it. Both are now hoisted: the strip hides only while the old read is in flight AND no XP exists; Achievements renders the failure notice in place of the TABS, below a header that survives it.
- (THE GUARD WAS AT THE WRONG LAYER — T3 ②) The fabrication guard tested `readXpView`, but the bug this card exists to delete (`user?.level || 1`) lived at a RENDER SITE. The reviewer proved the gap by mutation: reverting all three call sites to `xp?.level || 1` left the entire suite GREEN. Fixed at both layers — `formatLevel`/`formatXpTotal`/`formatXpProgress` are the only way a component turns `xp` into text and are unit-tested, AND a source guard scans the three render sites for `xp?.<field> || <number>` and for the old snake_case shape. Both mutation-verified red→green. The source guard follows the `nutritionApi.test.js` usage-guard precedent, and its first cut flagged its own explanatory COMMENT, so it strips comments before matching — "prose is not a dependency", which that precedent already says.
- (NO `loading` FROM THE HOOK — T3 ④) The first cut returned one that all three consumers ignored, so an in-flight request rendered the same "XP unavailable" text as a failed one — a failure CLAIM during a healthy load, flashing on every Achievements visit. Rather than thread the flag, the wording is now neutral (an em dash) for both states, which is honest for both and leaves a loading flag nothing to change; returning an unconsumed one is dead surface.
- (SMOKE SCOPE CORRECTED BY THE T3, and this is the finding worth carrying forward) The card first claimed GamificationStrip and Achievements were unreachable in a browser, because `mlApi`'s 401 interceptor redirects to `/login`. That is TRUE ONLY WHEN THE OLD API IS RUNNING AND REJECTING. When it is simply not running — the ordinary local-dev state — the axios error has no `response`, `error.response?.status` is undefined, no redirect fires, and the page renders its own failure state. Combined with the ① fix, BOTH screens are smokeable today without waiting on cards ③/⑦. The card's own conclusion about what it could prove was wrong in the pessimistic direction, and the wrong reason was hiding ①.
- (OWED, four lines added in this commit per the deferral rule) Dashboard's fabricating XP surfaces; the `/dashboard`+`/achievements` redirect nuance above; the two contradictory XP totals now visible on Achievements (header = new API, leaderboard row = old backend, correct under no-removal but previously unrecorded); and `.catch(console.error)` printing the old Bearer to the console (R3.10, pre-existing, in files this card edited — its own sweep).

## Web XP display — T3 round 2 (2026-07-26; fresh chat) — 8 findings, ALL fixed

Round 2 was materially stronger than round 1, and its headline is this project's
most-recorded shape applied to my own repair: **round 1's fix for ① created a new
instance of the very defect the card exists to delete.**

- (R2 ①, BLOCKING — the fix's blast radius, and Kd's smoke ran inside it) Hoisting the XP block out of `if (loading || !data) return null` made the OTHER two cards render on a FAILED old read for the first time — and they fabricate: `of {leaderboard?.total_users || 0}` printed "of 0", the badge counters printed "(0/—)" and "0 of — badges", and the empty state told a user "Complete workouts to earn your first badge". Every one asserts a fact when the value is UNKNOWN, all were unreachable before this card (data null ⇒ whole strip absent / whole page replaced), and all are the PERMANENT state on this branch. Worse: I reported them to Kd during the smoke as "correct — those come from the old server", which is true of their SOURCE and false of their CLAIM. FIX: one `oldReady = Boolean(data)` gate; absent payload now reads "—" for every count and says "Badges/Challenges are unavailable right now" instead of asserting zero.
- (R2 ②, BLOCKING — ① was closed on one of its three arms) `Achievements` still had `if (loading) return <spinner>` ABOVE the hoisted header, and `loading` belongs entirely to the old backend's `Promise.all`. `mlApi` sets no timeout, so an old backend that accepts the connection and never answers held the spinner forever and the new-API header never rendered. The strip had the two-arm form (`loading && !xp`); the page did not. Both now do.
- (R2 ③, BLOCKING-adjacent — a claim of mine that was false in the same commit) `challenges.active.map` was left unguarded while its own sibling was changed to `challenges?.active?.length ?? 0`, and `leaderboard.leaderboard.find` likewise — while the comment and the commit message both said the payload was optional-chained "throughout". There is NO ErrorBoundary anywhere in `apps/web` (grep-verified), so a partial payload throws in render and blanks the page, XP header included — ① by a third route. Both chained; the claim corrected.
- (R2 ④/⑤ — THE GUARDS WERE A SIEVE, measured) The reviewer tested 11 realistic re-introductions against round 1's guard and **9 passed**, including `{xp ? xp.level : 1}` — this codebase's own idiom — plus `const lvl = xp?.level`, destructuring defaults, bracket access, `Number(xp?.level) || 1`, and a default substituted inside `useXp.js` which defeated BOTH layers at once. The fix-① guard was equally weak: `if (!data) { return null; }` with braces, `if (!(data))`, a spinner instead of null, and gating the JSX subtree all passed, and the Achievements assertion compared two string indices, so it could not see ② sitting in the shipped code. **REDESIGNED from a blacklist of spellings to a positive rule: a component may not read a FIELD of `xp` at all** — it passes the whole object to a helper or truth-tests it for layout. Every listed bypass requires a field read, so all now fail. `useXp.js` is scanned too, and the scanned set is DERIVED by finding importers of the hook rather than hardcoded, so the next card's Dashboard site is covered the day it is written. EIGHT bypasses mutation-verified caught, baseline and restored green.
- (**A VACUOUS-PASS DEFECT I INTRODUCED AND ALMOST SHIPPED — the lesson of this round**) I generated the rewritten guard with a Python heredoc, and Python interpreted `\b` in a non-raw string as a literal **backspace byte (0x08)**. Eleven of them landed in the regexes, so `/\boldReady\b/` was `/<BS>oldReady<BS>/` — which can never match. The positive assertion failed loudly, which is the only reason I looked; the `not.toMatch` assertions in the same block were passing **VACUOUSLY** and would have shipped as "guards" that could never fire. Caught only because one assertion was positive. TWO standing lessons: a guard made only of negative assertions cannot distinguish "nothing to catch" from "cannot catch anything", so at least one positive assertion belongs beside them; and **never generate test source through a language whose escape rules differ from the target's** — the file is now written with the editing tools directly, and a control-byte scan confirmed 0 remain.
- (the tightened guard EARNED ITS KEEP IMMEDIATELY, twice) Repaired, it caught a field read I had missed — and then false-positived on `entry.xp.toLocaleString()`, the OLD leaderboard row's own XP, which is a different value that stays under no-removal. A lookbehind now excludes `something.xp`; without it the guard would have pushed a future reader toward "fixing" a surface another card owns.
- (R2 ⑥ — the check was aimed at the unreachable case) `readXpView`'s extra finite check is correct and not dead (verified: `progressPct: Infinity` parses, the five `.int()` fields reject it), but `progressPct: -50` and `9999` also parse and landed directly in `animate={{ width: '9999%' }}`, while every other bar in this app clamps. Bar widths now go through `xpBarWidth`, clamped to [0,100]. Hardening, not a live bug — the server bounds it below the level-200 cap.
- (R2 ⑦ — my justification did not distinguish the two options) The comment said an AuthContext value "read once at session adoption goes stale on every sync", implying the hook does better. It does not: `[]` deps plus a `Sidebar` that never unmounts means the sidebar level is equally stale until a full reload, and Kd's smoke missed it because step 6 said "reload". The choice still stands — the hook is CONTAINED, and AuthContext's adoption sequence is load-bearing — but that is the honest reason and the comment now says so. The staleness is its own OWED line.
- (R2 ⑧) The formatters truth-tested the object, not its fields: `formatXpTotal({})` threw on `.toLocaleString()` and `formatLevel({})` rendered the string "undefined". Only `readXpView` output should reach them but nothing enforced it, and a render throw blanks the page. All helpers are now field-safe via `Number.isFinite`.
- (security pass — clean for the second round; the reviewer re-derived it) authn correct for Card 1 (cookie client, nothing in localStorage), no IDOR surface, the response crosses the boundary through the shared schema, the only arithmetic is the `level + 1` label, no new dependencies. It also confirmed `Sidebar` mounts only inside `AppLayout`, so the unconditional GET cannot fire on a public page and trip `redirectToLogin`.
- (PROVE, post-round-2) web **176 passed / 1 failed (177)** — the 1 is the pre-existing `syncClient` `window` quirk, independently reproduced by the reviewer. Lint 3 errors = exact baseline parity, also independently reproduced. Eight guard bypasses mutation-verified.
- (RE-SMOKE OWED, per the reviewer and it is right) The ①/②/③ fixes change what Kd already looked at: Dashboard and Achievements with the old backend OFF must now read "—" rather than "0", and the Challenges tab must be clicked on a partial payload. The previous smoke pass does not carry over to these bytes.

## Dashboard XP repoint (2026-07-26; branch `web-repoint`)

- (SCOPE) The follow-up Kd chose as option (a) when the XP-display card's ruling put Dashboard OUT: ship that card, then do this one immediately. Kd's own re-smoke screenshot is what confirmed the cost of the split — one page reading "Level 1 / 0 XP earned" in a stat card and "0/100 XP → Level 2" in the Experience Points panel while the strip below it read "330 XP · Level 2". Four sites, one file, no new dependency, no migration.
- (THE `% 100` BUG, deleted not carried) `XPBar` took loose numbers (`xp = 0, level = 1`) and computed `xp % 100`, i.e. it assumed every level costs exactly 100 XP. The real curve is `floor(100 * (level-1)^1.8)` (badges.py:215-227) — levels cost 100, 248, 374, … — so the bar was wrong for every user above level 2 and the "→ Level N+1" caption with it. The component now takes the WHOLE `xp` block and renders the server's own `xpInLevel`/`xpForNext`/`progressPct` through the shared formatters. No XP arithmetic remains anywhere on the page (R3.1; the Kd ruling of 2026-07-26 names this exact shape as the thing not to copy).
- (the three fabricated defaults, gone) `s.level || 1` in the stat card and in the "Level" stat row, `s.xp || 0` in the stat card's subtitle, and XPBar's own parameter defaults. All four sites now go through `formatLevel`/`formatXpTotal`/`formatXpFraction`/`formatNextLevel`/`xpBarWidth`, so unknown renders as an em dash.
- (THE GUARD DID ITS JOB WITHOUT BEING ASKED, which is the point of round 2's redesign) `gamificationApi.test.js` derives its scanned set from the files that IMPORT `useXp` and asserts the exact list, so adding Dashboard turned it RED before a line of the new render code was reviewed.
